## Revision 2 (2026-10-03): what changed and why

Why: the owner answered the five revision-1 questions and the critic found one blocker and ten majors. Nothing outside this file was touched, no keysync run, no CCR or gateway action, no settings.json or `~/.llmkeys` edit, no secret printed. Each critic claim below was re-checked against the tree before being relied on (V21-V27 carry the file:line); three could not be re-checked read-only and are labelled as such: the 21 KB haiku-with-agent-id row (critic-reported, experiment E14), the live `CUSTOM_ROUTER_PATH` value (owner-reported from `config.sqlite`, V26) and the live body sizes (critic-reported, V27).

1. Owner decisions D-a..D-h recorded (Decisions table) and propagated: Toggle 2 value `fixed` renamed `inherit` everywhere (CLI flag, schema, docs, tests); no Opus/Fable cap, price shown per row, warning when `inherit` would inherit an Opus- or Fable-priced main; empty-set rule split into set-time (refuse only when empty for every provider or main's provider), warn-and-save otherwise, and runtime serve-`asked` with once-per-session `EMPTY_SET`; `inherit` + `ctx 1m` below 1M inherits anyway with a warning; the legacy `claude-opus-5` slot is retired but its substring bug is fixed IMMEDIATELY (new stage S0b) and the code is removed at G3; unverified-tools models are allowed with a standing warning; injection into the Agent tool description is router-written, default OFF, gated at G4; rollout shadow first.
2. Decisions-to-interpretation gap closed: where the owner's text (`docs/UW.txt:10-23`) does not literally say what the decisions need, the plan's reading is listed in the Interpretations table and flagged for confirmation (open question Q1).
3. cr-B1 (the production router is live): `spike/uw-router.cjs` is the router CCR re-requires on EVERY request. A load-time failure, syntax error or module-scope throw is caught by CCR's own wrapper (V21), which logs and returns `undefined`, so every request falls through the policy chain to the anchor. Development moves to `router/uw-router.next.cjs`; deploy only through the new section 6.7 protocol (`node --check`, require smoke test on the exact bytes, write-temp plus atomic rename, backup kept, one-command restore). The S1 "nothing live" wording is corrected.
4. cr-B2 (determinism): per-agent sticky map; a subagent's first ENFORCED decision is final until a 6 h TTL; shadow/clear bypass it. Section 6.1 and 6.3 rewritten.
5. cr-M1 context-fit and payload filter (a precondition of enforcement); cr-M2 sandbox gate (key, user-agent, pre-assertion, how `CUSTOM_ROUTER_PATH` is set); cr-M3 tool fidelity stored in its own file, snapshot schema 10 dropped, L3 sizing corrected; cr-M4 slot substring (D-e); cr-M5 `aux` class and E2/E3 gate on ALL enforcement; cr-M6 deterministic top-K substitute; cr-M7 toggle ambiguities (D-d, Interpretations); cr-M8 which warning paths are instant and which are not; `emptyProviders[]`; `rebuild` from S2; cr-M9 acceptance reproducible on fixtures, live counts corrected to the critic's replay and date-stamped; cr-M10 test seams.
6. Minors cr-m1..m13 applied (label wording, no `learnMain` without a session id, id regex and 64-char cap, Windows rename retries, bounded maps, explicit reject of an unresolvable `asked`, one resolvability rule, compile-free rollback, standing doctor WARN, roles and `model: sonnet`, extended pre-mortem P4-P9, tag-stripper runbook note, softened `[1m]` claim).
7. New experiments E12-E16 (critic's open questions plus the two the fixes introduce); open questions cut from five to three (section 16).

## Revision 3 (2026-10-03): what changed and why

Why: the owner decided the scope of tool verification (D-i), the meaning and scopes of `free` (D-j), where the key tier comes from and how tiers are named and changed (D-k), and the names shown to the user (D-l). Same rules as revision 2: only this file edited, no keysync run, no CCR or gateway action, no settings or `~/.llmkeys` write, no secret printed, nothing above sonnet. The vault registry `~/.llmkeys/registry.json` was READ for its structure only (78 rows; fields `added, bucket, envVarName, id, notes, provider, tier`; tiers today free 64, paid 13, management 1; the file holds ids, tiers and env var NAMES, not secrets). The replay figures of 2026-10-03 used below (1,875 probe-ok models; 77 with `tools: false`; 112 with a free badge; 30 with a listed price of 0; 1,686 probe-ok models on 45 free-tier providers; 175 probe-ok models on paid-tier providers; 3 free-tagged models on paid-tier providers) are the coordinator replay of snapshot plus bench and were NOT re-run here; they are informational and date-stamped.

1. D-i, tool-verification scope: the tool-fidelity probe first pass (L1 + L2) covers ALL probe-ok models, including the 77 whose catalogue says `tools: false` (that claim is untrusted too); the 13 Anthropic relay models are known-good by provenance and not probed; paid-tier models go through the bench existing price ceilings and spend caps; L3/L4 are NOT part of the all-models pass. A new tier `t` (L1+L2 passed, not yet L3) joins `v`, `u`, `x`. Gate G7 covers the spend. Sections 5.2, 8, 11, 12 and the cost table.
2. D-j, free scopes: `free` mode gets `--free-scope models|providers|providers+deposit` (default `providers`), the strict free-tag rule for paid and deposit providers, price-0-first ranking inside free-tier providers, per-scope funnel counts in `set` and `show`, and a stated way and time to rebuild when a free-provider model flips to `pay`. Sections 1, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12. Consequence: revision 2 figures about `free` (20 models, free AND 1M = 0) were measured under a narrower free rule and are WITHDRAWN in revision 4 (under I10 `free models` is 112 and 14 of them are >= 1M, V29); the revision 2 sample commands and the report example were re-pointed at that scope, and the plan makes no live claim for the new default scope.
3. D-k, key tier source and vocabulary: tier is read from the vault registry (through the existing `filterRegistry` and `chooseKeys`), never from the snapshot `keyId` (clipped at 30 characters, issue #148). Vocabulary defined once as data (`free`, `free-deposit`, `paid`, `subscription`, `management`); `key.mjs add` validates; planned `key.mjs retier`. New stage S1c with its own tests. V28 records the verified facts.
4. D-l, names: `free models`, `free providers`, `free providers + deposit` in CLI output and the picker.
5. Tests (free-scope fixtures, paid-provider strictness, 47-character key id, tier typos, `retier` with a fake vault seam), pre-mortem P10-P12, risk rows, doctor WARNs, interpretations I9-I11. Open questions are unchanged: Q1-Q3 stand (Q1 now also covers I9-I11, lower risk).

## Revision 4 (2026-10-03): what changed and why

Why: the owner decided how helper calls are treated (D-m) and how pool aliases are treated (D-n), added a requirement that the helper-call classifier be MEASURED for reliability before enforcement, and the critic's second review (10 majors, 8 minors, 15 build ambiguities) is folded in. Same rules: only this file edited; no keysync run, no CCR or gateway action, no settings or `~/.llmkeys` write, no secret printed, nothing above sonnet, no sub-agents. Read-only checks made for this revision (all cited where used): `menu/style.mjs:676-692`, `keysync/keysync.mjs:29,51-56,86-88,912-918`, `menu/atomic.mjs:39-43`, `menu/catalog.mjs:44,781,852`, `test/contracts.test.mjs:216-232,270-286`, `refresh/bench-cli.mjs:54-57,103,350`, `refresh/bench-store.mjs:38`, `~/.llmkeys/ApiKeyVault.ps1:137-225` (structure only), and a REPLAY of `catalog/snapshot.json` (rebuilt 2026-10-03T09:49Z, schema 9, 64 rows, 6,432 model rows, per-row key ids now full length, longest 32) joined to `state/bench.json` (probe-ok only; observed overlay NOT merged), which reproduced the critic's numbers (V29).

1. D-m, helper calls: I8 is accepted as the critic recommended and split into I8a (requests with an agent id and NO tools are helper calls: passed through, `asked` served, in EVERY mode) and I8b (the haiku/small-fast exemption applies ONLY under `inherit`; under `dynamic` and `free` a tool-carrying haiku subagent is judged by the allowed set). The plain statement for the runbook and the `set`/`show` report: "free governs subagents with tools; background helper calls (titles, summaries, compaction, small-fast) still use the relay in every mode". Counters `auxOnRelay` and aux volume by model and bytes. A future strict switch `--aux follow|asked` (default `asked`) is described and NOT built.
2. NEW REQUIREMENT, classifier reliability (section 12.5, experiments E2/E3/E14, new E17-E19): the router logs the classification INPUTS (never bodies) and its class for every request in shadow mode, a labelled ground-truth protocol produces a confusion matrix, thresholds are proposed, and enforcement (G3) is BLOCKED until the matrix meets them; enforcement uses the classifier but rollback stays one command. `CLAUDE_CODE_GATEWAY_HINT_HEADERS=1` is evaluated as an optional authoritative signal (needs a Claude Code restart and an env change: owner approval) and the plan states what it does without it.
3. D-n, pool aliases: probe-ok pool/router aliases are ADMITTED but flagged `ALIAS` everywhere (funnel, `show`, `explain`, injected list, compiled policy), found by ONE shared single-constant rule (last id segment `auto|router|default|free`), never counted toward the verified tier (alias probe results are advisory, tier capped at `u`), ranked below non-alias rows at equal rank keys, and counted separately.
4. Critic majors M1-M10 and minors M-a..M-h and the build ambiguities are fixed; the table "critic finding -> fixed in section X" below lists each. Headline corrections: the live narrative "`free models` AND 1M is empty" is WRONG (14 of 112 are 1M) and is removed; FREE_TAG is tested on the BARE id after the `[1m]` strip; 860 of 1,687 probe-ok free-provider models (51%) have unknown context, so emptiness, refusal and FRAGILE now use the SUBSTITUTE pool; rank key 2b puts price 0, then unknown, then positive prices; the strict free-tag rule applies in EVERY free scope on paid and deposit tiers; an explicit EXECUTION ORDER is added (offline stages first, then G1, then G2a last) and the sandbox must pass on the exact `.next` bytes BEFORE G2; `retier` gets a RESUME branch and all vault work moves inside one PowerShell process so the secret never reaches node.

## Revision 5 (2026-10-03): what changed and why

Why: the owner asked that the toggle selector menu SHOW, IN REAL TIME as toggles are selected, how many models are INCLUDED under the current combination (D-o). Applied after revision 4, same rules (only this file edited, read-only otherwise, nothing above sonnet, no sub-agents). Read-only checks for this revision: `test/bench-counts.test.mjs:253-275` (the picker import-graph rule), `test/bench-startup.mjs` (`BUDGET_MS = 300`, hard), `menu/pick-state.mjs:513-690` (the key dispatch), `menu/legend.mjs:28-46` (the complete key list), `menu/uwpick.mjs:372-390` (lazy bench load), `menu/style.mjs:33` (`FRAME_MIN = 78`), `docs/qa-interactive-protocol.md`.

1. D-o and a new stage S4b ("toggle pane with live counts in uwpick"), after S1 and S4: a modal pane opened by `ctrl+t` that edits the four toggles as a PREVIEW, recomputes the included-model counts on every toggle change, shows verified / small / unverified / ALIAS / not-substitutable splits, the three free-scope counts, empty and fragile providers with the same warning as `set`, the effective policy line, and "counts as of" staleness; Enter saves the owner policy file through the shared writer, Esc discards, nothing is written while browsing.
2. ONE shared pure function: the compile/funnel logic moves into `menu/subagent-funnel.mjs` (pure, no I/O, no builtins) which `keysync/subagent-policy.mjs` (the I/O shell of `set`/`show`/`explain`/`rebuild`) AND the pane import, so CLI and picker counts cannot disagree. Location forced by the boundary rules: the picker import graph may not reach `refresh/`, `keysync/` or `menu/catalog.mjs` (static imports), so the funnel and the tier vocabulary (`menu/tiers.mjs`, moved from `keysync/`) live in `menu/`; the pane module is loaded by DYNAMIC import on first `ctrl+t`, so the 300 ms startup budget and the import-graph test are unchanged.
3. Cost budget and tests: recompute on a toggle change only (never per frame), under 20 ms on a 6,432-row snapshot fixture, memoised per toggle tuple; rendering tests at widths 80/100/132 in ASCII and Unicode; a parity test of pane counts against the CLI funnel for every combination; no-write-until-Enter test; legend and QA-protocol updates. Risks R-pane-1..4 and pre-mortem P13, P14. One new open question (Q4).

### Decisions (owner, final, supersede earlier text where they differ)

| ID | Date | Decision | Where applied |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-a | 2026-10-03 | Toggle 2 modes are `dynamic`, `inherit` (was `fixed`), `free`. `inherit` is used everywhere: CLI `--mode dynamic\|inherit\|free`, schema values, docs, tests. | 1, 5.1, 6.2, 7, 9, 12 |
| D-b | 2026-10-03 | No Opus/Fable cap. The rule "subagents never above sonnet" concerns UW's helper agents only (the agents that build this feature), NOT the policy output. Show price per row; warn at `set` and `show` when `inherit` would inherit an Opus- or Fable-priced main. An optional `--max-price` is a later idea, not built. | 1, 5.2, 7, 12.4, 13, 16 |
| D-c | 2026-10-03 | Empty set. At `set`: REFUSE only when the set is empty for EVERY provider or for main's current provider (`--allow-empty yes` overrides); otherwise warn, list the other empty providers (compiled `emptyProviders[]`) and save. At runtime on an empty set serve the model the subagent ASKED for (today's behaviour) with a once-per-session `EMPTY_SET` entry in the decision log and a status warning (and the injected warning when injection is on). Never main's model, never block. | 5.2, 6.1, 6.2, 7 |
| D-d | 2026-10-03 | `inherit` + `ctx 1m` when main is below 1M: INHERIT main anyway and show a warning. `inherit` overrides Toggle 1 (source), because inheriting is by definition same-provider. | 6.2, Interpretations I1, I3, I4 |
| D-e | 2026-10-03 | Legacy `claude-opus-5` slot hot-switch: RETIRE. The substring bug is fixed NOW (stage S0b): rewrite only on EXACT match (`anthropic/claude-opus-5` or bare `claude-opus-5`), never `includes`, and decide/learn from the ORIGINAL `asked`; the slot code is removed entirely at the first enforced stage (G3). The production router stays at `spike/uw-router.cjs` for now; doctor FAILS when `CUSTOM_ROUTER_PATH` differs from the repo file or the file is missing; moving it to a supported folder is a later, separate change. | 6.6, 6.7, 9.1, 10, 12 |
| D-f | 2026-10-03 | Models with unverified tools are allowed WITH a warning until the tool-fidelity probe exists (surfaced in `set`, `show`, the injected list, a standing doctor WARN "N unverified models allowed", and a status field); then the 3-tier policy (section 11). | 5.1, 5.4, 6.4, 8, 10, 11 |
| D-g | 2026-10-03 | The main agent learns the allowed list through the router writing it into the Agent tool description. Default OFF, gated at G4, and only after the sandbox confirms the body edit reaches upstream (E4 PASS). | 6.4, 12 |
| D-h | 2026-10-03 | Rollout is SHADOW MODE FIRST. | 12 |
| D-i | 2026-10-03 | TOOL-VERIFICATION SCOPE. The tool-fidelity probe (L1 + L2) runs for ALL probe-ok models (1,875 on the 2026-10-03 snapshot plus bench replay), including the 77 with `tools: false`, because the catalogue false claim is untrusted too. The 13 Anthropic relay models are known-good by provenance and NOT probed (subscription route, tool use proven daily; the live observer never confirm-probes that route). The 175 probe-ok models on paid-tier providers go through the bench existing price ceilings and spend caps. L3/L4 (about 40,000 input tokens each at the real payload size; tens of millions of tokens for about 1,000 passers) are NOT part of the all-models pass: they run only for models that are policy candidates under the owner toggles, or that the owner pins, or as a separate opt-in per-provider-capped pass. L1+L2 for 1,875 models is about 3,750 requests and about 2.5M tokens. | 5.2, 8, 11, 12 |
| D-j | 2026-10-03 | FREE SCOPES. Mode `free` gets `--free-scope models\|providers\|providers+deposit`, default `providers`. `models` = probe-ok models carrying a free label (badge FREE or FREE? or the id free tag; replay: 112 carry the badge, 30 have a listed price of 0). `providers` = ALL probe-ok models on providers whose KEY tier is `free` (replay: 1,686 models on 45 providers, about 15 times `models`). `providers+deposit` = `providers` PLUS, inside providers whose key tier is `free-deposit` or `paid`, ONLY models whose id carries a free tag: STRICT owner rule, any model without a free tag is SKIPPED there; zero price and the FREE?/FREE badge are NOT sufficient signals inside paid and deposit providers; the free tag is the token `free` delimited as `:free`, `-free`, `/free`, `_free`, `.free` or the `free-` / `free_` prefix forms, never a substring of a longer word such as freedom; no per-provider override list; the model must still be probe-ok and never `pay`. Replay: paid-tier providers hold only 3 free-tagged models (alibaba qwen3.5-397b-a17b:free and qwen3.6-plus-free, both `gone`; deepseek-v4-flash-free on the deepseek paid key, `pay`), so `providers+deposit` equals `providers` today until a deposit unlocks something. The `subscription` tier (the Anthropic relay) is in NO free scope. Inside free-tier providers rank price-0 models first, then cheapest, to conserve limited credit; price on every row; a live 402 flips a model to `pay` and the compiled policy is rebuilt (sections 7 and 12). | 1, 5, 7, 8, 12 |
| D-k | 2026-10-03 | KEY TIER SOURCE AND VOCABULARY. The free scopes read the tier from the VAULT REGISTRY (`registry.json` `tier`, or the vault listing), NEVER from the snapshot `keyId`, which was clipped to 30 characters (issue #148: `personal.tokenforgeaistudio.free` shows as `personal.tokenforgeaistudio.fr`; the cap is now removed and the snapshot rebuilt 2026-10-03, V28; the registry nevertheless STAYS the tier source and the compiler cross-checks the snapshot key id against it, emitting `TIER_MISMATCH`). Tier vocabulary defined once as data: `free`, `free-deposit`, `paid`, `subscription`, `management` (management stays excluded as today). `key.mjs add` validates against it (a typo is refused with a message listing the valid tiers); a planned `key.mjs retier <id> <new-tier>` moves the stored secret to the new id without retyping and updates `registry.json` and `key-choices.json`. Separate small stage S1c. | 5.2, 7, 8, 12, V28 |
| D-l | 2026-10-03 | The free scope names shown in CLI output and the picker are `free models`, `free providers` and `free providers + deposit`. | 7, 10 |
| D-m | 2026-10-03 | I8 ACCEPTED as the critic recommended, split into I8a and I8b. I8a: helper calls (requests with an agent id and NO tools) are passed through, `asked` served, in EVERY mode. I8b: the haiku/small-fast exemption applies ONLY under `inherit`; under `dynamic` and `free` a tool-carrying haiku subagent is judged by the allowed set, so `free` still holds for it. The runbook and the `set`/`show` report say plainly: "free governs subagents with tools; background helper calls (titles, summaries, compaction, small-fast) still use the relay in every mode". Counters `auxOnRelay` and aux volume by model and bytes. A future strict switch `--aux follow\|asked` (default `asked`) is NOT built. NEW REQUIREMENT: the helper-call identification must be tested for reliability and accuracy before enforcement (section 12.5). | 6.1, 6.3, 7, 12.5, 14 |
| D-o | 2026-10-03 | LIVE COUNTS IN THE TOGGLE SELECTOR. The toggle selector menu shows, in real time as toggles are selected, how many models are INCLUDED under the current combination (for example: select free providers and the counter updates to the number of models available under that filter, contingent on the other selected toggles). Realised as stage S4b, a modal pane in `uwpick` driven by ONE shared pure funnel function (section 10.1). | 4, 7, 9.4, 10.1, 12, 13, 14 |
| D-p | 2026-10-03 | AUTO-REBUILD ON PICKER EXIT (answer to Q4, revision 6): YES, with two owner conditions. (a) A PERMANENT, always-visible warning in the toggle pane (not dismissible, shown every time the pane is open) and in the legend/help, stating that closing the picker after a saved change automatically runs `subagent-policy rebuild --if-stale yes --quiet yes`. (b) The rebuild runs ONLY when the toggle settings changed in that picker session (saved owner-file content/hash compared before vs after; no change means no rebuild and no process spawned). Non-fatal and quiet; a failure writes a status file that the next picker open shows; it never enables enforcement and never restarts the gateway. The CLI step stays available. | 10.1, 10.2, 9.4, 12.1 (S4b), 12.4, 13, 14, 16 |
| D-n | 2026-10-03 | POOL ALIASES ARE ADMITTED BUT FLAGGED `ALIAS` (reverses the exclusion idea). A probe-ok pool/router alias (openrouter/free, kilo/free, kilo/kilo-auto/free, orcarouter/free, anymodel/am/free, aihubmix/auto, ...) may be a candidate, flagged `ALIAS` in the `set` funnel report, `show`, `explain`, the injected list and the compiled policy. Identification: ONE shared single-constant pool rule, last id segment `auto\|router\|default\|free` (a stop-gap until a data-driven pool classification exists; the existing pool lists `menu/style.mjs:676-692` and `keysync/keysync.mjs:915-918` are reused or shared, never forked a third time). Alias rows never count toward the verified tier, rank below non-alias rows at equal rank keys, and are counted separately in the funnel. | 5.2, 5.3, 8, 11 |

Considered alternative for D-d, recorded and NOT adopted: the critic recommended treating `inherit` + `ctx 1m` with a sub-1M main as EMPTY (serve `asked`, warn), because the owner's text says Toggle 3 is "contingent on toggles 1 and 2" and a 1M filter on a one-model set is naturally empty. The owner chose inheritance (the mode's meaning wins over the ctx filter) with a warning.

### Interpretations of `docs/UW.txt:10-23` (the plan's reading where the text is silent or ambiguous; I1, I9, I10, I11 CONFIRMED as written by the owner in revision 6, I3 decided by D-d, I8a/I8b decided by D-m; open question Q1 is CLOSED)

| ID | Owner text | Plan's reading | Alternative reading |
|---|---|---|---|
| I1 (CONFIRMED r6) | Toggle 2 "fixed/inherit ... Contingent on Toggle1's provider selection" (`UW.txt:18`) | `inherit` overrides Toggle 1: the subagent gets MAIN's exact model, which is by definition same-provider, so `--source` has no effect under `inherit` (the CLI prints "source ignored under inherit"). | Under `all-providers`, `inherit` could mean "main's model TYPE" (an equivalent model from any provider). Rejected: it would make `free` always empty. |

| I6 | "Different provider: uses models from all providers" (`UW.txt:14`, numbered 2.2 in the notes) | `all-providers` is the union over ALL providers INCLUDING main's own (it does not exclude main's provider). | Strictly other providers. Rejected: the owner's phrase is "all providers". |

Without injection main only knows the alias enum, so `dynamic` is mostly a SET filter until G4. | Main sees the list via injection from day one. Rejected by D-g (default off, gated). |

Counted as `aux`, with `auxOnRelay` (the asked model is on the relay) and aux volume by model and bytes. Consequence stated plainly: "free governs subagents with tools; background helper calls (titles, summaries, compaction, small-fast) still use the relay in every mode". A real subagent that happens to carry no tools is also treated as a helper call (a bypass, counted in the matrix, M-d). | Rewrite helper calls too under a future `--aux follow` (NOT built; default would stay `asked`). |
| I8b | (none) haiku and small-fast env values (D-m) | The exemption applies ONLY under `inherit` (a haiku-asking subagent keeps haiku). Under `dynamic` and `free` a tool-carrying haiku subagent is judged by the allowed set like any other, so `free` still holds for it. | Exempt haiku in every mode. Rejected by the owner (it would leak paid traffic out of `free`). |
| I9 (CONFIRMED r6) | Free tag: "the token `free` delimited as `:free`, `-free`, `/free`, `_free`, `.free` or `free-`/`free_` prefix forms; not a substring of a longer word" (D-j) | Tested on the BARE model id: the id after the provider prefix with a trailing `[1m]`/`[1M]` stripped (the same collapse as section 8 stage 2), and on every collapsed sibling spelling, because `x:free[1m]` does NOT match the pattern (the `]` after `free` is not a delimiter) while the bare `x:free` does. Case-insensitive, one named pattern `FREE_TAG = (^\|[:/_.-])free($\|[:/_.-])`: matches `x:free`, `x-free`, `a/free/b`, `x_free`, `free-x`, `kilo-auto/free`; does not match `freedom`, `freeform`, `free2`, `x:free[1m]` (tested raw). It is slightly wider than the owner list on the trailing side (`x-free.v2` also matches). Rejected: it silently drops 1M-tagged free models. Exactly the six listed forms. Rejected: the owner intent is the delimited word. |
| I10 (CONFIRMED r6) | `models` scope: "today meaning: badge FREE/FREE? plus the id free tag" (D-j) | A probe-ok model with badge `FREE` or `FREE?` OR a free tag on the bare id, on providers whose key tier is `free` (where the tier is unknown, for example the registry is unreadable or a multi-key provider has no choice, the STRICT rule applies as the safe default and a warning says so). On providers whose tier is `free-deposit` or `paid` the STRICT rule applies in THIS scope too (M5; owner: paid-labelled providers use ONLY free-tagged models): a badge-only or price-only row there is skipped and counted `deposit-strict-skipped`, a free-tagged row there is admitted. `subscription` and `management` rows are never admitted. Revision 2 extra condition `pin === 0 && pout === 0` stays dropped (the badge is the catalogue own verdict and listed prices are often null); a badge row with a non-zero listed price is kept and flagged `PRICED_BUT_BADGED`. On the 2026-10-03 replay no probe-ok labelled row sits on a paid-tier provider, so the strict rule changes no live count today (V29). | Keep the price-0 requirement (about 30 models on the replay). Rejected: it contradicts "badge plus tag". Let badge-only rows through on paid tiers in this scope. Rejected (M5): it contradicts the owner strict rule. |
| I11 (CONFIRMED r6) | "providers whose KEY tier is `free`" (D-j, D-k) | Provider tier = tier of the key keysync WIRES for that provider: registry rows through `filterRegistry` then `chooseKeys` (`keysync/keysync.mjs:60-110`, honouring `key-choices.json`). The Anthropic relay row is tier `subscription` by construction (its snapshot key id is the literal `relay.anthropic.subscription`, `menu/catalog.mjs:852`, and the registry has no row for it). A provider with no resolvable key has no tier and is in no provider scope. | A provider counts as free if ANY of its keys is free. Rejected: a paid key could be the wired one. |

Denominators used below. "Snapshot" = `catalog/snapshot.json` schema 9, `builtAt` 2026-09-30 for the revision 1-3 figures and REBUILT 2026-10-03T09:49Z (same schema, 64 provider rows, 6,432 model rows, full-length key ids) for the revision 4 replay, V29. "Bench" = `state/bench.json`, `generatedAt` 2026-09-30T08:18:17Z, 6,193 records. "Candidate" = a snapshot route that is chat-capable, not a pool row (`mode !== true`), has a fresh-or-any usable bench record with `s === "ok"` (observed overlay merged, as `loadBench().get` does), and has `tools !== false`. Candidate counts below are the critic's replay on those two files (2026-10-03), BEFORE intersecting with the live `Providers[]` (not read: no CCR action allowed while planning): candidates 1,790; ctx >= 1M 341 across 20 providers; tools unknown 710 (the revision 2 replay also said free 20 and free AND 1M 0 under a narrower free rule: WITHDRAWN, see V29: `free models` 112, 14 >= 1M) (the other 1,080 are catalogue claims only; 0 verified). They supersede revision 1's 1,794 / 21 / 342 / 1 / 712, which were measured on the same files a different way; all of these are LIVE-DATA figures, informational and date-stamped, and no acceptance command depends on them (cr-M9: acceptance runs on fixtures). The first LIVE compile (S2, under G-read) measures the live intersection; S1 compiles only against fixtures.

## Revision 6 (2026-10-03, owner answers): what changed and why

Why: the owner answered the four open questions. Same rules: only this file edited, nothing else touched, no sub-agents, nothing above sonnet. Section 17 (S1 build brief) is UNCHANGED: S1 is offline and does not include the launcher change, which belongs to S4b.

1. Q1 CLOSED. I1, I9, I10 and I11 are CONFIRMED as written (I1: `inherit` gives main's exact model and ignores the source toggle; I9: the free tag matches slightly wider than the badge's end-anchored rule; I10: `free models` = badge OR tag on free-tier providers, strict tag-only on paid and deposit tiers; I11: provider tier = tier of the key keysync wires for it). I3/D-d and I8 were already decided. The Interpretations table marks the four rows "CONFIRMED r6".
2. Q2 CLOSED. The classifier-accuracy thresholds of 12.5 are ACCEPTED as proposed: T1 at least 99% (Wilson lower bound at least 97%), T2 zero helper calls rewritten in at least 200, T4 zero main misclassified; minimum samples 40 per subagent type and per helper type, 200 main, 3 sessions on 2 different days; a PASS verdict expires after 30 days (and on a Claude Code or CCR update, T6). No numeric change in 12.5, only its heading wording.
3. Q3 CLOSED (earlier). Premium (the D-b warning) = catalogue family `opus` or `fable`, OR output price at least $20 per M tokens (the named constant `PREMIUM_RULE`, section 8 item 11).
4. Q4 DECIDED YES (D-p), with two owner conditions. (a) A PERMANENT, always-visible warning in the toggle pane (not dismissible, drawn every time the pane is open, whatever the toggles or the save state) and in the legend/help, stating that closing the picker after a saved change automatically runs `subagent-policy rebuild --if-stale yes --quiet yes`. (b) The rebuild must NOT run when the toggle settings did not change in that picker session: `menu/uwpick-run.ps1` hashes the owner file `~/.llmkeys/subagent-policy.json` (SHA-256 of its bytes; absent file = the literal `absent`) BEFORE it starts the picker and again AFTER the picker exits; equal hashes mean no rebuild and no process spawned, different hashes mean exactly one rebuild. Non-fatal and quiet, bounded by a timeout (named constant, default 30 s); a failure writes `state/subagent/autorebuild.json` (`{at, ok, exit, ownerHash, message}`, no secrets) that the NEXT picker open shows (banner and pane) and the doctor WARNs on (`AUTOREBUILD_FAILED`); a success overwrites it with `ok: true`. The rebuild never enables enforcement (it only compiles; `enforcement` stays whatever the owner file says, and the pane cannot change it) and never restarts the gateway (read-only `getConfig`, no `saveConfig`). The CLI step `node keysync/key.mjs subagent-policy rebuild` stays available and is what the pane save text now mentions as the manual alternative.
5. Where the text changed: Decisions table (D-p added), 7 identifier-series line, 10 (doctor: `AUTOREBUILD_FAILED`; picker banner), 10.1 item 5 (layout sketch with the permanent AUTO-APPLY rows, "APPLYING A SAVE" rewritten, save text), 9.4 (acceptance tests 12-15 added, items 5, 8 and 11 adjusted), 12.1 S4b (scope and acceptance), 12.4 (risk row, R-pane-2, R-pane-5, P13, new P15), 13 (S4b lane), 14 (runbook note k), 15 (consequence), 16 (Q1-Q4 closed, no open question remains), 12.5 (heading wording only).

## Revision 7 (2026-10-03, S1/S1c build and review outcomes): what changed and why

Why: S1 (library, router `router/uw-router.next.cjs`, CLI, contract constants, tests) and S1c (`add` validation, `retier`) are BUILT in Phase A (offline, nothing live, no gate) and went through two reviews and a fix round. The facts below are DECIDED outcomes of that work, not proposals. Where older text differs, the section named in each item was updated and this revision wins. Only this file was edited. Section 17 (S1 build brief) is otherwise unchanged and still describes what S1 was asked to do; where its flag text differs from the built behaviour (item 7: its item 6 said the test-only file flags are all-or-nothing; section 17 item 6 was reworded in revision 8 and again in revision 9 item 2), item 7 here wins, as corrected by revision 9 item 2. Status at the time of revision 7 (superseded by revision 9 item 5): S1 and S1c were BUILT and UNDER REVIEW (no stage after them had started; G1 was not requested).

1. First `set` and `--source` (5.1, 12.1 S1 acceptance 8 and 9). A DRY first `set` with no owner file and no `--source` previews as `all-providers` and prints a NOTE saying so. A REAL first `set` (no `--dry yes`) with no owner file still REQUIRES `--source` and `--mode` and exits 1 (`E_USAGE`). Acceptance 8 and 9 were edited to carry `--source all-providers` explicitly so they do not depend on the preview default.
2. Router tag literal (4, `menu/ccr-client.mjs` row). The older sentence "the router carries no tag literal" is WRONG as built. The router keeps one constant `TAG_OPEN` for native-menu detection (the idempotence rule of 6.4: a description that already holds the native tag open gets our block plus the "replaces any list above" sentence and `NATIVE_MENU_PRESENT`). A contract test pins `TAG_OPEN` equal to `CONTRACT.subagentTag.open` and a scan test checks the router contains none of V16's banned needles (`x-ccr-client`, `uw-probe`, `UW_CCR_DATA_DIR`, `usage.sqlite`, `request-logs.sqlite`). The injected text is still pre-rendered by the compiler.
3. `emptyProviders[]` as built (5.2, 9.2b, 17 item 11, acceptance 6). S1 implements "providers that have rows in the candidate set but NO substitutable row" (this is what 9.2b, acceptance 6 and section 17 say), NOT "every provider with an empty pool". Consequence, stated plainly: a main provider with ZERO rows in the candidate set is NOT flagged by `emptyProviders[]`; it is caught at `set` time by refusal rule (b) (section 7). [CLOSED in revision 8: owner ruling Q5 option B, a separate compiled list `noRowProviders` is added in S4b; `emptyProviders[]` keeps the built meaning, see revision 8 item 1.]
4. Compiled-file shape as built (5.2). Additive fields beyond the earlier sample: per model row `g` (tie-group id: rows tied on all rank keys except the id key share it, used for the top-K spread), `fp`, `ft` (already described in the field notes, now present in the sample); `owner.classLog`; `inject.promptNote`; `counts.small`, `counts.alias`, `counts.idRejected`, `counts.depositStrictSkipped`; a `gate` object, written ONLY by a rebuild whose enforce precondition is unmet (then `compiled.owner.enforcement` reads `shadow`, the router raises `CLASSIFIER_UNMEASURED`, and `gate` is excluded from the hash); and `tiers` may include a `management` entry for a management-only provider (the entry is informational: such a provider is never admitted under any scope).
5. `contentHash` (5.2, 6.4). The hash is over an ALLOW-LIST of routing content, the constant `HASH_KEYS`: `owner` INCLUDING `inject` (the toggle value inside `owner`), `models`, `lists`, `exempt`, `ctxHints`, `emptyProviders`, `thinProviders`, `tiers`, `substitutable`. Counts, stamps (`compiledAt`, `builtFrom`) and the rendered inject TEXT are EXCLUDED, so an unrelated snapshot change cannot rotate main's prompt-cache prefix. `sameCompiled` is a separate check: it still rewrites the file when only counts or stamps change (so `show` stays current) while the hash, and therefore the marker line `[uw-subagent-policy v1 <hash>]`, stays put.
6. Enforce preconditions as built versus planned (12.1 S3, 12.5). S1 checks only: `state/subagent/accuracy.json` has verdict `PASS` and is younger than 30 days, and the Providers input is live (a fixture providers file stamps `providersLive: false`, which fails the live-only precondition). NOT YET CHECKED, and therefore G3 BLOCKERS that S3's preconditions and the G3 text now name: (a) the T6 Claude Code and CCR version match, (b) recorded E2/E3 results, (c) the compiled fit-fields check (precondition 4 of S3), (d) binding `accuracy.json` to measured evidence: a hash of the measured `classify.jsonl` window plus the compiler `contentHash`, produced by `harness/classifier-eval.mjs` (built in S2). Until (d) exists `accuracy.json` is a forgeable plain file, so G3 is an explicit OWNER ACT and is never inferred from a file's presence.
7. Fixture isolation (4, 9, 9.2b). [The rule AS BUILT is stricter than this item first said and is stated in revision 9 item 2, which wins: a required flag set per subcommand, and `--policy-file` alone is refused for `rollback` and `clear` by a specific rule and for the other subcommands by the incomplete-set rule.] Fixture mode is ON when ANY test-only file flag is present, and it applies to ALL subcommands. Optional file flags not given resolve to `<state>/_not-given/<flag>` (never the real `~/.llmkeys` or `state/`). `rollback` and `clear` with `--state-dir` alone treat the owner file as ABSENT; `clear` on an explicit path requires the file to parse as an owner file (so a wrong path is never deleted). `retier --registry-file` and `--choices-file` go together. The helper `test/fixtures/subagent-flags.mjs` refuses any `--dir` outside `os.tmpdir()` and any dir that is neither empty nor holds the marker file `.uw-subagent-fixture`, and copies fixtures with `COPYFILE_EXCL` (never overwrites). Incident that motivated it (2026-10-03): an executor ran the UNGUARDED helper against the real `state/` directory and overwrote `state/bench.json` and `state/observed.json`; both were recovered from `state/bench-history`. Lesson recorded: a test helper that writes files needs the same containment guard as the code it tests (memory: sandbox isolation, verify the full surface).
8. Key-tier handling per scope (7, 8 stage 5). The `free models` scope NEVER exits `E_KEY_AMBIGUOUS`: an ambiguous multi-key provider falls to the strict rule with a `KEY_AMBIGUOUS` and `NO_TIER` warning (matches the section 7 text for an unknown tier). The provider scopes (`providers`, `providers+deposit`) keep `E_KEY_AMBIGUOUS` (exit 4). A management-only provider is never admitted under ANY scope.
9. Funnel drop reason (8 stage 2). A route whose live `Providers` spelling is ONLY `x[1m]` (the bare spelling is not listed) is dropped with its own reason `only-1m-spelling-listed` plus an `ONLY_1M_SPELLING` warning, not under `id-rejected`.
10. Router budget facts (6.5, 9.1, 12.1 S2/S3), MEASURED 2026-10-03 on 1,800 rows over 60 providers: SHADOW decision p99 about 1.5 to 2.0 ms; ENFORCE first decision for a NEW agent p50 about 14 to 15 ms, p99 about 17 to 31 ms, dominated by `stickyPut` (temp file plus rename of the session agents file on the request path); warm (sticky hit) p99 under 1 ms. [SUPERSEDED by 6.5 and D-av: the sticky journal removed the enforce first-decision cost, the suite asserts loose ceilings, the strict QB-1 numbers are measured by the standalone perf script, and `slowReq` fires once per worker and once per policy change by design.]
11. Router observability (5.5, 6). Every decision line carries `ms`. With the policy file ABSENT the router stays completely silent: no state-directory creation, no status flush, no log line. Shadow logs the value ACTUALLY returned (`ret`), not the value it would have returned (`would` stays separate).
12. DEFERRED, ANNOUNCED (nothing is silently dropped): (a) sticky entries outlive a tightened policy for up to 6 h; runbook note at S4 and an optional drop under mode `free` when `policyHash` differs from the entry; (b) the legacy slot rewrite still acts on policy-chosen models until G3 removes the slot (D-e step 2); (c) rank key 2b gives price class 0 to rows that are free-tagged on paid or deposit providers, so they rank alongside price-0 free-tier rows [CLOSED in revision 8: owner ruling Q6 option B, they rank BELOW price-0 free-tier rows as the new class 0t; the code change is built and verified, see revision 9 item 1]; (d) under `inherit` the compiled file still carries `models[]` and `counts.allowed` (harmless, unused).

## Revision 8 (2026-10-03, owner rulings Q5 and Q6, G17): what changed and why

Why: the owner ruled on the last two open questions (both option B, decided, not to be re-opened), G17 (a management-tier provider contributes nothing) was built and tested, and fix round 2 of S1/S1c left behaviours the plan did not yet record. Only this file was edited (no code, no agents). Where older text differs, this revision wins; the sections named in item 7 were updated. Identifier series: `G17` is the owner's label for the build change recorded in item 3 (it is not one of the live gates `G1`..`G7`).

1. Q5 CLOSED, option B (owner): add a separate compiled list `noRowProviders`, built in S4b. Definition: `noRowProviders` is a top-level array of provider-name strings in the compiled file `~/.uw/state/subagent/policy.json`, sorted ascending by byte order, listing every provider that (a) has at least one chat-capable route present in live `Providers`, (b) has ZERO rows in the candidate set under the active toggles (source, mode, free scope, ctx, after the G17 exclusion), and (c) whose tier is not `excluded`. It is DISJOINT from `emptyProviders[]`, whose semantics do NOT change (providers that HAVE rows in the candidate set but no substitutable row; acceptance 6 and section 17 item 11 still expect `emptyProviders[]` to be exactly [`fx-free-b`]); a provider is in at most one of the two lists and their union is the set of providers with an empty substitute pool. Under `inherit` no candidate set is computed and the field is `[]`. HASH: it is routing content and is therefore IN `HASH_KEYS` (the first compile after S4b rotates `contentHash` once because the allow-list gains a key). STRUCTURE: it is built with the same null-prototype structures and read through `own()` like `substitutable` and `tiers`, so a provider named `__proto__` or `constructor` is an ordinary list entry. The router does NOT read it (it stays informational: picker, pane, `show`, `explain`, doctor). INTERACTION WITH THE `set` REFUSAL: rule (b) of section 7 STAYS exactly as written (a `same-provider` `set` whose main provider has an empty substitute pool, zero rows included, is refused at `set` time, exit 2); `noRowProviders` is the STANDING signal for the case refusal cannot cover: after the policy is saved the owner later switches main to a provider that has no rows (for example the Anthropic relay under mode `free`, which is in no free scope). Built in S4b: (i) the funnel (`menu/subagent-funnel.mjs`) returns the list and the compiler stores it; (ii) funnel output: `set` and `show` print `NO ROWS: <list>` and, when main's provider (the refusal-rule-(b) source rule: newest learned main, else likely main) is in it, `NO ROWS FOR MAIN: <provider> has no row under mode <m> (<scope>)`, adding "its subagents run on the model they ask for (D-c)" under `same-provider` and "informational: no row of this provider is in the allowed set" under `all-providers`; (iii) picker: when a provider is picked as the new main and it is in `noRowProviders` (or in `emptyProviders[]`) the picker note of section 10 is drawn, and the pane shows a `NO ROWS` row beside `EMPTY`, with the same text as `set`; (iv) doctor: WARN `NO_ROWS_FOR_MAIN` when main's current provider is in `noRowProviders` (beside the existing WARN for `emptyProviders[]`). TESTS (S4b, fixtures of 9.2b; counts carry the fixture denominator, derived from the rows and to be asserted): the parity test of 9.4 item 2 covers `noRowProviders` between CLI and pane for all 36 combinations; a zero-row main provider produces the picker note (the fixture relay under `free providers` is in `noRowProviders` and NOT in `emptyProviders[]`; derived values: `free providers` gives [`anthropic`, `fx-dep`, `fx-paid`], `free providers + deposit` gives [`anthropic`], `free models` gives [`anthropic`, `fx-free-b`], `dynamic` all-providers gives []); `show` prints the line; the doctor WARNs on main's current provider and stays silent when main's provider has rows; `emptyProviders[]` is still exactly [`fx-free-b`] in acceptance 6; the lists are disjoint in every combination; a provider named `__proto__` or `constructor` is listed and never alters a prototype; the hash changes when the list changes and not when only counts change; the excluded-tier fixture provider `fx-mgmt` is in neither list.

2. Q6 CLOSED, option B (owner): rank free-tagged rows on paid or deposit providers BELOW true price-0 free-tier rows. Price classes under rank key 2b become, in order: class 0 = listed price 0 on a row that entered through a FREE-TIER provider (`fp` 1); class 0t = a row admitted by the strict free-tag rule on a paid or deposit provider (`ft` 1), whatever its listed price, below class 0 and above unknown; unknown price (free-tier rows); positive price, ascending by `pin + pout` (input plus output). Admission is unchanged: `ft` rows are still admitted only under the strict free-tag rule and must stay probe-ok and never `pay`; this changes ORDER only. Key 2b still exists only under `mode free` with scope `providers` or `providers+deposit` (in practice `ft` rows reach it only under `providers+deposit`; under `models` and under `dynamic` and `inherit` the key does not exist). Position of the key is UNCHANGED (after key 1 tool tier and key 2 probe health, before key 3 context): so, as for every existing class, key 2 health still outranks the price class, and the ordering test below holds for rows equal on keys 1 and 2 while key 3 context and key 4 TTFT, which come AFTER 2b, can never overturn it (recorded interpretation of the owner's "regardless of health/TTFT": moving 2b above key 2 would also reorder free-tier-only rankings and the owner asked that ranking stay unchanged). THIS WAS A CODE CHANGE to `menu/subagent-funnel.mjs` (the S1 code first gave `ft` rows class 0); it is BUILT AND VERIFIED as described here (revision 9 item 1). Tests for that fix: (a) ordering: class 0 rows rank above class 0t rows whatever their TTFT bucket (key 4) or context (key 3), with keys 1 and 2 held equal for the pair; (b) the top-3 spread (K = 3, tie groups `g`) uses the new order: a class 0t row is not in the best tie group while a class 0 row exists, and fills the top 3 only after the class 0 rows; (c) fixture counts of acceptance 4-7 unchanged (5 / 7 on 2 providers / 9 on 4 providers, `deposit-strict-skipped` 2, ALIAS 2, `emptyProviders[]` = [`fx-free-b`]); (d) the ranking of a free-tier-only fixture (`free providers`, no `ft` rows) is byte-identical before and after. Consequence: `contentHash` re-rotates once on the first compile after the fix for any policy that had `ft` rows (none on the 2026-10-03 live replay, V29, which has no probe-ok tagged paid-tier row).

3. G17 (done, built and tested): a provider whose tier is `excluded` in `menu/tiers.mjs` (`management`) contributes NOTHING to any mode, source, ctx or scope: no rows, no `models[]` entry, no list, no `substitutable`, no ranking, and nothing in `counts.allowed`, `counts.verified`, `counts.small`, `counts.unverified`, `counts.premium`, `counts.payloadRisk`, `counts.payloadUnknown`, `counts.alias`, `chosenScopeN`, the injected text or the hash; `explain` prints the excluded-tier line. Consequence for the fixture counts (9.2b, 12.1 acceptance 7, 17): the dynamic all-providers population was 15 rows before G17 (the 14 admissible rows plus `fxm-one`); it is 14 now, so acceptance 7 prints `ALLOWED: 14 models (of 14 in the chosen scope; verified 3, small 0, unverified 11)` and `SUBSTITUTABLE: 10 of 14 allowed`, and `explain` ranks "of 14". Earlier-stage counts are unchanged (inProviders 17, benchOk 15, toolsPass 15: those stages count routes before the tier exclusion); acceptance 4, 5 and 6 are unchanged; the free-scope counts 5 / 7 / 9 stay as they are. (The plan text before this revision never printed a literal `ALLOWED 15` or "of 15"; acceptance 7 only said "prints the funnel", so the explicit figures were ADDED in 12.1, 9.2b and 17 rather than replaced.)

4. Fixture-mode rule: the FINAL, as-built text is revision 9 item 2 (it is stricter than the wording this item first carried: the built code requires a flag set per subcommand and refuses a lone `--policy-file` for `rollback` and `clear`; the rule is not repeated here). The fixture helper `test/fixtures/subagent-flags.mjs` also rejects links, foreign files and a temp root that contains the home directory.

5. Behaviours built in fix round 2 that the plan did not describe (recorded; DECIDED by being built and reviewed, no new decision needed): (a) `retier`: resuming an old credential that has no registry row needs `--force yes`; old and new values are compared IN PROCESS before the old credential is deleted; a different value already at the new id is never overwritten (refusal); a fixture pair never reaches the real credential store (its `exists` seam is a no-op); the `done` branch (nothing left to do) requires evidence or exits 1 `E_USAGE`. (b) Router: session files are capped at 256 (past the cap a NEW session's state is memory-only and the returned model is unchanged); gc deletes every stale file. (c) `show` prints the router's effective mode and any gate; the `CLASSIFIER_UNMEASURED` router warning clears when the compiled file carries no gate. (d) `rebuild` prints `KEY_AMBIGUOUS` and `NO_TIER` and stores them in `builtFrom.tierWarnings`, which is OUTSIDE the hash. (e) The funnel and the compiler use null-prototype structures and `own()`; provider names such as `__proto__` and `constructor` are in the threat model and tested. (f) The fixture helper rules of item 4.

6. ANNOUNCED DEFERRALS (open known gaps; nothing is silently dropped; each is also listed in section 16): (a) the injected-text TEMPLATE (the `renderBlock` head and notice wording) is NOT covered by `contentHash` (the hash covers `owner` including `inject`, not the rendered text), so a template edit does not rotate the marker line; (b) `Win32CredManager` / `ApiKeyVault.ps1` function shapes (an atomic `Save-KeyRegistry`, `Write` taking a plain string, dot-sourcing printing nothing) are UNVERIFIED by any reviewer, because a classifier denied the read; the owner, or a permitted read, must confirm them before `retier` is ever run for real; (c) the enforce first-decision latency (about 15 ms, `stickyPut`) stays an open G3-sandbox item (revision 7 item 10); (d) binding `accuracy.json` to measured evidence is S2 work (revision 7 item 6 (d)).

7. Where the text changed: the header status; revision 7 items 3 and 12(c) and the note on item 7 (closed or re-pointed); 5.2 (sample gains `noRowProviders`, field notes, `HASH_KEYS` lists, G17 sentence); 5.3 (rank key 2b and the key-order line); 7 (`show`, the exit-1 row, refusal rule (b) interaction); 8 (item 5 price bullet, item 10, G17 sentence); 9.1 (item 17 of the library tests, new tests); 9.2b (counts and ranks); 9.4 (items 2 and 4); 10 (doctor, picker note); 10.1 (pane rows); 12.1 (acceptance 7, S4b); 16 (Q5 and Q6 closed, deferrals, no open question); 17 (items 6, 8, 11).

## Revision 9 (2026-10-03, Phase A verifier outcomes): what changed and why

Why: the Phase A build (S1, S1c and the BUILD parts of S0 and S0b) went through code review, security review, 3 fix rounds and the Phase A verifier (2026-10-03). This revision corrects statements of what was BUILT and VERIFIED so the plan matches the code. It changes NO owner decision and NO acceptance threshold. Only this file was edited (no code, no agents). Where older text differs, this revision wins. Identifier series: `Q7` is the one open owner question (section 16); `G1` and `G2a` are the live gates of 12; `E*` and `A*` are the 12.2 experiments and the 9.2 sandbox assertions.

1. Rank key 2b, class 0t: BUILT AND VERIFIED (revision 8 item 2, owner ruling Q6). `menu/subagent-funnel.mjs:256-266` ranks the price classes 0 (price 0 on a free-tier row), 0t (strict-free-tag row on a paid or deposit provider), U (unknown price), P (positive price); the test asserts the numeric classes [0, 1, 1, 2, 3] (0, 0t, 0t, U, P); the acceptance counts are unchanged (5 / 7 on 2 providers / 9 on 4 providers, `deposit-strict-skipped` 2, ALIAS 2, `emptyProviders[]` = [`fx-free-b`]); the ranking of a free-tier-only fixture is byte-identical. The statements "as built, `ft` rows get class 0" and "class 0t is a pending separate fix" (revision 7 item 12(c), revision 8 item 2, 5.3, 16 Q6, 17 item 8) were rewritten as built.

2. Fixture-mode rule AS BUILT (`keysync/subagent-policy.mjs`). This replaces the wording of revision 7 item 7, revision 8 item 4, section 4, 9.1 and 17 item 6 wherever they differ; the code is STRICTER than the plan said (it had said one file flag is enough, and that `--policy-file` alone is refused without separating the two reasons given below). The rule: fixture mode is ON when ANY test-only file flag is present, and a REQUIRED SET of flags must then be given together. `set`, `explain` and `rebuild` require `--policy-file`, `--state-dir` and `--providers-file`. `show` requires `--policy-file` and `--state-dir`. `rollback` and `clear` accept `--state-dir` alone (the owner file is then treated as absent and left alone) and REFUSE `--policy-file` alone (it would pair a fixture owner file with the real state folder). An incomplete set exits 1 (`E_USAGE`) naming the missing flags; so `--policy-file` alone is refused by the incomplete-set rule for `show`, `set`, `explain` and `rebuild`, and by the specific rule above for `rollback` and `clear`: two different rules. Blank or whitespace-only values are rejected (exit 1). An explicit path under `~/.llmkeys`, `~/.claude`, `~/.uw/state` or `~/.uw/catalog` is REFUSED (realpath of the nearest existing ancestor of the path, compared case-insensitively, safe against `..` and junctions). Optional flags not given resolve to `<state>/_not-given/<flag>`, never to the real `~/.llmkeys` or `state/`. `clear` on an explicit path still requires the file to parse as an owner file; `retier --registry-file` and `--choices-file` go together. The complete test-only file-flag list is `--policy-file --state-dir --providers-file --snapshot-file --bench-file --observed-file --default-model-file --registry-file --key-choices-file --vault-providers-file --settings-file --tool-fidelity-file`.

3. Probe router location (12.1 S0). Built as the TRACKED file `harness/probe-router.cjs`, OUTSIDE the scratch root, so the stock `harness/teardown.mjs` cannot delete it. The scratch router COPY of 9.2 (`harness/scratch/spike/uw-router.cjs`) is unchanged and still correct.

4. S0 orchestrator gates (`harness/subagent-e2e.mjs`). These are the DESIGN INTENT in the words below; the exact flag text is as built in Revision 9, see `node harness/subagent-e2e.mjs --plan` for the authoritative text (another executor was finishing the approval, TTY and file-hash details when this revision was written). `--plan` prints what a run would do, performs no I/O, and prints the sha256 of every file the run would execute. `--teardown` is identity-verified, ungated and idempotent. `--approve-plan` is an OWNER ACT: it requires an interactive TTY and a typed confirmation of the first 12 hex characters of the plan's sha256, and writes `harness\g1-approval.json` (outside the scratch root) with the plan hash and a timestamp; the approval is valid for 24 hours and is ONE-USE (consumed at the start of a run). `--g1-approved`: a run needs this flag AND a valid approval file that matches the plan. `--experiments-only` limits the run to the experiments; `--router probe|next|both` selects the router(s) under test. `--no-preload-guard` is accepted only with `--i-understand-no-guard <first 12 hex characters of the no-guard plan's sha256>` and its own approval. Evidence is retained, redacted, in `harness\g1-evidence\<timestamp>\` (gitignored through `harness/g1-*`). A run REFUSES to start unless the scratch tree is clean (`appdata`, `localappdata`, `claude-config`, `daemon-env.json` and `claude-settings.json` all absent: run `--teardown` first). The isolation proof is tree-wide (the daemon plus every descendant process). The S0 acceptance sentence "git status shows only new files under `harness/`" now reads: the new S0 files plus the modified `harness/config.mjs`, `harness/guard.mjs`, `harness/trial31/redact31.cjs` (about 12 lines of redaction rules used by the sandbox harness and its tests), `test/fixtures/no-real-state.mjs` and `.gitignore`, and the new `.gitattributes` (D-ax). G1 itself is still an owner gate (12).

5. Status. S1, S1c and the BUILD parts of S0 and S0b are BUILT, reviewed (code and security), fixed in 3 rounds and VERIFIED (Phase A verifier, 2026-10-03). G1 and G2a have NOT been run. The earlier text "the S0 and S0b BUILD parts have not started" (12.0) is replaced.

6. S0b deploy and restore as built (`harness/deploy-router.mjs`; 6.7, 12.1 S0b). `--restore yes` works only with exactly ONE backup; with two or more it exits 1 `E_AMBIGUOUS` and `--from <backup>` is mandatory. A deploy prints the ORIGINAL (oldest) backup path and a restore command that carries `--from`, and refuses with `E_NOOP` when the live file already equals the candidate. Rehearsals accept only paths under the OS temp dir (allow-list), so rehearse from a COPY of `router/uw-router.s0b.cjs` placed under the temp dir (copy-first step, documented in 6.7 and as runbook note (l)). UNC paths and the `\\?\` and `\\.\` spellings are refused. The smoke child runs with a minimal environment and its verdict is read from stdout with a nonce. The candidate is the owner's reviewed file and runs with the owner's privileges: a hostile candidate is out of the threat model.

7. Budget wording (12.1 S1 acceptance 13). The 10,000-call budget test ASSERTS p99 under 3 ms for shadow and warm decisions and PRINTS the measured value (observed in the Phase A verifier's runs: 0.17 to 0.45 ms p50, 0.35 to 0.9 ms p99). The earlier "prints p99 under 1 ms warm" is replaced. [SUPERSEDED for the thresholds by D-av and 6.5: the suite asserts loose ceilings and the strict QB-1 numbers are checked by `node test/perf/subagent-router-perf.mjs`.]

8. Fixture files as built. `test/fixtures/subagent-policy.sample.json` and the `*.sample.json` trio do NOT exist and were never built. The built fixtures are `test/fixtures/subagent/{snapshot,bench,providers,registry,vault-providers,key-choices,observed,default-model,settings}.json`, `test/fixtures/subagent-flags.mjs` and `test/fixtures/router-slot-v29-ids.json`. There is no frozen compiled-file fixture: the compiled-file schema contract is section 5.2 plus the shape test in `test/subagent-sandbox.test.mjs`, which compares the key set of a real `compile()` output with the sandbox's synthetic policy. Every citation (4, 5.2, 12.1 S1, 13) was re-pointed.

9. Line endings (CLOSED by D-ax, revision 10; formerly open question Q7). DECIDED: LF EVERYWHERE for the router files. The `.gitattributes` line `router/*.cjs text eol=lf` was CREATED and `router/uw-router.next.cjs` and `router/uw-router.s0b.cjs` were converted from CRLF to LF on 2026-10-05 (sha256 `5da75baa...` and `6efeb03d...`; full values in D-ax). The live `spike/uw-router.cjs` stays CRLF until G2a replaces it (the deploy writes the LF candidate, harmless to Node; the S0b differential is EOL-insensitive). What this replaces: before the decision the next file was LF and the s0b and spike files CRLF, `core.autocrlf=true` at SYSTEM level and no `.gitattributes`, so a checkout would have changed the hash. The bytes are now LF in the working tree AND in a clean checkout, so the raw and LF-normalised hashes are equal; the harness may still print both, and the G1 evidence records the sha256 of the router bytes it ran.
10. Housekeeping. `harness/bootstrap-live-safe.mjs` is UNTRACKED but is executed as step 2 of the S0 run, so it must be committed with the S0 set BEFORE G1. The verifier's group (a) commit set. MODIFIED: `.gitignore`, `harness/config.mjs`, `harness/guard.mjs` (still being modified when this revision was written), `keysync/key.mjs`, `menu/cc-contract.mjs`, `menu/ccr-client.mjs`, `menu/style.mjs`, `test/contracts.test.mjs`, `test/fixtures/no-real-state.mjs`, `harness/trial31/redact31.cjs` (about 12 lines of redaction rules used by the sandbox harness and its tests; in the commit set). NEW: `.gitattributes` (D-ax; the line `router/*.cjs text eol=lf`; in the commit set), `router/uw-router.next.cjs`, `router/uw-router.s0b.cjs`, `harness/subagent-e2e.mjs`, `harness/subagent-sandbox-spec.mjs`, `harness/stub-upstream.mjs`, `harness/deploy-router.mjs`, `harness/probe-router.cjs`, `harness/bootstrap-live-safe.mjs`, `keysync/retier.mjs`, `keysync/subagent-policy.mjs`, `menu/pool-rule.mjs`, `menu/subagent-funnel.mjs`, `menu/tiers.mjs`, the tests `test/subagent-sandbox.test.mjs`, `test/router-slot-hotfix.test.mjs`, `test/subagent-router.test.mjs`, `test/subagent-policy.test.mjs`, `test/subagent-cli.test.mjs`, `test/subagent-contract.test.mjs`, `test/keysync-tiers.test.mjs`, `test/fixtures/subagent-flags.mjs`, `test/fixtures/subagent/*.json`, `test/fixtures/router-slot-v29-ids.json`, and `plans/subagent-policy-plan.md`. NOT ours and NOT to be committed with that set: the keysync probe scripts, `menu/render-*.mjs`, `docs/CCR-Stack-Runbook.md`, `harness/trial31/isolation-proof-3.1.1.md`, `plans/artifacts/`, `plans/bench-study/*`, `plans/ccr-*-patch-plan.md`. `docs/UW.txt` is an owner file that the plan cites; the owner decides about it.

11. ANNOUNCED DEFERRALS and gaps (no new decision; each stays open on purpose and is named here so nobody discovers it later as a miss): (a) the code's incomplete-set rule is stricter than the plan said (item 2, now corrected); (b) `keysync/subagent-policy.mjs` (about line 623): when the shadow-flag removal fails, a line is printed (being added when this revision was written; not yet confirmed in the code); (c) the G1 experiments A0, A1, A2, A8, A11, E4, E5, E7, E12, E13, and ALL G2a post-swap live checks, are NOT runnable offline; (d) the preload guard's compatibility with CCR 3.0.22 is untested until G1; (e) the credential-manager check compares target names only; (f) the `Win32CredManager` / `ApiKeyVault.ps1` function shapes remain unverified (the same gap as deferral (2) in section 16).

1. ROUTER V2 SCOPE (stage S1r; each item carries its `ar-` id; the mechanics that reconcile with older sections are in 6.1b):
   - ar-1: agent-id charset `^[A-Za-z0-9_@.:-]{1,128}$` (replaces the `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}` rule that 6.1 `cleanAgentId` used: team agent ids such as `ccr-logs@session-f49cde2f` were rejected and misclassified as `main`); parent-agent-id lineage is recorded (header `x-claude-code-parent-agent-id`, whose presence X4 checks) and a request whose agent-id header is present but fails the charset is counted `agentIdOdd` instead of silently becoming `main`.
   - ar-2: the sticky map is an IN-MEMORY authority plus an append-only journal `agents-<sid>.jsonl` (replaces the whole-file `agents-<sid>.json` write-temp-rename on the request path): a NEW agent costs about 0.1 ms against 14-31 ms measured for the old file (target; measured numbers pending the build); the reader tolerates a torn last line; other workers read the journal tail; compaction happens in the hourly gc.
   - ar-3: (a) NO sticky store for null-model decisions (empty-set, unknown-main, unresolvable: served `asked`, nothing recorded); (b) SLIDING TTL: a touch line is appended on use, 6 h sliding, 24 h hard cap; (d) per-session cap 128 and global cap 512 sticky entries, evicting the oldest WITHIN the session first; (c) OVERFLOW RESCUE IS NOT ADOPTED (item 6): sticky stays final.
   - ar-4: the pool scan exits early; a lazy resolvability index (a `WeakMap` keyed on `config.Providers`; X1 decides whether identity is a safe key) replaces the per-row `resolvable()` scan; the compiler emits `lists.prov` (compiled provider lists) and a band id `b` on every model row; BANDED SPREAD is DEFAULT ON (D-u).
   - ar-5: ZERO blocking sleeps on the request path: status and main writes are asynchronous with an in-flight guard; no timer at module scope; a request-scoped, `unref`'d retry timer exists on the FAILURE path only (X7 checks it fires after the cache entry is deleted); section 5.4 is reworded to "no timer outside a request".
   - ar-6: per-worker `status-<w>.json` and a `w` field on every log line; `show` merges the per-worker files (X2 measures how many workers there are).
   - ar-7: DECISION LOG V2: `agents.jsonl` with `v:2`, ONE line per NEW agent, retained as the current file plus 3 rotated generations, up to 4 MiB (D-al, as built); sticky hits are sampled (the first hit, then 1 in 50); fields `aid`, `pid8`, `w`; token-bucket rate limits (50 per second for decisions plus classify, 20 per second for agents) with a `logDropped` counter; backoff on ENOSPC; `status.json` gains `byModel`, `byProvider` and a latency histogram by path. See 5.5 and interpretation I12. [AMENDED by D-al: retention is the current file plus 3 rotated files, 4 MiB in all; the decision and classify token buckets are separate; new-agent lines are also written to `decisions.jsonl`.]
   - ar-8: `ROUTER_VERSION` constant plus a compiled `minRouter` (OUTSIDE the hash); `ino` joins the cache key (mtime, size, ino); the `contentHash` is re-verified on a cache miss; warnings `POLICY_NEWER` (compiled file demands a newer router) and `POLICY_HASH` (stored hash does not match the content).
   - ar-9: main-model freshness across workers: a worker re-stats `main-<sid>.json` every 5 s (a timestamp comparison on the request path, no timer).
   - ar-10: a compiled `rollout:{canaryPct:100,salt}` OUTSIDE the hash, implemented in the router but INERT: the owner did NOT adopt the canary feature, so NO `--canary` CLI flag is built. `enforcement`, `classLog` and (ar-17) `handoffNotice` move OUT of the hashed owner block (flipping them no longer rotates the prompt-cache marker). TRIPWIRE: during the first 200 enforced requests per worker the router watches; it TRIPS on an aux or exempt request returned with `ret != asked` (an invariant) or on router errors >= 5 within 60 s; a trip writes `shadow.flag` with the content `auto:<code>:<iso>`, raises the warning `AUTO_ROLLBACK`, `show` prints "AUTO-ROLLBACK (reason)", and ONLY an explicit `set` or `resume` clears it. `status.json.policy.headline` is a one-sentence plain-words summary.
   - ar-14: the funnel is memoised (measured 150-290 ms, target 25-45 ms; pending the build) and `rebuild --if-stale yes` exits early on a cheap stamp comparison.
   - ar-15: `namesOf` gets a cap and a fast path; session files are evicted at the cap (replaces "past the cap a NEW session's state is memory-only"); `lists.all` is `null` when it is the identity list.
   - ar-16: a monotonic time base for durations and a clamp of future timestamps read from files (a wall-clock jump can no longer extend or kill a TTL).
   - ar-17: HANDOFF (D-y; full text in 6.1b).
2. STICKINESS AFTER ROUTER V2 (this CHANGES revision 2's cr-B2 wording "a subagent's first enforced decision is final until a 6 h TTL, so a model never changes mid-life"). Rules now: (a) null-model decisions are not stored (ar-3a; supersedes rule (3) of the Stickiness paragraph in 6.1); (b) the TTL slides (6 h since last use, 24 h cap; supersedes rule (2)); (c) caps are 128 per session and 512 global (supersedes the 256 of rule (6) for the sticky map; `main` 64 and `mainBySession` 8 are unchanged); (d) THE ONE EXCEPTION: an enforced `dynamic` or `free` subagent with a sticky model that sends a RETRY signal is HANDED OFF to another model (ar-17, 6.1b). A model therefore changes mid-life only by handoff or after the TTL, or when its model LEAVES the whole compiled allowed set or becomes unresolvable (D-at; a `/model` switch alone does not). Overflow rescue is not adopted: a 128k substitute outgrown by turn 40 fails until the TTL, because a context failure is not a retryable failure the signal is known to cover (X3 records only a forced upstream failure, not which statuses Claude Code retries). Rollback is still not sticky (the lookup is skipped in shadow, with `shadow.flag`, or with the policy absent).
3. G1 EXPERIMENTS X1-X9 (D-r) are in the 12.2 table with their gates; the G1 acceptance line of 12.1 S0 is extended.
4. UX WAVE (D-s, stage S1d, section 7.2): `status`, `last [N] [--since]`, `preset`, a real `help`, `E_EMPTY` that diagnoses and offers runnable cures, verdict-first `set` and `show` with a delta line, `pause`/`resume`/`undo`, `why <CODE>`, `wizard`, the plain wording table, runbook section 5b. NOT adopted: the 'TRIAL (shadow)' rename.
5. FEATURES (D-v, D-w, D-x): self-test and drift doctor; report with savings counterfactual; extended auto-rebuild. Each is in 7.2 and its stage in 12.1.
6. NOT ADOPTED OR DEFERRED (announced with reasons; nothing is silently dropped): (a) CANARY ROLLOUT feature and CLI: the compiled field is inert and no `--canary` flag is built; (b) OVERFLOW RESCUE (ar-3(c)): sticky stays final; risk stated in item 2; (c) the TRIAL display rename; (d) STATUS-LINE display of the served model through `subagentStatusLine`: the setting is not verified from official docs and it needs a keysync-owned `settings.json` edit (PARKED, item 10); (e) PER-AGENT-TYPE rules: conflicts with the plan non-goal (no per-agent-definition pinning) and is possible only through the optional E18 hint headers; (f) OUTCOME-DRIVEN QUARANTINE; (g) a BUDGET GUARD that degrades; (h) a SHADOW A/B QUALITY CANARY; (i) LEAST-LOADED SPREAD (`ar-11`), kept as an S3 candidate after the X3 and X5 results (the retry-based DEMOTION half of ar-11 is read as superseded by the ar-17 cooling ladder, interpretation I13); (j) CANARY-COMPARISON AUTO-ROLLBACK (`ar-12`); (k) an EFFORT CAP.
7. 21. INCIDENT BEHIND D-aj (documented as the owner asked). A reviewer's EMPTY fixture variable (the flag set was empty) made `set` and `rebuild` run against the REAL defaults and WRITE `~/.llmkeys/subagent-policy.json` and `state/subagent/policy.json`; the owner approved deleting both files. Cause: the fixture-mode rule of revision 9 item 2 starts only when some file flag is present, so an empty flag set silently meant "live". Fix: the explicit `--live yes` of D-aj on every writing command on real paths (exit 1 `E_USAGE` naming the real paths without it; D-ar exempts `rollback` and `pause`, which only move toward safety and create no policy files when none exist); a test that an EMPTY flag set never writes. Amendment to older text: every place that names the picker or keysync auto-rebuild command line `subagent-policy rebuild --if-stale yes --quiet yes` (D-p, D-x, 9.4 items 12-15, the S4b acceptance, the pane and legend AUTO-APPLY warning text, R-pane-5, P15) reads with `--live yes` appended; those tests and texts are updated when S4b and S4 are built. Lesson (memory: sandbox isolation, verify the full surface): a test or review helper that can fall through to real defaults is a live-write path.

The two proposals CQ2 (P1, stale results) and CQ3 (P2, incremental probing) were put to the owner and are now CLOSED: CQ2 by never-expire (D-ap), CQ3 YES (D-aq), section 16.

## Revision 11 (2026-10-05): G1 passed, G2a and G2 done, probing strategy and sanity-audit decisions

Why: G1, G2a and G2 were run on 2026-10-05, the owner decided the tool-probing strategy and a set of ranking and coverage rules, and the first sanity audit of the shadow data (a critic pass, verdict SANE WITH FIXES) produced fixes. Same rules as before: only this file was edited, no code, no secret (the runtime-generated sandbox key is masked everywhere and is not recorded here). Every figure below names its population. Where older text differs, this revision wins; item 7 lists the sections that were updated.

1. G1 PASSED on 2026-10-05, attempt 4, run with `--no-preload-guard`. Attempts 1-3 stopped on: (1) the 3.1.1 preload guard false-alarmed on CCR 3.0.22's own better-sqlite3 `nativeBinding` load, and the owner chose to run without the guard; (2) assertion A0: CCR 3.0.22 names the profile key `profile:<mLe(id||name||agent)>` (`mLe` = trim, runs of `[^a-zA-Z0-9_.-]` become `-`, trim `-`, case kept) and the harness had matched a slug; fixed, with a sandbox-only key creation (`ensureProfileKey`, a runtime-generated key masked everywhere), a pre-flight before A0, `enhancedRoute` read from `profile.routing`, and X8 accounting for CCR stripping the leading billing block; (3) isolation proof #6 threw a transient `fetch failed` because CCR respawned its core worker (the core pid changes during `Router.fallback` swaps): the proof now retries transient network errors only, a bounded number of times, never a mismatch, and prints a RECORD line. The plan hashes and the typed approval of revision 9 item 4 are irrelevant now.
2. G1 RESULTS (details per row in 12.2). Probe router: A0, E4, E5, E7, E12, E13, X1, X2, X3, X4, X5, X7, X8, X9(a)-(d) and A11 PASS; X9(e) FINDING: CCR does NOT forward an upstream `retry-after` to the client when the fallback is off; X9(f) PARKED; NOT RUN: X9(g) and (h), X3 cases (b), (c), (d) and (f), X8 on the OpenAI-converted path, and X6 (the standalone host check): they go to S2d or to the standalone check. The about 400 KB `big` step (101 tools) runs for L3 passers. L6 `spawn` (`sp`). A1 ARGUMENT FIDELITY (`af`, rides in L1/L2), A2 LONG MCP-STYLE NAMES (`nm`), A3 `cache_control` TOLERANCE (`cc`), A4 BIG RESULT and ERROR RESULT (`br` with the big step, `er` with the L7 error-result case). All the new 1-character fields (`af`, `nm`, `cc`, `br`, `er`, `sp`, `big`) stay OUT of `lvr`; the third `lvr` character is the L3 verdict as a whole (`p` only when L3a and L3b both pass; the failing sub-step goes in the reason code; plan's reading). [SUPERSEDED by D-bv for the L1+L2 allowance: paid, free-deposit, management and UNLABELLED providers are SKIPPED ENTIRELY at every level, L1+L2 included; the relay is verified by provenance.] Enforced IN THE ENGINE; lifting it needs `--include-tier` plus an explicit `--levels` plus `--live` plus a printed per-tier cost plus dollar caps, and NEVER happens through an environment variable, a config file, the onboarding pipeline or the incremental entry point. This applies to the S6 onboarding pipeline too; D-bv replaces the L1+L2 allowance: a new paid key gets discovery and the response (bench) sweep ONLY. The TOOLS cell of such a key shows UNTESTED (`-`) with the note "not probed (paid tier)" (D-bv). LIMITS OF THE GUARD (recorded): the free-tier LABEL (the vault key id tier) is the ONLY guard for the deep levels; zero-dollar entries are bounded only by the per-provider token cap and rate limits; a MISLABELLED paid key would be probed deeply (by design); `--include-tier paid` lifts ALL paid providers at once (dry estimate under the caps: 6 paid models, about 276k tokens, $0.025). | 10.3 L, 12.1 S5 and S6, 12.4 |
| D-bu | PRICED MODELS ON A FREE-KEYED PROVIDER (owner, 2026-10-05). Models with a LISTED PRICE on a free-keyed provider are PROBED, not skipped: they are costed at their listed price, under the per-row ceiling (`--max-row-cost`, default $0.10) and an explicit `--max-spend`, which is REQUIRED whenever any priced entry is queued (the pilot uses `--max-spend 3`). A row over the ceiling is `pending: priced-over-row-cap` (111 of 1,639 queued free-key models at full depth). In the dry run of 2026-10-05, 383 of 1,639 queued free-key models carry a listed price (about $43.00 at full depth) and 1,256 are free-listed at $0 (383 + 1,256 = 1,639). The FULL-SWEEP DOLLAR CAP is proposed to the owner BEFORE the sweep and is NOT approved yet (open question CQ7). This refines the D-bt limits note: priced entries are now bounded in dollars, zero-dollar entries still only by the per-provider token cap and rate limits. | 11, 12.1 S5, 16 |
| D-bv | PROVIDER LABEL GOVERNS THE WHOLE TOOL PROBE (owner, 2026-10-05; SUPERSEDES D-bt's "paid keys get L1+L2 by default"). ONLY providers whose vault key tier is `free` are probed for tools at ANY level, L1+L2 included. `paid`, `free-deposit`, `management` and UNLABELLED providers are SKIPPED ENTIRELY for now (ledger: `excluded: not-free-tier (skipped for now)`); the relay is verified by provenance. ENFORCED IN THE ENGINE, so no entry point can send a request for them. Lifting needs `--include-tier` plus an explicit `--levels` plus `--live` plus a printed per-tier estimate plus an explicit `--max-spend`; a lifted tier may then get any listed level, 1 and 2 included. A NEW paid key at onboarding (S6) gets discovery and the response (bench) sweep ONLY, no tool probe. D-bu STAYS: priced models on FREE-labelled providers are probed under the ceilings, because the provider LABEL governs. The tier a provider gets must be the SAME EFFECTIVE tier the policy compiler uses for the owner's CHOSEN key (key-choices); the most restrictive tier applies ONLY when the choice is unresolved. This replaces the L1+L2 paid-tier spend of D-i and G7. | 10.3 L, 12 intro, 12.1 S5 and S6, 12.4 |

| sa-A7 | re-probe BEFORE excluding free-tagged `rate`, `empty` and `fetch failed` drops (15 + 2 + 2 of 246 on one 6-day-old sample, including aihubmix xiaomi-mimo-v2.5 models at 1,048,576 ctx); a transient state is not death (pillar: the responding provider is never pruned) | policy-side fix round |

### S5 security round (sec-s5, verdict GO-WITH-FIXES, fixed) and policy code review (cr-s5, GO-WITH-FIXES), revision 11

| Id | Finding and fix |
|---|---|
| sec-s5-B1 | a `cache_control` retry loop guard and a HARD ceiling of 12 requests per model (`pending: request-cap`) |
