## 0. Verdict in ten lines

1. Everything the owner asked for is restart-free, because the one hot lever CCR exposes is the custom router: CUSTOM_ROUTER_PATH is re-required on every request, and a JSON policy file the router reads is not part of CCR's config at all, so a policy change never touches `Providers` and never reaches `_E` (the restart predicate).
2. CCR 3.0.22 ALREADY ships the machinery this feature needs, and it is currently switched off by our own #47 fix: the `<CCR-SUBAGENT-MODEL>Provider/model</CCR-SUBAGENT-MODEL>` tag, the Agent-tool-description injection text, and `request.builtInSubagentModel`. Our router claims every request by returning `asked`, and a custom-router string beats the tag (policy order), so the tag is ignored today. The design reuses CCR's own grammar and wording instead of inventing one (section 3, finding F1).
3. A router body edit very probably reaches upstream: the router receives `n` whose `n.body` is the same object `r` that `routeRequest` later returns as `{body: r}`, and CCR's own enricher edits the same object the same way (F2). It is still tested in the sandbox, but the risk moved from "unknown" to "regression".
4. The empty-set requirement is not an edge case, but its live shape is not what revision 2 and 3 said. Live-data figures are INFORMATIONAL and date-stamped 2026-10-03 (replay of the rebuilt snapshot joined to the bench, probe-ok only, V29): 1,862 distinct non-relay probe-ok selectors (plus the 13 relay rows); `free models` (badge or bare-id free tag, I9/I10) = 112, of which 14 are >= 1M, so `free models AND >= 1M` is NOT empty on live data (the earlier narrative "`--ctx 1m` + free is empty" is withdrawn); `free providers` = 1,687 probe-ok models on 46 providers (the coordinator counted 1,686 on 45; the difference is not reconciled and is informational), of which 860 (51%) have UNKNOWN context and so fail the substitute floor and 772 are >= 128k; 392 carry a positive listed price, 30 a price of 0, the rest an unknown price. The Anthropic relay (13 models, tier `subscription`) is in no free scope, so `same-provider + free` with an Anthropic main is EMPTY under every scope by construction, not by data. The owner rule (D-c) stands: `set` refuses only when the SUBSTITUTE pool is empty for every provider or for main's current provider (M3), otherwise it warns, lists `emptyProviders[]` and saves; at runtime an empty set serves what the subagent asked for, which in `free` mode is a visible PROMISE BREAK (section 6.2 note h). No acceptance command depends on these numbers (fixtures, section 12).
5. The catalogue `tools` flag is untrustworthy for six separate reasons, one of them proven by five real models that pass the bare probe and fail under Claude Code's payload (section 11). Policy keys on a new verified tool-fidelity result (the tool-fidelity record, stored in its own file `state/tool-fidelity.json`), never on `tools`; until the probe exists, unverified models are allowed WITH a warning (D-f) and every output prints the unverified count (710 of the 1,790 candidates have `tools` unknown, the other 1,080 are catalogue claims only; 0 are verified), including a standing doctor WARN.
6. Rollout is shadow first (D-h): the router computes and logs what it would do, still returns the requested model, and one owner setting (`enforcement`) turns enforcement on. Injection into the Agent tool description is a SECOND, separate owner setting (`inject`, default off, gate G4, D-g) because it changes what the main model reads.
7. Never again a silent anchor fallback. Every router code path returns `asked`, and before returning a substitute the router checks it against the live `config.Providers` it is handed (an unresolvable string falls to the anchor `anthropic/claude-opus-5` silently, which is the #47 failure class). The same failure class applies to the ROUTER FILE itself: it is live and re-required per request, so a load-time error sends every request to the anchor (cr-B1, V21); it is developed at `router/uw-router.next.cjs` and deployed only through the section 6.7 protocol.
8. Main's model is learned in-process (a `globalThis` map survives the require-cache deletion) with a per-session file as the durable copy; only requests with a session id, no agent-id, no subagent billing marker AND an Agent/Task tool in `body.tools` teach it. A subagent's first enforced decision is sticky (cr-B2; revision 10: a 6 h SLIDING TTL with a 24 h cap, kept in memory plus an append-only journal), so a model changes mid-life only by the retry-triggered handoff of ar-17 (6.1b) or after the TTL.
9. The shared hot files (`spike/uw-router.cjs` only at deploy time, `keysync/key.mjs`, `docs/runbook.md`, optionally `keysync/run.mjs`) are touched by exactly one writer at a time; lanes are split by file, and the policy JSON schema is frozen as a fixture before any lane starts.
10. Eight stages, S0, S0b (the slot substring hotfix, D-e), S1, S1c (tier vocabulary and `retier`), S2 to S5, each with a runnable acceptance command (on fixtures, not live counts), a rollback that never restarts the gateway, and an owner gate before anything live. EXECUTION ORDER (section 12.0, owner choice): the offline stages first (S1, S1c, the offline BUILD parts of S0 and S0b), then G1 and the sandbox run, then G2a LAST among the pre-policy live actions, then G-read, G2, G3. Enforcement is additionally blocked until the helper-call classifier meets measured accuracy thresholds (section 12.5). REVISION 10: the order is REPLACED (12.0: router v2 S1r first, then G1 on its exact bytes), and stages are added: S1r (router v2), S1d (UX wave), the sub-stages S2a (report), S2c (self-test) and S2d (end-to-end scenario suite, G3 precondition (10)), and S5c (picker `TOOLS` column and legend).

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 1. Goal and non-goals

Goal. Three owner toggles decide which model each Claude Code subagent runs on, enforced at the CCR custom router, changeable by one CLI command, effective on the next request with no gateway restart, no settings.json edit and no keysync run:

| Toggle | Owner doc | CLI name | Values |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 source | `UW.txt:12-14` ("2.2 different provider" = models from all providers) | `--source` | `same-provider` or `all-providers` |
| 2 mode | `UW.txt:16-19` | `--mode` | `dynamic` (main chooses freely inside the set), `inherit` (always main's model; overrides toggle 1; was `fixed`, D-a), `free` (dynamic, restricted by `--free-scope models\|providers\|providers+deposit`, default `providers`: D-j) |
| 3 ctx | `UW.txt:21-23` | `--ctx` | `any`, the hard floors `128k`, `200k`, `256k`, `512k`, `1m`, or the soft preferences `prefer-256k`, `prefer-512k`, `prefer-1m` (context in tokens; D-bk expands the toggle; D-ag added `prefer-1m`: rows >= 1M form a higher band and smaller rows are used only when those are unusable or limited; `1m` stays the filter) |

Plus one sub-option of mode `free`, `--free-scope models|providers|providers+deposit` (default `providers`; shown to the user as `free models`, `free providers`, `free providers + deposit`: D-l), and two rollout switches that are not toggles: `--enforce shadow|enforce` and `--inject off|on`. The full reading of the owner's text, including where this plan interpreted rather than copied it, is the Interpretations table (I1-I11, with I8a and I8b).

Hard requirements: R1 no gateway restart to assign subagent models; R2 a prompt warning on an empty candidate set, through the exact paths listed in section 10 (not all paths are instant; native `/model` typed inside Claude Code is NOT covered); R3 policy keyed on verified tool fidelity, with the reason `tools` is untrustworthy understood and fixed.

Non-goals (stated so nobody builds them): no automatic task-class routing; no per-agent-definition pinning (owner pins are S5 and only for unverified models); no HUD change (`omc-hud.mjs` is the live statusLine and `menu/hud-shim.mjs` is not installed and cannot append text); no use of `Router.rules` or script rules (INERT in this config, `spike/uwmodels-web.mjs:272-276`; keysync refuses enabled rules at `keysync/run.mjs:1788-1789`; script rules also cannot read files); no use of CCR's native `modelDescriptions` menu (changing it is a `Providers` change and restarts the gateway); nothing on CCR 3.1.1 (`harness/trial31` is a record only, the owner stays on 3.0.22); no change to the six tier aliases or to `keysync/default-model.mjs`; no settings.json or `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` use (it hard-overrides in old Claude Code versions; the doctor asserts it is unset; revision 10 item 7(b): that rationale is out of date, current docs say a per-invocation value and a definition value beat the env var and `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL_FORCE` is the hard override, so the assertion stays but is re-dated by Claude Code version); no Claude Code restart as part of normal operation; no `--max-price` (D-b, later idea); no coverage of the native `/model` command typed inside Claude Code (it sets main's model and is not routed by this feature; documented, not fixed); no move of the router out of `spike/` (D-e, later and separate); no snapshot schema bump (cr-M3).

"Subagents never above sonnet" (standing constraint) is decided (D-b): it binds UW's helper agents, i.e. the pipeline that BUILDS this feature (every Agent call this plan names carries an explicit `model` of sonnet or haiku, per the memory note `subagents-never-above-sonnet`; planner, architect and critic default to Opus, so EVERY call must pass `model: sonnet`, section 13). It does NOT cap what the policy hands out: no Opus/Fable cap, price shown on every row, a warning when `inherit` would inherit an Opus- or Fable-priced main. An optional `--max-price` is a later idea and is not built here.

## 3. New findings that change the design (re-verified in the installed CCR 3.0.22 bundle)

Bundle: `C:\nvm4w\nodejs\node_modules\@musistudio\claude-code-router\dist\main\cli.js` (minified, single line; citations are function names plus approximate byte offsets, re-find with `indexOf`). The patched `.bak-*` copies beside it also contain the code, so the findings are not artefacts of our patches.

F1. CCR's native subagent routing. Constants (offset about 1,088,000): tag open `<CCR-SUBAGENT-MODEL>`, close `</CCR-SUBAGENT-MODEL>`, grammar `Provider/model` (slash, not the older comma). `BUe` is the text appended to the Agent tool's description: "the prompt parameter MUST start with `<CCR-SUBAGENT-MODEL>Provider/model</CCR-SUBAGENT-MODEL>` on its own first line ... When the tool model field accepts exact strings, set it to the same client model ID ... If the model field only permits built-in aliases, leave it unset and use the tag as the routing fallback. Do not omit the tag or put it in description or subagent_type." `OUe(body, config, profile)` runs in the claude-code enricher, matches tools whose lower-cased name is in `{agent, task}` (`TUe`) and appends via `rQ`, which is idempotent on the substring `<CCR-SUBAGENT-MODEL>`. The model list (`HUe`) comes only from `Providers[].modelDescriptions`, and `GUe` returns nothing when that list is empty. Live `Providers` has 0 providers with descriptions (research fact; the first read-only `getConfig` in S2, gate G-read, re-checks it, E11), so today the native text is never injected, and our claim-everything router would ignore the tag anyway.
Consequence: (a) our injected text copies CCR's wording and tag so it is the behaviour CCR was designed around; (b) in enforce mode the router must honour the tag itself, because "custom router string beats the tag"; (c) if a provider ever gains `modelDescriptions`, two lists appear: doctor warns (check `native-menu`).

F2. Router body edits reach upstream. `routeRequest(t)`: `r = t.bodyOwnership === "owned" ? t.body : deep-clone(t.body)`, `n = {body: r, headers: t.headers, ...}`, enrichers run on `n` (`OUe`, `MUe`, `VUe`, `jUe`), `n.sessionId` and `n.tokenCount` are set, THEN `resolveCustomRoute(n)` calls our function with `n`, and the method finally returns `{body: r, ...}` after only `r.model = selector`. `n.body === r`, so `request.body.tools[i].description = ...` inside the router mutates the very object that goes on. The trace confirms the order: `customer.custom-router`, `router.route-output` (mutation: replace `/body`, add headers `x-ccr-route-reason`, `x-ccr-route-source`, `x-ccr-routed-model`), then `plugin.request-transform:uw-schema-sanitizer` (V18), then upstream. Two corollaries: with `bodyOwnership === "owned"` the caller's body is edited in place and a retry can re-route the same object, so injection MUST be idempotent (marker-replace, section 6.4); and the sanitizer sees our injected text (harmless).
Still UNCONFIRMED until the sandbox run: exact bytes at the stub upstream, and whether an ADDED header (`request.headers` is the same object the pipeline reuses) reaches upstream and the request log.

F3. Detection signals available before the router runs: `VUe` reads `body.system[0]`, requires it to start with `x-anthropic-billing-header` and to carry `cc_is_subagent=true` (either `k=v;` form or JSON), then REMOVES that block (`t.shift()`) and sets `request.builtInClaudeCodeSubagent`; `jUe` finds the tag in `system` or in the first two user messages, STRIPS it from the body and sets `request.builtInSubagentModel`; `cFe` sets `request.sessionId` from header `x-claude-code-session-id` (lower-case keys, so `request.headers` is lower-cased) else `x-claude-session-id`, else `metadata.user_id` after `_session_`. So the router sees booleans, never the billing text. Live `request-logs.sqlite` (read-only, 2026-10-03): 28 rows, 23 carry `x-claude-code-agent-id`, 28 carry `x-claude-code-session-id`, 0 carry `x-claude-code-request-class` (hint headers are off, correctly). The 5 rows without agent-id are the main-class candidates; whether they carry tools is experiment E2. Critic-reported (live logs, not re-queried here): a 21 KB haiku request WITH `x-claude-code-agent-id` exists, i.e. an agent-id does NOT prove "a subagent doing real work"; it may be an auxiliary call (title, summary) issued under an agent. Classification therefore needs tools too (cr-M5, section 6.1) and E14 measures what that row is. All of this holds only for requests the enricher touches (V22): the sandbox must satisfy that gate (cr-M2).

F4. The trace is a visibility channel. `request_route_traces.trace_json.hops[]` records `customer.custom-router` (decision, and a `custom-model-not-configured` diagnostic when our string does not resolve) and `router.route-output` changes. `request_logs` has `requested_model`, `resolved_model`, `response_model`, `request_headers`. A live trace already shows a diagnostic `profile-model-not-configured: "anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5[1m]"`: that specific `[1m]`-spelled selector was not a configured `Providers` model. Softened reading (cr-m13): CCR's own model registry does know `[1m]` variants for 1M-capable models it ships, so this does NOT prove that every `[1m]` spelling is unroutable; it proves only that a selector must be exactly what `Providers[].models` lists (relay rows spell it bare). The compiler therefore emits the exact `Providers[].models` spelling and never the snapshot's `[1m]` id, and bare selectors remain safe either way (E6 settles the `[1m]` question in the sandbox, not as a dependency).

F5. Cost realities (critic replay, live data 2026-10-03, informational; section 0 item 4): candidates 1,790; 1M 341 across 20 providers; tools unknown 710 (the free figures of that replay, 20 and free AND 1M = 0, are WITHDRAWN: V29 gives `free models` 112 with 14 >= 1M). Snapshot `routable` is `true` on all 6,432 rows, so it does not discriminate; the live `Providers` check at compile time is mandatory.

F6. Two latent defects in the router we are about to extend, now fixed IMMEDIATELY rather than carried (D-e, stage S0b): a corrupt `slot.json` returns `undefined` and falls to the anchor (V1), and the opus-5 slot read applies to ANY request whose model CONTAINS `claude-opus-5` (V2, a substring test that rewrites longer ids today). The fix is exact-match-only rewrite that can never return `undefined`, deciding from the ORIGINAL `asked`; the slot code is deleted at G3.

F7. CCR's tag stripper is not specific to subagents: `jUe` runs inside the claude-code enricher for every matched request, so the literal `<CCR-SUBAGENT-MODEL>` is also STRIPPED from MAIN requests' `system` and first two user messages (cr-m12). A project file or CLAUDE.md that quotes the tag literal would be edited in flight, and a user pasting the tag would see it disappear. Runbook note (section 14); not a defect of this feature, but injection (D-g) makes the literal more common in main's own output.

F8. `n.tokenCount` and `n.sessionId` are set before `resolveCustomRoute` runs (re-verified in the bundle: `n.sessionId=o,n.tokenCount=i;` immediately before the call), so the router gets a cheap token count without stringifying the 0.2-2.7 MB body; this is what makes the context-fit check (cr-M1, section 6.2) affordable per request.

## 4. Architecture

```
owner CLI                                   state (local, gitignored: .gitignore:35 `state/`)
node keysync/key.mjs subagent-policy ...    ~/.llmkeys/subagent-policy.json      owner intent (toggles), CLI-written only
        |                                   ~/.uw/state/subagent/policy.json     COMPILED: toggles + ranked candidate lists + injection text
        v                                   ~/.uw/state/subagent/status.json     router -> world: counters, warnings
keysync/subagent-policy.mjs (library)       ~/.uw/state/subagent/decisions.jsonl router -> world: one line per subagent decision (1 MiB cap, 1 rotation) [REV 10: succeeded by agents.jsonl v:2, 5.5, I12; plus status-<w>.json, cooling.json]
  reads: catalog/snapshot.json              ~/.uw/state/subagent/main-<sid>.json durable copy of "main's model" per session
         state/bench.json + observed.json   ~/.uw/state/subagent/agents-<sid>.jsonl [REV 10, ar-2: append-only JOURNAL of the per-agent STICKY map (cr-B2); in-memory is the authority]
         state/tool-fidelity.json (S5)      ~/.uw/state/subagent/shadow.flag     compile-free rollback marker (router acts as shadow while present)
         live Providers (rpc getConfig, read-only)
         ~/.llmkeys/default-model.json (likely main)
         settings env (read-only: the six alias values, for the `exempt` and ctx hints)
         ~/.llmkeys/registry.json + key-choices.json (read-only: key TIER per provider for the free scopes, D-k)

router/uw-router.next.cjs   DEVELOPED here (never CUSTOM_ROUTER_PATH, never loaded from its repo location by tests)
        |  deploy only via section 6.7 (node --check, require smoke test on the exact bytes, temp + atomic rename, backup)
        v
spike/uw-router.cjs  (ONE file, re-required per request; THE LIVE ROUTER)  <-- CCR custom router
  reads: policy.json (stat + globalThis cache), shadow.flag (stat), cooling.json (stat + cache; REV 10), main-<sid>.json and the agents-<sid>.jsonl journal tail on memory miss
  writes: agents.jsonl (append; REV 10, was decisions.jsonl), status-<w>.json (async, coalesced; REV 10, was status.json), main-<sid>.json (async), agents-<sid>.jsonl (append), cooling.json (async, coalesced)
  never reads: snapshot, bench, tool-fidelity, config.sqlite, ~/.llmkeys   [REV 10, D-ae: it DOES read state/observed.json, read-only, stat + cache, as an age-capped soft hint, 6.1b F]
```

Constraint on the router: it cannot import ESM and CCR only re-requires the ENTRY file (helper modules stay cached), so all logic is in the one `.cjs`; JSON is read with `fs.readFileSync` + `JSON.parse`, never `require`. The Claude Code header names are duplicated in the router as literals; `test/subagent-contract.test.mjs` asserts they equal the `cc-contract.mjs` constants (the Phase 7 rule "coupling to Claude Code stays inside `cc-contract.mjs`" is kept by a test, because a `.cjs` cannot import it). The router file must not contain any literal from V16's banned list. It must also have NO module-scope side effect of any kind (cr-B1, cr-m4): no timer AT MODULE SCOPE (revision 10, ar-5: a module-scope timer would leak one per request because the file is re-required per request; the old rule "no `setTimeout`/`setInterval` anywhere in the file" is REPLACED by "no timer outside a request": the only permitted timer is a REQUEST-SCOPED, `unref`'d retry timer on the FAILURE path of an asynchronous state write, created inside the route function, never at load time, and the source scan checks that no `setTimeout`/`setInterval` call is reachable from module scope; X7 verifies such a timer fires after the cache entry is deleted), no file read, no throw, no top-level `await`; every `globalThis` map is created lazily inside a guard and bounded (cr-m5, section 6.3). REVISION 10, D-an and D-ao: CCR re-requires the router file on every request and each re-require leaks a Module record (12.5 KB per request for v2), so the router body is evaluated ONCE per process behind a stat-keyed `globalThis` wrapper (6.1b H item 7); the module-scope rule above is AMENDED to allow exactly that wrapper (EXACTLY one `statSync` of the router's own file, one `globalThis` lookup and one GUARDED splice of the module from `module.parent.children` in try/catch; D-aw, contract pin 17) and nothing else. `seam.tamper` and the `drain()` timer are not in the production file (6.1b I); tests use a patched copy. Test seam (cr-M10): `module.exports.__test = { reset, now }` hangs off the exported function; CCR calls only the function (V21 takes the module itself when it is a function), so the seam is unreachable from CCR; `now` is an injectable clock (default `Date.now`) and `reset` clears the `globalThis.__uwSub` state so tests are order-independent.

Where state lives, and why not an env override: the router derives its state directory from its own location, `path.join(__dirname, "..", "state", "subagent")`, with NO environment override. The live router can therefore never be redirected by an ambient variable (the `default-model` lesson, V13), and the sandbox gets an isolated state simply by running a COPY of the router in a scratch tree. Corollary: `router/uw-router.next.cjs` run from its repo location would resolve to the SAME `~/.uw/state/subagent` as the live router, so no test or script ever requires it from `router/`; every test copies it to `tmp/spike/uw-router.cjs` first, and a contract test fails if any test file requires the repo path directly. The CLI accepts `--policy-file <path>`, `--state-dir <path>`, `--providers-file <path>` (so tests never call `rpc()`, which reads `service.json` by default, V6), `--snapshot-file`, `--bench-file`, `--observed-file`, `--default-model-file`, `--registry-file`, `--key-choices-file`, `--vault-providers-file`, `--settings-file` and `--tool-fidelity-file` (M6; the compiler therefore never reads `~/.claude`, `state/observed.json`, `~/.llmkeys/providers.json` or `state/tool-fidelity.json` in a test), and ignores any ambient env var, printing a loud line if one is set. Safety rule (AS BUILT, revision 9 item 2): FIXTURE MODE is on when ANY file flag is present and applies to ALL subcommands, and a REQUIRED SET of flags must then be given together: `set`, `explain` and `rebuild` need `--policy-file`, `--state-dir` and `--providers-file`; `show` needs `--policy-file` and `--state-dir`; `rollback` and `clear` accept `--state-dir` alone (the owner file is treated as absent) and refuse `--policy-file` alone; an incomplete set exits 1 naming the missing flags; blank or whitespace-only values are rejected (exit 1); an explicit path under `~/.llmkeys`, `~/.claude`, `~/.uw/state` or `~/.uw/catalog` is refused (realpath of the nearest existing ancestor, compared case-insensitively, safe against `..` and junctions); an optional flag not given resolves to `<state>/_not-given/<flag>`, never to the real `~/.llmkeys` or `state/`; `clear` on an explicit path requires it to parse as an owner file; `retier --registry-file` and `--choices-file` go together. A half-fixtured run therefore can never mix fixture input with the real `~/.llmkeys` or `state/`.

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 5. Data shapes

### 5.1 Owner file `~/.llmkeys/subagent-policy.json` (written only by the CLI)

```json
{
  "schema": 1,
  "source": "same-provider",
  "mode": "dynamic",
  "freeScope": "providers",
  "ctx": "any",
  "enforcement": "shadow",
  "inject": "off",
  "unverified": "allow-warn",
  "allow": [],
  "setAt": "2026-10-03T12:00:00.000Z"
}
```
`source`: same-provider | all-providers (ignored under `inherit`, I1). `mode`: dynamic | inherit | free (D-a; the value `fixed` is NOT accepted anywhere, there is no alias, because no file exists yet). `freeScope`: models | providers | providers+deposit (D-j; default `providers`; read only when `mode` is `free`, and `set --free-scope` with a non-free mode is refused with a message). `ctx`: any | 128k | 200k | 256k | 512k | 1m | prefer-256k | prefer-512k | prefer-1m (revision 11, D-bk: the hard floors `128k`..`1m` exclude rows below them and an empty result triggers the empty-set warning; the soft `prefer-*` values form a higher band and exclude nothing; revision 10, D-ag: `prefer-1m` is new; `any` has no effect, `prefer-1m` makes rows >= 1M a higher band, `1m` is the unchanged filter). `classLog`: on | off (D-m, default on; the classification-input log of section 5.5). `enforcement`: shadow | enforce. `inject`: off | on. `unverified`: allow-warn (now, D-f: tiers `v`, `t`, `u` all eligible with warnings) | allow-t (S5: `v` and `t` eligible, `u` only by pin) | pin-only (S5: only `v` eligible, everything else only by pin; M10: because the all-models pass yields `t`, not `v`, for almost every model, `pin-only` would collapse the set, so `allow-t` is the realistic strict setting and `pin-only` is for an owner who has run L3 widely); the owner flips them, gated G6. `allow`: S5 explicit pins `provider/model`. Absent file = policy off (router behaves exactly as today). Corrupt file = loud CLI error, never "unset" (V13). Size cap 4 KB. First `set` with no file requires `--source` and `--mode` (no hidden defaults for the two toggles that change who serves work); revision 7, item 1: this holds for a REAL first `set` (exit 1 without them), while a DRY first `set` (`--dry yes`) without `--source` previews as `all-providers` with a NOTE saying so; `--ctx` defaults to `any`, `--enforce` to `shadow`, `--inject` to `off`. Later `set` calls merge only the flags given. Not in the schema: any price cap (`--max-price` is a later idea, D-b). REVISION 10 additions to the owner file: `banded`: `yes` | `no` (D-u; default `yes`, an owner file without the key reads as `yes`, I16; CLI `set --banded yes|no`) and `handoffNotice`: boolean (D-y, ar-17; default TRUE; CLI `set --handoff-notice yes|no`, explicit value like every flag, V14). The owner file keeps `enforcement` and `classLog` as before, but in the COMPILED file these two and `handoffNotice` live in an owner block that is OUTSIDE the content hash (5.2 revision 10 paragraph). No `canary` key and no `--canary` flag exist (the owner did not adopt the canary feature, item 6(a)).

### 5.2 Compiled file `~/.uw/state/subagent/policy.json` (the router reads only this)

```json
{
  "schema": 1,
  "contentHash": "9f3a1c20b7d4",
  "compiledAt": "2026-10-03T12:00:00.000Z",
  "owner": { "source": "all-providers", "mode": "dynamic", "freeScope": "providers", "ctx": "any",
             "enforcement": "shadow", "inject": "off", "unverified": "allow-warn", "classLog": "on" },
  "builtFrom": { "snapshotBuiltAt": "2026-09-30T08:18:20.565Z", "snapshotSchema": 9,
                 "benchGeneratedAt": "2026-09-30T08:18:17.893Z", "observedWrittenAt": null,
                 "providersLive": true, "providersHash": "ab12cd34ef56", "tiersHash": "77aa01c3d9e2", "compiler": 1 },
  "empty": false,
  "emptyReasons": [],
  "emptyProviders": ["groq", "openai"],
  "noRowProviders": ["anthropic"],
  "thinProviders": ["cohere"],
  "tiers": { "anthropic": "subscription", "groq": "free", "deepseek": "paid" },
  "substitutable": { "groq": 0, "openai": 0, "cohere": 2, "anthropic": 13 },
  "exempt": ["anthropic/claude-haiku-4-5"],
  "ctxHints": { "anthropic/claude-sonnet-5": 1000000 },
  "counts": { "universe": 6432, "allowed": 1790, "verified": 0, "unverified": 1790, "premium": 8,
              "payloadRisk": 31, "tfAsOf": null, "small": 0, "alias": 2, "idRejected": 0, "depositStrictSkipped": 2,
              "freeScopes": { "models": {"n": 112, "providers": 40}, "providers": {"n": 1686, "providers": 45}, "providers+deposit": {"n": 1686, "providers": 45} },
              "funnel": [ {"stage":"chat-capable","n":6080}, {"stage":"in-providers","n":6080},
                          {"stage":"bench-ok","n":1868}, {"stage":"tools-not-false","n":1790} ] },
  "models": [ { "s": "anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5", "c": 1000000, "f": 0, "t": "u",
                "h": 0, "m": 1, "n": 1, "i": "$3/$15", "p": 0, "pb": 0, "al": 0,
                "g": 0, "fp": 0, "ft": 0 } ],
  "lists": { "all": [0, 17, 4], "byProvider": { "anthropic": [0, 3], "groq": [9] } },
  "main": { "ttlSec": 21600 },
  "sticky": { "ttlSec": 21600, "maxEntries": 256 },
  "inject": { "all": "...text...", "byProvider": { "anthropic": "..." }, "empty": "...warning text...", "promptNote": "..." },
  "gate": { "enforce": "unmet", "reasons": ["..."] }
}
```
(The numbers in this sample are illustrative. There is no frozen compiled-file fixture file (revision 9 item 8): the compiled-file schema contract is this section 5.2 plus the shape test in `test/subagent-sandbox.test.mjs`, which compares the key set of a real `compile()` output with the sandbox's synthetic policy.) Revision 7 (items 3 to 5) fields as built: per model row `g` (tie-group id of rows equal on every rank key except the id key, used by the top-K spread), `fp`, `ft`; `owner.classLog`; `inject.promptNote`; `counts.small`, `counts.alias`, `counts.idRejected`, `counts.depositStrictSkipped`; `gate`, an object written ONLY by a rebuild whose enforce precondition is unmet (the sample shows it for illustration only; it is ABSENT from a normal file; with it present `compiled.owner.enforcement` reads `shadow`, the router raises `CLASSIFIER_UNMEASURED`, and `gate` is excluded from `contentHash`; its inner field names are illustrative); `tiers` may include a `management` entry for a management-only provider (never admitted). `noRowProviders` (revision 8 item 1, owner ruling Q5; added in S4b, so a file compiled before S4b lacks it and readers treat an absent field as `[]`): top-level array of provider names, sorted ascending by byte order, of providers that have a chat-capable route in live `Providers` and ZERO rows in the candidate set under the active toggles; disjoint from `emptyProviders[]`; routing content, IN `HASH_KEYS`; built with null-prototype structures and `own()`; read by the picker, pane, `show`, `explain` and doctor, never by the router. G17 (revision 8 item 3): a provider whose tier is `excluded` (`management`) contributes nothing to any count, list, ranking, injected text or hash; its `tiers` entry is informational. `contentHash` is computed over the ALLOW-LIST `HASH_KEYS` only (`owner` including `inject`, `models`, `lists`, `exempt`, `ctxHints`, `emptyProviders`, `noRowProviders` (from S4b), `thinProviders`, `tiers`, `substitutable`); counts, stamps and inject text are excluded so an unrelated snapshot change cannot rotate main's prompt-cache prefix, and `sameCompiled` still rewrites the file when only counts change.

Field notes. `models[]` is the deduplicated allowed universe for the ACTIVE toggles; `s` is the exact `Providers` spelling `${p.name}/${model}` (never the snapshot's `[1m]` id, and always passed through the id sanitiser: provider-supplied ids are UNTRUSTED, so the compiler drops any whole selector that fails `^[A-Za-z0-9_./:@+~-]{1,64}$` (so also any longer than 64 characters; REVISION 11, sa-A10: the cap is raised to 160 because 13 of 6,080 routes were rejected at 64) before it can reach compiled lists or injected text, cr-m3; the drop is counted in the funnel as `id-rejected` and the rejected ids are listed by `set`/`explain`, so a legitimately long id is visible rather than silently missing); `c` effective ctx; `f` 1 if free; `t` tool tier `v` verified at the recorded size (L3 passed) or known-good by provenance (the Anthropic relay rows, not probed), `t` tools work at small payload (L1+L2 passed, L3 not run: D-i), `u` unverified (S1-S4 everything is `u`, D-f; also any model not yet probed), `x` failed (never present in lists); `h` TTFT bucket 0/1/2; `m` 1 if main-eligible 1M basis; `n` 1 if the 1M claim is listing-only and delivery needs the context-1m beta header (Anthropic relay, experiment E6b); `i` display price (`$in/$out` per M tokens, shown on EVERY row, D-b); `p` 1 if PREMIUM (Opus- or Fable-priced: the catalogue family is `opus` or `fable`, or output price is at least $20 per M tokens; one named constant `PREMIUM_RULE` in the library, listed by `explain`, drives the D-b warning; the exact threshold is open question Q3); `pb` payload cap in bytes from the snapshot's per-provider entry (V27) or `0` when unknown (cr-M1). `lists.*` are index arrays in rank order; for `source: all-providers` only `lists.all` is populated, for `same-provider` only `lists.byProvider` (the router picks by main's provider). `lists.byProvider` OMITS providers whose set is empty, so on its own it cannot distinguish "empty" from "not computed" (cr-M8): `emptyProviders[]` (REDEFINED in revision 4, M3) lists every provider that has at least one chat-capable route in live `Providers` and an EMPTY SUBSTITUTE POOL under the active toggles (not merely an empty set: see `substitutable` below), so the picker and `show` can say "empty" with certainty [AS BUILT, revision 7 item 3: S1 flags the providers that have rows in the candidate set but no substitutable row, which is the 9.2b and acceptance 6 behaviour; a provider with ZERO rows in the candidate set, including main's own provider, is NOT in `emptyProviders[]` and is caught at `set` time by refusal rule (b); owner ruling Q5 (revision 8 item 1): `emptyProviders[]` keeps this built meaning and the zero-row case gets its own list `noRowProviders` in S4b]; `thinProviders[]` lists providers whose substitute pool has 1 or 2 rows. `exempt[]` are the selectors of the haiku and small-fast env values (read-only from settings env at compile time) that `inherit` leaves alone (I8); `ctxHints` maps the six tier-alias values, the default model and every allowed row to a context size so the router can apply the context floor to `asked` without a catalogue (section 6.2). `inject.*` are PRE-RENDERED strings (the router does no formatting, so it stays small and the text is byte-stable). Hard caps enforced by the compiler: file at most 1 MiB, each inject string at most 2,048 bytes, at most 20 shown entries per string. `contentHash` covers only the routing-content ALLOW-LIST `HASH_KEYS` (revision 7 item 5, plus `noRowProviders` from S4b: `owner` including `inject`, `models`, `lists`, `exempt`, `ctxHints`, `emptyProviders`, `noRowProviders`, `thinProviders`, `tiers`, `substitutable`; no timestamps, counts, stamps or inject text); the compiler skips the write when the hash is unchanged AND `sameCompiled` finds nothing else changed (a counts-only change still rewrites the file but leaves the hash, so no spurious prompt-cache rotation, section 6.4). `counts.payloadRisk` = allowed rows whose known `pb` is below 1,000,000 bytes (the named constant `PAYLOAD_RISK_BYTES`, set above the 919 KB largest subagent body the critic observed, V27; REVISION 11, sa-A1: 71 of 337 (21%) classified sub requests in the live shadow exceed 1 MB, so this constant sits BELOW observed bodies and `pb` is 0 on all 110 rows); unknown caps are counted separately as `payloadUnknown`. Free-scope fields (D-j, D-k): `owner.freeScope`; `counts.freeScopes` holds, for ALL THREE scopes regardless of the chosen one, the probe-ok model count and the provider count after the tools stage (the figures in the sample are illustrative; the `set` and `show` funnels print them under the names `free models`, `free providers` and `free providers + deposit`, D-l); `builtFrom.tiersHash` hashes the provider-to-tier map the compile used, so the doctor can tell that the registry changed since compile; every row carries `fp` 1 when it entered through a free-tier provider and `ft` 1 when it entered through a free tag on a paid or deposit provider, so `explain` can say why a model is in the set. The compiled file ALSO carries `tiers`: the small map `{provider: tier}` the compile derived from the registry (no secrets; revision 5: the picker pane reads tiers from here because it may not import `keysync/`), and `builtFrom.tiersHash` hashes that map.
Substitutable and usable (M3, M-h). A row is SUBSTITUTABLE when its effective context is KNOWN and at least 128,000 (`c >= 128000`; the substitute floor of section 6.2, so a row with unknown context `c = 0` is never substitutable) and its selector is resolvable; `substitutable[provider]` counts such rows per provider (`lists.all` for `all-providers` is also reported under the key `*`). A row is USABLE for one request when it is substitutable AND fits that request (token and payload fit, section 6.2). Emptiness is judged on the SUBSTITUTE POOL, not on the set: `emptyProviders[]`, the `set` refusal rule and the `FRAGILE` warning all use `substitutable` (a set of 40 rows with unknown context is empty for substitution purposes); the honour path (a tag main chose from the set) still uses the whole set. On the 2026-10-03 replay 860 of 1,687 probe-ok free-provider models (51%) have unknown context (V29), so under `free providers` many providers are thin or empty for substitution even though their sets are large; `set` prints the number of providers with `substitutable < 3` (section 7). ALIAS rows (D-n): `al` 1 when the model id matches the shared `POOL_ALIAS_RE` (section 4, `menu/pool-rule.mjs`); alias rows are flagged `ALIAS` in every output, counted separately (`counts.alias`, funnel line), tested for the free tag like any other row, and an alias probe result never raises the tier above `u` (section 11); an alias row is substitutable only if its catalogue context is known (pool aliases usually carry none, so they are usually honour-only).
Router v2 additions to the compiled file (revision 10; they SUPERSEDE the `HASH_KEYS` text of revision 7 item 5 and of the paragraphs above where they differ). (1) The `owner` block is split: the HASHED part keeps `source`, `mode`, `freeScope`, `ctx`, `unverified`, `inject`, `banded`; `enforcement`, `classLog` and `handoffNotice` move to an UNHASHED owner part, so flipping enforcement, the classification log or the handoff notice no longer changes `contentHash` and no longer rotates the prompt-cache marker line (ar-10). (2) New top-level fields OUTSIDE the hash: `minRouter` (integer; the oldest `ROUTER_VERSION` allowed to serve this file: a router older than it serves `asked` and raises `POLICY_NEWER`, ar-8) and `rollout:{canaryPct:100,salt}` (INERT: read by the router, no CLI writes anything but the default, ar-10). (3) New fields INSIDE the hash (routing content): `lists.prov` (compiled per-provider lists the router can use without a scan, ar-4) and on every model row `b`, a small integer BAND id (REDEFINED by D-ag: rows equal on TOOL TIER, HEALTH (current probe-ok yes/no, age ignored), CONTEXT PREFERENCE CLASS (the `prefer-1m` split) and PRICE CLASS; the recency classes are not in the band), plus the existing `g` tie group. `lists.all` is `null` when it is the identity list (ar-15). (4) The router recomputes the hash of the file content on a cache miss and compares it with `contentHash`: a mismatch raises `POLICY_HASH` and the router serves `asked` (ar-8). (5) The cache key of the policy file is (mtimeMs, size, ino) (ar-8). A compiled file written before router v2 lacks these fields; nothing is live, so no such file serves traffic, and how a v2 router treats an old-shape file (fail open to `asked` with `POLICY_SCHEMA`, the safe default, is the plan's reading) is pinned by a test in S1r. The first rebuild after S1r rotates `contentHash` once because the allow-list changed. (6) STATE FILES the router adds or reads beside the compiled file (revision 10; neither is compiled content, neither is in the hash): `state/subagent/cooling.json` (D-ad, D-ao; the BUILT shape, replacing the proposed one): `{"v":1,"models":{"<provider/id>" or "prov:<provider>": {"u":until,"l":rung 0..3,"t":lastFail,"n":streak count,"t0":streakStart}}}`, with `u`, `t` and `t0` in epoch MILLISECONDS on the router's monotonic base (ar-16) and at most 64 keys counting models and provider keys (`prov:<provider>`) together, written asynchronously and coalesced, read through stat plus cache, fail-open; and `state/observed.json` (D-ae), the existing live observer overlay, which the router only READS (never writes) as a soft hint (6.1b F). The compiled file carries no cooling or overlay data and no new cooling constants: the ladder (2 min, 10 min, 60 min, 6 h), the provider-key window (5 min) and the overlay age caps (2 h `rate`, 24 h `pay`/`auth`) are router constants, reported by `show` and `why`.

### 5.3 Ranking rule (what is ranked, with which inputs, and what is deliberately not)

Rank is a reliability-and-speed ordering, NOT a capability ranking; nothing intelligence-related is invented. Ascending key, first wins:
1. tool tier: `v`, then `t`, then `u` (alias rows are capped at `u`, D-n);
2. probe health class: observed-live ok within 7 days (overlay record, `get.isLive`), then fresh probe ok within 14 days (`BENCH_FRESH_MS`, `menu/bench-data.mjs:255`), then older ok (age never excludes: a stale-but-ok model stays a candidate and shows its age, matching the "stale metadata is a warning, not a prune" rule);
2b. free price class, ONLY under `mode free` with scope `providers` or `providers+deposit` (D-j; under `dynamic`, `inherit` and the `models` scope the key does not exist, so every row is equal on it). REVISED in revision 8 (owner ruling Q6, option B). Price classes, ascending, first wins: (class 0) listed price 0 on a row that entered through a FREE-TIER provider (`fp` 1); (class 0t) a row admitted by the strict free-tag rule on a paid or deposit provider (`ft` 1), whatever its listed price, ranked BELOW class 0 and ABOVE unknown price; (class U) UNKNOWN price on a free-tier row; (class P) positive price on a free-tier row, ascending by `price` (M4: a known positive price is the surest way to drain credit on a free key, so a positive-priced row ranks below a row whose price is unknown). Why 0t sits below 0: a class 0t row lives on a billed account, where a wrong free tag costs money, while a class 0 row costs free-tier credit only. Both classes are admitted exactly as before (class 0t only by the strict free-tag rule, probe-ok, never `pay`); only the ORDER changed. The key keeps its position after keys 1 and 2, so health still outranks the class and context (3) and TTFT (4) can never overturn it. As built and verified (`menu/subagent-funnel.mjs`, function `keyOf`): class 0 is price 0 on a free-tier row, class 0t is a strict-free-tag row on a paid or deposit provider, then U, then P; the test asserts the numeric classes [0, 1, 1, 2, 3] (that is 0, 0t, 0t, U, P) in rank order, the acceptance counts are unchanged and a free-tier-only ranking is byte-identical (revision 9 item 1). `price` = `pin + pout` per million tokens when both are listed, the listed one when only one is, unknown when neither (the sum, not the output price alone, because subagent turns are input-dominated: a tool-laden request is about 40,000 input tokens against a few hundred output tokens, so input price drains credit at least as fast as output price; the sum is a monotone proxy used ONLY for ordering and is displayed as the usual `$in/$out`). Positive-priced models are NOT excluded from `free providers` (the owner scope is every probe-ok model on a free-key provider; 392 of 1,687 carry a positive listed price on the 2026-10-03 replay, 30 carry 0, V29), but the `set` report counts them (`CREDIT: N positive-priced models in the chosen set`). Purpose: free providers have token or credit limits, and a fan-out of subagents on an expensive frontier model on a free key can drain them. Inside `dynamic`, `inherit` and the `models` scope this key does not exist;
3. larger context first (a catalogue fact; `null` last);
4. TTFT bucket from the bench `t` field: under 1,000 ms = 0, under 3,000 ms = 1, else or missing = 2 (bucketed because a raw number would reorder the list on every sweep and rotate the prompt cache);
5. non-alias before alias (D-n: an `ALIAS` row ranks below a non-alias row only when every key above is equal);
6. id ascending (byte-stable tie-break).
The complete key order is therefore: 1, 2, 2b (when it applies, else equal for all), 3, 4, 5, 6; where 2b applies every row has a class (0, 0t, U or P, revision 8 item 2), and where it does not apply (`dynamic`, `inherit`, the `models` scope) all rows are equal on that key; "tied" below always means equal on ALL keys except the final id key (6).
Price is displayed on every row and is never ranked outside key 2b (D-j, free-tier providers under `mode free` only) (ranking cheap-first would silently bias both the substitute and main's choice; cost is main's and the owner's call; D-b adds no cap). The rank carries NO quality implication: it orders by reliability and then context, so on a policy that allows the Anthropic relay the Opus and Fable 1M rows would LEAD the list purely because of context size (cr-m1); every label that shows the order says "ordered by reliability then context, not by quality or price". The injected shortlist is rank order with at most 3 entries per provider when `source: all-providers`, so one provider cannot fill it.
Substitute selection is deterministic AND spread (cr-M6): the substitute for a request is chosen among the top K = 3 candidates of the applicable list (candidates tied with the best on all keys except the id key first, in id order; if fewer than 3 are tied, the next in rank order fill up to 3) by `fnv1a32(utf8(agentKey)) mod K` (FNV-1a, 32-bit, offset basis 2166136261, prime 16777619, over the UTF-8 bytes of `agentKey`, unsigned; `agentKey` is the agent id, and for a billing-marker-only subagent that has NO agent id it is `sid:bl:asked`, so all such requests of one session and one asked model share one hash and therefore one model: spread is impossible without an agent id, which is why the counter `blNoAgentId` exists and the doctor WARNs if it is non-zero, since E1 expects every subagent to carry the id). The same agent always lands on the same model (this also supports stickiness, section 6.3) and a fan-out of N subagents spreads over up to 3 models instead of hammering one free tier (pre-mortem P8). `set` WARNS when the applicable list has fewer than 3 usable candidates (K < 3, "fragile: fan-out concentrates on N model(s)"). CCR's own `Router.fallback` stays OFF (a chained model could mask a failing route); the doctor asserts it (section 10). Tok/s (`o / ((d - t)/1000)`) is NOT used: the probe asks for 5 words (`menu/bench-data.mjs:32`), so `o` is tiny and the figure is noise.
BANDED SPREAD (revision 10, D-u, owner decision for rule R3; DEFAULT ON, owner switch `banded`, CLI `set --banded yes|no`). The spread above no longer reaches past the lead row's band: the substitute is chosen by `fnv1a32(utf8(agentKey)) mod K'` among the rows that share the LEAD row's band id `b` (REDEFINED by D-ag: equal TOOL TIER, HEALTH, CONTEXT PREFERENCE CLASS and PRICE CLASS; the earlier definition by rank keys 1, 2 and 2b is REPLACED, because key 2 mixes in 7 d and 14 d recency classes that would make a band calendar dependent), with K' = min(K, bandSize) where bandSize is the number of usable rows in that band; so a fan-out never spills from a verified, live, price-0 row onto an unverified or priced one just to reach K = 3. When K' < 3 the decision carries `FRAGILE_SET` (as the existing K < 3 warning does, and `set` still WARNS on thin lists). With `banded no` the earlier rule (top K = 3 by tie group, then next in rank) applies unchanged. D-AG DEFINITION (owner, after the router v2 reviews). The band is TOOL TIER + HEALTH + CONTEXT PREFERENCE CLASS + PRICE CLASS. HEALTH is the model's current or latest state being probe-ok, a yes/no taken from the latest bench status and the observer overlay's latest status; AGE IS IGNORED, so a band does not change with the calendar. The 7 d / 14 d recency classes (rank key 2) are NOT in the band; they may remain an ORDERING key inside a band, which still makes the choice of the top 3 calendar dependent when a band has more than 3 rows (stated, not hidden). The context preference class exists under the third ctx value `prefer-1m` (rows >= 1M form a HIGHER band; smaller rows only when those are unusable or limited); under `any` it has no effect and under `1m` the filter has already removed smaller rows. The price class stays in the band in free scopes. Consequence: before S5 (every row tool tier `u`), dynamic with ctx `any` behaves exactly like unbanded when rows share tool tier, health and price class; the tension flagged in the first text of this paragraph (the owner's "no effect before S5" against health and price banding) is RESOLVED by this definition. In user terms: a band is a group of equally good candidates; a fan-out spreads only inside the lead band. FALLBACK ACROSS TOOL TIERS (D-ah): when every usable row of the lead band is cooling or demoted, the router may fall to a LOWER tool tier only if that tier is TESTED (`v` to `t`), NEVER to untested `u`; crossing the health, price and ctx bands inside a tool tier stays allowed; if nothing tested remains the current model is kept (`handoffNone`); the full candidate order is I29. Before S5 this only matters for the relay. Cooling (ar-17) is applied INSIDE the band: a cooling row is used only when no non-cooling usable row exists in the band.
Responding-provider rule: a model excluded because its bench status is `pay`, `auth` or `rate` is not pruned from routing or from the picker; `set` prints "N responding models excluded for account state" so the owner sees they are state, not death.

REVISION 11 RANKING AND CONTEXT CHANGES (D-bg, D-bh, D-bi, D-ba, D-bb; sa-A1..sa-A12, sa-T3): (1) the asked-side ctx floor follows the ctx toggle (D-bg): `any` = 128k plus the per-request fit check, the hard floors `128k|200k|256k|512k|1m` exclude rows below them (an empty result raises the empty-set warning), the soft `prefer-256k|prefer-512k|prefer-1m` form a higher band and exclude nothing (D-bk), and the asked-model `ctxHints` never raise the floor; the compiler's `hintFor` must not let an asked-model 1M hint raise the floor under `any`. (2) Inside a band, rank by ctx CLASSES (>= 1M, >= 512k, >= 256k, >= 200k, >= 128k; D-bk adds >= 200k) and TTFT QUANTILE buckets, with TTFT AHEAD of raw ctx (D-bh); this REPLACES the order of keys 3 and 4 above (ctx first, then a fixed 1 s / 3 s TTFT bucket). (3) An inferred ctx from a same-name sibling is a floor-only prior flagged `c?` and never the asked floor; the non-agent denylist (`safety|guard|embed|rerank|ocr|lora`) applies (D-bi). (4) `big` and L4 pass/fail are rank keys INSIDE tool tier `v`; first-strike models rank below clean ones (sa-T3); a `likely-x` row (D-bc) ranks below the unknowns of its provider and is never excluded by inheritance alone. (5) `pb`: filled from `capBelow` and from gateway-log refusals (sa-A1); `maxBytes` never sets `pb`; unknown-`pb` stays last for requests over 200 KB only after the router-bytes-DEFERRED change. (6) The id sanitiser cap rises from 64 to 160 characters (sa-A10) and the free verdict must be consistent across a provider's own and a reseller's spelling of one model (sa-A11); `FAMILY_RE` no longer flags `opus-mt` (sa-A12).

### 5.4 Status file `~/.uw/state/subagent/status.json`

```json
{ "schema": 1, "updatedAt": "ISO", "since": "ISO", "pid": 1234, "routerVersion": 1,
  "policy": { "state": "ok", "compiledAt": "ISO", "contentHash": "9f3a1c20b7d4",
              "enforcement": "shadow", "inject": "off", "rollbackFlag": false,
              "unverifiedAllowed": 1790, "premiumAllowed": 8, "payloadRisk": 31 },
  "counters": { "req":0, "main":0, "sub":0, "aux":0, "keep":0, "honourTag":0, "substitute":0, "inherit":0,
                "emptySet":0, "unknownMain":0, "unresolvable":0, "rejectUnresolvable":0, "error":0, "slotError":0,
                "injected":0, "detectorDisagree":0, "ctxUndelivered":0, "ctxSkip":0, "payloadSkip":0,
                "stickyHit":0, "stickyNew":0, "stickyEvict":0, "policyBad":0, "slowReq":0,
                "auxOnRelay":0, "freeBreak":0, "blNoAgentId":0 },
  "aux": { "byModel": { "anthropic/claude-haiku-4-5": { "n": 0, "bytesSum": 0, "bytesMax": 0 } }, "maxModels": 16 },
  "matrix": { "a1.b1.t1.g0": 12 },
  "mainBySession": { "a1b2c3d4": { "model": "anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5", "t": "ISO" } },
  "warnings": [ { "code": "EMPTY_SET", "since": "ISO", "detail": "mainProvider=groq source=same-provider mode=free" } ],
  "last": { } }
```
`matrix` keys: `a` agent-id header present, `b` `builtInClaudeCodeSubagent` true, `t` body has at least one tool, `g` body has an Agent/Task tool; each 0/1. It is the whole experiment table for E1, E2, E3 and E14 in 16 counters (cell `a1.*.t0.*` is the aux class). `mainBySession` keeps at most 8 entries. Counters live in `globalThis` (so they reset when the daemon restarts; documented) and are flushed at most once per 5 s and on any change of the warnings set. `warnings[].code` is a closed list: `POLICY_ABSENT_SKIPPED` (not a warning), `POLICY_CORRUPT`, `POLICY_OVERSIZE`, `POLICY_SCHEMA`, `EMPTY_SET`, `UNKNOWN_MAIN`, `UNRESOLVABLE_CANDIDATE`, `UNRESOLVABLE_ASKED`, `SLOT_ERROR` (removed with the slot at G3), `NATIVE_MENU_PRESENT`, `CTX_UNDELIVERED`, `INHERIT_BELOW_CTX`, `INHERIT_PREMIUM_MAIN`, `UNVERIFIED_ALLOWED` (standing while any allowed row is unverified, D-f; the status field `policy.unverifiedAllowed` carries N), `PAYLOAD_RISK`, `FREE_PROMISE_BREAK` (mode `free` served a model outside the free set, section 6.2 note h; counter `freeBreak`), `CLASSIFIER_UNMEASURED` (enforcement is on but the accuracy matrix of section 12.5 has not been recorded or has expired), `TIER_MISMATCH` (snapshot key-id tier differs from the registry tier, M-a), `FREE_LIST_STALE` (the live overlay shows models in the compiled free lists now `pay` or `auth`, D-j), `TIER_UNREADABLE` (the registry or key choices could not be read at compile time, D-k), `DETECTOR_DRIFT`, `ROUTER_ERROR`.
Writing and reading on Windows (cr-m4): `status.json` is written by write-temp then rename; a rename over a target that any reader holds open fails with EPERM/EBUSY. Rule: the WRITER retries 3 times with 10, 30 and 90 ms backoff and on a final failure skips that flush (non-fatal; the next change retries) [REVISION 10, ar-5: on the REQUEST PATH there is no blocking sleep at all: the write is asynchronous with an in-flight guard (a second flush while one is in flight is coalesced), and the 10/30/90 ms retry runs only on the failure path, driven by a request-scoped `unref`'d timer, never by a sleep]; every READER (CLI `show`, doctor, picker banner) retries up to 5 times with 20-100 ms backoff on EPERM/EBUSY/ENOENT and then reports "status unreadable right now", never "no status". There is no timer outside a request (revision 10, ar-5; the router is re-required per request, section 4): the 5 s throttle is a timestamp comparison made on the request path, not a timer.
Router v2 status additions (revision 10). (1) PER-WORKER FILES (ar-6): each worker writes `status-<w>.json` (`w` identifies the worker, the same `w` as on every log line; X2 measures how many distinct workers exist) instead of one shared `status.json`, so two workers never overwrite each other; `show` and `status` MERGE the per-worker files (counters summed, warnings unioned, the latest `updatedAt`). `status.json` is therefore the MERGED view produced by the readers, not a router-written file (plan's reading; the build pins the exact file layout). (2) New content (ar-7, ar-10, ar-17): `byModel` and `byProvider` served-counts, a latency histogram by path (`warm`, `new-agent`, `shadow`, `handoff`), `policy.headline` (a one-sentence plain-words summary, for example "logs what it would do, changes nothing" in shadow), `routerVersion` is the `ROUTER_VERSION` constant (ar-8). (3) New counters: `retry` by source (`retryHdr`, `retryLen`), `handoff`, `handoffNone`, `handoffCap`, `noticeApplied`, `noticeFail`, `agentIdOdd`, `logDropped`, `stickyJournalTorn`. (4) New warning codes: `POLICY_NEWER`, `POLICY_HASH` (ar-8), `AUTO_ROLLBACK` (ar-10 tripwire; shows the code and time of the trip), `HANDOFF` (ar-17; carries the latest `from -> to` and time, clears after 1 h, and is part of `policy.headline`), `LOG_DROPPED`. (5) The mainBySession copy is bounded as before. (6) Revision 10 third delivery: counters `coolMark`, `coolProvider` (D-ad: a model, or a provider key, was marked cooling), `coolDemote` (a cooling row was skipped for a non-cooling one), `overlayDemote`, `overlayIgnored` (D-ae); `status` also reports the cooling entries (model or provider key, rung, time left) and whether the overlay hint was in use, so `last`, `show` and `status` can say why an agent was steered away. New warning codes: `COOLING` (at least one model or provider key is cooling; carries the count and the latest, clears when none is) and `OVERLAY_UNREADABLE` (the overlay file exists but could not be read or parsed; the hint is ignored). Both are in the one warning-code table of `menu/subagent-codes.mjs` (7.2 item 8) with their fix text. D-ao: the counters built in S1r include at least `retryHdr`, `retryLen`, `stickyJournalTorn`, `coolMark`, `coolProvider`, `coolDemote`, `overlayDemote` and the warnings `COOLING`, `OVERLAY_UNREADABLE`, `LOG_DROPPED`; the executor's final report fixes the list; the first synchronous status flush per worker is REMOVED (D-ai) and tests flush through a seam. The same retry rule applies to `main-<sid>.json`, `agents-<sid>.json` and the compiled `policy.json`. (Sequence-named files plus an atomic pointer were considered and rejected: more files and a pointer that has the same rename problem.)

### 5.5 Decision log line (`decisions.jsonl`)

```json
{"t":"2026-10-03T12:00:00.123Z","v":1,"sid":"a1b2c3d4","role":"sub","ag":1,"bl":1,"tools":39,"agentTool":0,
 "asked":"anthropic/claude-sonnet-5","tag":null,"main":"anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5","mp":"anthropic",
 "pol":"shadow","act":"substitute","would":"groq/openai/gpt-oss-120b","ret":"anthropic/claude-sonnet-5",
 "why":"not-in-set","ph":"9f3a1c20b7d4","stk":0,"price":"$0.15/$0.60","ms":0.31,"flags":[]}
```
`act` closed list: `keep`, `honour-tag`, `substitute`, `inherit`, `empty-set` (logged ONCE per session and reason, later ones only counted: D-c), `unknown-main`, `unresolvable`, `reject-unresolvable`, `sticky` (a repeat request served from the per-agent sticky map), `aux` (agent-id or billing marker with no tools: passed through, sampled at most one line per session per minute), `bypass` (policy absent or bad), `main-learn`, `main-inject`. `role` is `main`, `sub` or `aux`. `ph` is the policy `contentHash` the decision used, `stk` 1 when the decision came from the sticky map, `price` the chosen row's display price (D-b: price is visible after the fact too). `ret` is what the router actually returned (equal to `asked` in shadow, a test asserts it on every line; until G3 removes the slot, the exact-match slot rewrite can make it differ only when `slot.json` is not the identity, and the test then compares against `slot(asked)`). Redaction rules, tested: only the enumerated fields; selectors and tags must match `^[A-Za-z0-9_./:@+\[\]~-]{1,160}$` else logged as `"?"`; `sid` is the first 8 characters of the sanitised session id; never any request or response body, header value, message text, config object or key. Main-role requests are not logged per request, only `main-learn` (model changed) and `main-inject` events, at most one per session per minute. Volume cap: 1 MiB, then rename to `decisions.1.jsonl` (replacing the previous one): at most 2 MiB total. Size is `statSync`-checked every 50th append (counter in `globalThis`), not on every append.

Classification log `state/subagent/classify.jsonl` (D-m, section 12.5; owner key `classLog`, default `on` in shadow and `on` in enforce until the accuracy gate has passed, then the owner may set `off`). ONE line for EVERY request that reaches the policy path, main requests included, never a body, never a header VALUE other than the closed enumerations below:
```
{"t":"2026-10-03T12:00:00.123Z","sid":"a1b2c3d4","cls":"aux","ag":1,"bl":0,"nt":0,"ga":0,"sysb":"s2","m":"anthropic/claude-haiku-4-5","rc":"auxiliary","at":null,"bb":"b1","tc":4210}
```
`cls` is the router class (`main`, `sub`, `aux`, `exempt`); `ag` agent-id header present (0/1); `bl` `builtInClaudeCodeSubagent` flag (0/1); `nt` the NUMBER of tools (`tools.length`); `ga` Agent/Task tool present; `sysb` system-prompt size bucket (`s0` none, `s1` under 2 KB, `s2` 2-16 KB, `s3` over 16 KB; computed from the length of the `system` field without storing it); `m` the body model (`asked0`, same sanitiser as selectors); `rc` the value of `x-claude-code-request-class` when present and in the closed set `main|subagent|workflow|compaction|auxiliary`, else null (it is present only if `CLAUDE_CODE_GATEWAY_HINT_HEADERS=1`, section 12.5); `at` the `x-claude-code-agent-type` value when present, sanitised and clipped to 32 characters, else null; `bb` byte-size bucket of `content-length` (`b0` under 50 KB, `b1` 50-200 KB, `b2` 200 KB-1 MB, `b3` over 1 MB; null when the header is absent, E12); `tc` `req.tokenCount`. Cap: 2 MiB with one rotation (`classify.1.jsonl`), same size check cadence as the decision log; about 160 bytes a line, so roughly 13,000 requests per file, which is why the accuracy protocol labels sessions by time window (section 12.5) and why the aux and main volume is also summarised in `status.json` counters.

DECISION LOG V2 (revision 10, ar-7; SUPERSEDES the `decisions.jsonl` retention and sampling text above, I12). The per-decision log is `state/subagent/agents.jsonl`, `v:2`, ONE line per NEW agent (the first decision of an agent key), retained as the CURRENT file plus 3 rotated files, 4 MiB in all (D-al, the built values; `agents.1.jsonl`..`agents.3.jsonl`); sticky hits are SAMPLED (the first hit of an agent is logged, then 1 in 50); every line carries `w` (worker), `aid` (the sanitised agent id, clipped), `pid8` (the first 8 characters of the parent agent id when present, ar-1) and the old redacted fields (same sanitiser, no bodies, no header values). Rate limits are token buckets, SEPARATE for decisions and for classify (D-al; the rates are pinned by the build) and 20 per second for agents; a line that would exceed a bucket is dropped and counted in `logDropped` (and the warning `LOG_DROPPED` is raised), never blocking the request; on ENOSPC the writer backs off for a period and stops trying until it passes (no sleep: a timestamp comparison). New `act` values: `handoff` (ar-17: fields `from`, `to`, `reason` = `retry:<rsrc>:<count>`, `hop`, `tc`) and `handoff-none`/`handoff-cap` when a retry produced no handoff; in SHADOW the handoff path writes an `act:"handoff"` line marked `shadow:1` with the model it WOULD have chosen in `would` and changes nothing (I14). `classify.jsonl` keeps its section 5.5 shape and cap, and gains the field `w`. `last`, `report` and `show` read `agents.jsonl` (7.2); S2 acceptance commands that name `decisions.jsonl` (12.1) read `agents.jsonl` after S1r. D-al AMENDS this: new-agent lines are ALSO written to `decisions.jsonl` until the old test readers migrate, so `decisions.jsonl` is not dropped yet (I12 amended).

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 6. The router, precisely

### 6.1 Pseudo-code

```
module.exports = async function route(req, config, ctx) {         // async kept; ALL fs calls inside are synchronous
  const t0 = hrtime();
  let asked0 = "";                                                 // the ORIGINAL asked: every decision, learn and log uses it (D-e)
  try {
    asked0 = String(req?.body?.model ?? "");
    if (!asked0) return undefined;                                 // unchanged legacy behaviour
    const out = r => (r === asked0 ? legacySlot(asked0) : r);      // D-e: exact-match slot rewrite, ONLY when the router is passing asked through; never undefined; removed at G3
    const P = loadPolicy();                                        // {state, policy}; statSync + globalThis cache keyed (mtimeMs,size); size cap 1 MiB
    if (P.state !== "ok") { warnOnce(P.state); return out(asked0); }   // absent is silent; corrupt/oversize/schema are loud in status
    const pol = P.policy, h = req.headers ?? {};                   // keys are lower-case (F3)
    const sid = cleanSid(req.sessionId || h["x-claude-code-session-id"] || h["x-claude-session-id"]);   // [A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64} else "nosession"
    const tools = namesOf(req.body.tools);                         // lower-cased names, function.name too
    const hasAgentTool = tools.some(n => n === "agent" || n === "task");
    const agentId = cleanAgentId(h["x-claude-code-agent-id"]);     // "" when absent; REV 10 (ar-1): charset ^[A-Za-z0-9_@.:-]{1,128}$ (team ids `name@session` pass), a present-but-invalid header counts agentIdOdd
    const ag = agentId !== "", bl = req.builtInClaudeCodeSubagent === true;
    matrix[`a${+ag}.b${+bl}.t${+(tools.length>0)}.g${+hasAgentTool}`]++;
    if (ag !== bl) counters.detectorDisagree++;
    const cin = classInputs(req, h, tools, ag, bl, asked0);        // D-m: agent-id present, billing flag, tools.length, system-prompt size bucket, body model,
                                                                   // request-class and agent-type headers when present, byte-size bucket; NEVER bodies

    if (ag || bl) {
      if (tools.length === 0) {                                    // cr-M5: AUX class (a 21 KB haiku call WITH an agent-id exists in live logs)
        count("aux"); auxStats(asked0, cin.bb); classLog(cin, "aux"); return out(asked0);   // I8a: helper call, any mode; never rewritten, never inherits, never substituted; auxOnRelay and volume by model and bytes
      }
      return handleSubagent();                                     // below
    }
    // ---- main loop request (no agent-id, no billing marker)
    classLog(cin, "main");
    if (hasAgentTool && sid !== "nosession") {                     // cr-m2: never teach main without a session id; ONLY Agent-tool requests teach
      learnMain(sid, asked0, has1mBeta(h));                        // memory write; file write only if model changed or entry older than 60 s
      if (pol.owner.inject === "on" && pol.owner.mode !== "inherit" && !rollback())
        injectAgentTool(req.body, injectTextFor(pol, providerOf(asked0)));
    }
    return out(asked0);                                            // main is never rerouted by this feature

    function handleSubagent() {
      const forced = rollback() || pol.owner.enforcement !== "enforce";   // rollback(): shadow.flag exists (compile-free flip, cr-m8)
      const main = readMain(sid, pol.main.ttlSec);                 // memory first, then main-<sid>.json, stale beyond TTL -> null
      const tag = req.builtInSubagentModel ? cleanSel(req.builtInSubagentModel) : null;
      const key = ag ? `${sid}:${agentId}` : `${sid}:bl:${asked0}`;   // cr-B2 sticky key
      if (!forced) {                                               // shadow and rollback BYPASS stickiness and return asked at once
        const s = stickyGet(key, now());                           // TTL 6 h; resolvable(s.model) re-checked; stale or unresolvable -> recompute
        if (s) { count("stickyHit"); log({ act: "sticky", ret: s.model, stk: 1 }); return s.model; }
      }
      const d = decide(pol, main, tag ?? asked0, asked0,
                       { beta1m: has1mBeta(h), tokenCount: req.tokenCount, bytes: Number(h["content-length"]) || 0,
                         agentKey: agentId || `${sid}:bl:${asked0}`, hasTag: tag !== null }, config);   // no agent id: all such requests share one hash (5.3), counted blNoAgentId
      classLog(cin, d.exempt ? "exempt" : "sub");                  // D-m: inputs and class, never bodies (section 5.5)
      if (forced) { log({ act: d.act, would: d.model, ret: asked0, why: d.why, flags: d.flags }); return out(asked0); }   // shadow: ALWAYS asked
      const ret = out(d.model ?? asked0);                          // empty-set / unknown-main / unresolvable all serve asked (D-c)
      stickyPut(key, { model: ret, policyHash: pol.contentHash, t: now() });   // the FIRST ENFORCED decision is final until the TTL (cr-B2)
      if (d.act === "empty-set") logOncePerSession(sid, "EMPTY_SET", d);     // D-c; status warning on every occurrence
      else log({ act: d.act, would: d.model, ret, why: d.why, flags: d.flags });
      if (d.dropTag) req.builtInSubagentModel = undefined;         // cr-m6 / E13: stop CCR's policy chain honouring an out-of-set tag
      return ret;
    }
  } catch (e) {
    count("error"); warnOnce("ROUTER_ERROR");                      // never log e.message wholesale: name only
    return asked0 || undefined;                                    // asked0 is "" only when there was no model, where undefined is the legacy answer
  } finally { budgetCheck(t0); }                                   // records ms; > 5 ms increments slowReq, never throws
};
module.exports.__test = { reset, now: Date.now };                  // cr-M10 seam, unreachable by CCR (section 4)
```

Invariant (tested by a source scan and by fuzzing): every path returns a string equal to `asked0` (or its exact-match slot rewrite) or a vetted selector, never `undefined` unless `asked0` is empty; no `await` between any file open and its rename; no module-scope side effect (section 4).

Stickiness (cr-B2). A decision must not change under a running subagent: if main switches model (a `/model` command), the bench or `Providers` moves, or the owner edits the policy between two turns of the SAME subagent, the subagent keeps the model it first got, because a mid-life model switch can break thinking-signature continuity, change the tool dialect and change the context window under a half-built conversation (pre-mortem P5, E16). Rules: (1) record `globalThis.__uwSub.agent[sessionId + ':' + agentId] = { model, policyHash, t }`, persisted per session to `state/subagent/agents-<sid>.json` with the same write-temp-rename, retry and 60 s-or-on-change discipline as `main-<sid>.json`, read only on a memory miss; (2) TTL 6 hours from the first decision (`sticky.ttlSec`), after which the entry is ignored and the next request decides fresh; (3) every ENFORCED outcome is recorded, including "serve asked" outcomes (empty set, unknown main, unresolvable): an agent never flips between asked and a substitute mid-life; (4) a recorded model that is no longer resolvable in `config.Providers` is dropped and the request decides fresh (counted `stickyEvict`); (5) rollback is NOT sticky: in shadow, with `shadow.flag` present, or with the policy absent, the stickiness lookup is skipped and `asked` is returned immediately (an incident flip takes effect on the very next request); (6) the maps are bounded (cr-m5): at most 256 entries in the sticky map, 64 in `main`, 8 in `mainBySession` (the status copy), evicting oldest by `t`, and expired entries are pruned on every 50th subagent call (no timer); (7) a tool-less agent request (aux) never creates an entry. Required test: between two turns of one agentId change main's model AND replace the policy file; the returned model is identical; then create `shadow.flag` and the next return is `asked`. REVISION 10 (ar-2, ar-3, ar-17) CHANGES rules (1), (2), (3), (6) and the opening sentence of this paragraph: the in-memory map is the authority and its durable copy is the append-only journal `agents-<sid>.jsonl` (not a whole-file rewrite); the TTL slides (6 h since the last use, 24 h cap); null-model outcomes are not recorded; the caps are 128 per session and 512 global; and a decision DOES change under a running subagent in exactly one case, the retry-triggered handoff of 6.1b. Section 6.1b states the full v2 behaviour. D-at adds that a sticky entry stays valid while its model is in the WHOLE compiled allowed set, so a `/model` switch does not re-decide running agents.

### 6.1b Router v2 (revision 10: ar-1..ar-10, ar-14..ar-17; built in stage S1r BEFORE G1)

This section holds the mechanics that reconcile router v2 with 6.1-6.5; where it differs from them, it wins. The pseudo-code of 6.1 stays the shape of the function; the changes are listed here, and the exact code is the executor's job under the S1r acceptance (12.1).

A. STICKY STORE (ar-2, ar-3, ar-15, ar-16). Authority: the in-memory map `globalThis.__uwSub.agent`. Durable copy: `agents-<sid>.jsonl`, append-only, one JSON object per line: a `new` line (agent key, model, `policyHash`, time), a `touch` line (agent key, time; refreshes the sliding TTL; throttled so a busy agent does not append on every request) and a `handoff` line (agent key, new model, reason, hop; ar-17). A reader tolerates a torn last line (ignored, counted `stickyJournalTorn`) and replays lines in order, so the LAST model line of an agent wins and a worker restart reproduces the handed-off model. Other workers read the tail of the journal from the byte offset they last read (cross-worker tail reads, so a second worker serves the same model as the first). CROSS-WORKER FILE INDEX (D-aw, from the second review): when a worker's in-memory index of session files misses a file that another worker created, ONE `statSync` of the expected path finds it (I43); there is no directory scan. STICKY VALIDITY (D-at): an entry is valid while its model is in the WHOLE compiled allowed set (I41). Compaction (drop expired and superseded lines) runs in the HOURLY gc together with the existing file deletions of 6.3 item 5. Null-model decisions (empty-set, unknown-main, unresolvable: served `asked`) are NEVER journalled (ar-3a). Time: durations use a monotonic base, and any timestamp read from a file that lies in the future is clamped to now (ar-16). Session files past the cap are EVICTED (oldest first) instead of the session becoming memory-only (ar-15).

B. REQUEST PATH WITHOUT BLOCKING (ar-5, ar-6, ar-9). No blocking sleep and no synchronous write retry on the request path; `status-<w>.json`, `main-<sid>.json` and `cooling.json` writes are asynchronous with an in-flight guard and coalescing; the failure path may use ONE request-scoped, `unref`'d retry timer (X7); every file read stays a `statSync` plus a cache hit. Freshness across workers: a worker re-stats `main-<sid>.json` at most every 5 s (ar-9). The funnel and compile side (ar-14) are CLI-side: the funnel is memoised and `rebuild --if-stale yes` exits early on a cheap stamp comparison.

C. VERSION AND INTEGRITY (ar-8). `ROUTER_VERSION` is a constant; the compiled file carries `minRouter` outside the hash; a router with `ROUTER_VERSION < minRouter` serves `asked` and raises `POLICY_NEWER`; the cache key is (mtimeMs, size, ino); on a cache miss the router recomputes `contentHash` over `HASH_KEYS` and a mismatch raises `POLICY_HASH` and serves `asked`.

D. TRIPWIRE (ar-10). For the first 200 enforced requests of each worker the router checks two invariants and TRIPS on either: an `aux` or `exempt` request that is returned with `ret != asked`, or router errors >= 5 within 60 s. A trip writes `shadow.flag` with the content `auto:<code>:<iso>`, raises `AUTO_ROLLBACK`, and the router serves `asked` from the next request on (the same compile-free path as `rollback`); `show` prints "AUTO-ROLLBACK (reason)"; ONLY an explicit `set` or `resume` removes the flag (never the router, never a rebuild). This is in addition to, not instead of, the owner's manual watch of 12.1 S3.

E. SEAMLESS HANDOFF (ar-17, D-y). Goal (owner words): if a free subagent runs out of limits mid-work, its context and assigned task are handed off seamlessly to the next free agent so it picks up and resumes, and the user clearly sees when a handoff took place. KEY FACT: the Anthropic messages API is stateless, every subagent request carries the full history (task prompt, tool calls, results), so a handoff is the router assigning a DIFFERENT model for the agent's NEXT request; NO context is copied. The router sees requests, not responses, so the failure signal is the RETRY. Scope: ENFORCE mode only; modes `dynamic` and `free` only; never for `aux`, `exempt` or `main`; never under `inherit`; never in shadow (shadow only LOGS what it would do, I14).
 1. RETRY SIGNAL. (a) Header `x-stainless-retry-count` parsed as an integer and clamped to 0..20 (counter `retryHdr`; the header is in the confirmed live header set, `research/phase6/15-zero-token-switching.md:112-121`; X3 verifies it under a forced upstream failure; RESEARCH, 6.1c: the header was `0` on 243 of 243 live rows, so this signal PROBABLY NEVER FIRES and `len` is the signal expected to fire live). (b) Fallback `len` (agents WITH an agent id only; D-ak and D-an): the same agent key sends the same `body.messages.length` as its previous request within 120 s (only a NUMBER is stored per agent; counter `retryLen`; X5 measures the length per turn). Both are logged and the log line records which one fired (`rsrc` = `hdr` or `len`). A retry count of 0 or an absent header with a changed length is not a retry. D-ak: a BILLING-ONLY subagent (no agent id) uses the HEADER signal only, never `len` (two parallel built-in subagents sharing the key `sid:bl:asked` would otherwise look like a retry). D-an: the header alone is not enough for an agent WITH an agent id, which needs the `len` signal for a handoff (I30).
 2. COOLING. A per-model cooldown ladder (REVISION 10, D-ad, REPLACING the earlier 2/10/60 text): 2 min, 10 min, 60 min, 6 h; a repeat failure of the same model escalates one rung, the 6 h rung is reached ONLY after 3 consecutive failures of the same model within 24 h (D-am: it is the 4th consecutive failure; "consecutive" is APPROXIMATED by the cooldown plus 1 h of quiet, so a model failing every 30-60 min reaches 6 h in about 3 h), and the ladder resets after the cooldown plus 1 h of quiet. NO per-session escalation limit (D-as: dropped; the ladder lengths pace it, so one session can reach the 6 h rung about 75 minutes after the first failure) and the per-sid cap is 4 distinct models cooled per hour, with a denied failure still marking the model at rung 0 (2 min). PROVIDER-KEY COOLING (D-ad): when two DIFFERENT models of one provider key (I22: the provider's wired key) are cooling within 5 min of each other, the provider key is cooled too, so every model behind that key is demoted; this too is demotion only. State is kept in memory per worker plus a small shared file `state/subagent/cooling.json` (shape in 5.2; cap 64 entries counting models and provider keys together; asynchronous coalesced write like status; read through stat plus cache so OTHER workers and NEW agents see it at once; no blocking sleeps; fail-open: an unreadable or corrupt file means nothing is cooling).
