A cooling model is DEMOTED, never removed: `pickSubstitute` uses a cooling row only when no non-cooling usable row exists in the band, so routing is never blocked (pillar: the responding provider is never pruned).
 3. HANDOFF DECISION. On a retry signal for an agent that has a sticky model M: mark M cooling, then run `pickSubstitute` with cooling rows excluded, with the same banded-spread rules (D-u) and the same context and payload fit using the CURRENT `tokenCount` and `content-length` (a candidate whose context is too small for this request is skipped), then update the sticky entry (journal line with reason `handoff`) and return the new model. CANDIDATE ORDER (D-ag, D-ah, I29): (1) non-cooling rows of the lead BAND (tool tier + health + context preference class + price class); (2) non-cooling rows of other bands of the SAME tool tier; (3) non-cooling rows of the next lower TESTED tier (`v` to `t`), NEVER `u`; a handoff uses only (1)-(3) and, when none exists, keeps M and counts `handoffNone`; a NEW agent decision may finally fall back to cooling rows (demoted, never removed) so routing is never blocked. HANDOFF COST FOLLOWS THE TOGGLES (D-au): there is NO price cap on a handoff; the allowed set already encodes the toggles, so a handoff NEVER LEAVES THE SET (free mode: only free models, and when every free model is cooling the result is `handoffNone`); in dynamic modes a Haiku agent may end on Opus inside the set; the 3-per-hour cap holds. The TESTED-TIER rule (from a tested tier fall only to tested tiers, never to `u`) applies to EVERY handoff REGARDLESS of `--banded` (a review defect: with banding off a handoff had gone from a tested model to an untested one). GUARDS: at most 3 handoffs per agent per hour (counter `handoffCap`, the agent then stays on its current model); never back to a model the agent left in the last 30 min unless it is the only usable one (a trail of up to 5 models per agent is kept in memory); when no alternative exists keep M and count `handoffNone`. If the retry was not caused by the model (a network error or a 5xx) the handoff is still SAFE (the API is stateless) but not NEEDED: the cost is a model switch that was not required and a short cooldown on a healthy model; this is documented in the runbook (14 note n).
 4. VISIBILITY, three layers. (i) an `agents.jsonl` event `act:"handoff"` with `from`, `to`, `reason` (`retry:<rsrc>:<count>`), `hop` number and `tc`; counters `handoff`, `handoffNone`, `handoffCap`; the status warning `HANDOFF` (latest `from -> to` and time, cleared after 1 h) and `policy.headline` includes it. (ii) a pure helper `formatHandoff(line)`, exported for the future `last` and `report` CLI, prints `HANDOFF groq/x -> sambanova/y (retry 1, hop 1)`. (iii) NOTICE: the owner switch `handoffNotice` (owner-file boolean, default TRUE, compiled into the unhashed owner part, CLI `set --handoff-notice yes|no`, in the usage text). On the FIRST request after a handoff ONLY, the router appends ONE text block to the subagent request's SYSTEM prompt (`system` may be a string or an array of blocks: both are handled), never touches `messages` or `tools`, caps the block at 400 characters, and wraps the edit in try/catch (any error means NO mutation and the counter `noticeFail`). Text: `[UW handoff] Your previous model <from> hit a limit. You are now <to>. Continue the same task from the conversation above without restarting or re-planning. Begin your next reply with this exact line: ↪ handoff: continued on <to> after <from> limit`, where the arrow is U+21AA (I15: written as an escape in the router source). The selectors in the note pass `logSel`. Whether a router edit of the system prompt reaches upstream is NEW experiment X8 (E4 covers only the Agent tool description); the router counts `noticeApplied`, and the note is BEST EFFORT. This is the ONE place the router touches a system prompt; 6.4's "no system prompt is touched" is amended accordingly (the Agent-tool injection still touches only tools).
 5. NOT BUILT: any response-side detection, use of the CCR fallback chain, SQLite reads, a daemon.
 6. TESTS (S1r): retry via the header and via the length; the cooling ladder with the `now` seam; cooling visible to a NEW agent and, through `cooling.json`, to a second worker; band and fit respected (a too-small-context candidate is skipped); the hop cap; the trail rule; no handoff in shadow, `inherit`, `aux` or `main`; fail-open on any throw; the notice appended once and not on the second request, for both system shapes, with no mutation on error and `messages` and `tools` byte-identical; the sticky journal replays the handed-off model after a worker restart; BUDGET: the handoff path adds under 3 ms p99 at 1,800 rows over 60 providers (MEASURED on the final router bytes: 1.35-1.55 ms best p99 of 3 batches, 6.5).

F. OVERLAY HINT (D-ae; part of the S1r router bytes). The router reads `state/observed.json` (the live observer overlay that `loadBench` merges, V11) through `statSync` plus a cache keyed (mtimeMs, size, ino), exactly like `policy.json`, at the path `path.join(__dirname, "..", "state", "observed.json")` (no environment override, section 4: the sandbox copy of the router therefore reads the scratch file, never the real one). It is a READ-ONLY, AGE-CAPPED SOFT HINT: a model with a `rate` observation younger than 2 h, or a `pay` or `auth` observation younger than 24 h, is DEMOTED (the same demotion semantics as cooling: used only when no other usable row exists in the band, never removed from the set) for NEW agent decisions and for handoff picks. Plan's reading, flagged: it does not move an agent that already has a sticky model (only a retry handoff does) and it does not veto a tag that main chose from the allowed list (the honour path). The hint is IGNORED, fail-open, when the file is absent, unreadable, malformed or has no record inside its age cap. A read-size cap is applied (its value is pinned in S1r once the real file's size is measured; the plan has not measured it). Pillar check: `pay` and `auth` are account state, not death (responding provider is never pruned), so the effect is demotion, never exclusion. STALENESS CAVEAT (owner): the observer runs when the picker or keysync triggers it, so the hint can LAG by hours; it is a hint, not a guarantee, and the cooling ladder (E.2) is the router's own fast signal. Counters: `overlayDemote`, `overlayIgnored`. Tests (S1r): a fixture overlay demotes a `rate` model younger than 2 h and not one older; `pay`/`auth` at 24 h; absent, corrupt and stale files are ignored; the demoted model is still chosen when it is the only usable row; a sticky agent is not moved; the router never reads the real `state/` in tests.

G. HOOK CHANNEL FOR USER-VISIBLE HANDOFF (D-af; NO router bytes; stage S1e after G1). A second, opt-in visibility channel next to the best-effort notice of E.4: a Claude Code hook (a `PostToolUse` hook that fires inside subagents and/or `SubagentStart`; which of them carries the subagent's `agent_id` is decided by X9(g)) runs a small script that reads the TAIL of `agents.jsonl` (a bounded number of bytes, never the whole file), finds a `handoff` line whose `aid` equals the hook's stdin `agent_id` and that has not been printed yet, and prints a JSON `systemMessage` for the USER (the text of `formatHandoff`, for example `HANDOFF groq/x -> sambanova/y (retry 1, hop 1)`); it exits 0 on EVERY path, is silent on any error and never blocks a tool call (I25). It is installed into `settings.json` THROUGH KEYSYNC (settings.json is keysync-owned) ONLY with the owner's approval at install time (a printed diff and a typed yes; nothing is written silently; a `remove` and a read-only `status` exist). It depends on the G1 check X9(g): the hook's `agent_id` must equal the `x-claude-code-agent-id` header that the router logs as `aid`, `systemMessage` must reach the TUI or the `-p` stream, and hooks installed before launch must fire. If X9(g) fails, the hook is not offered and the notice plus `last` remain the channels.

H. HANDOFF ABUSE BOUNDS, SESSION IDS, JOURNAL LIMITS AND ROUTER MEMORY (D-an; from the security review; built in S1r). (1) PER-SID CAP (D-as): at most 4 DISTINCT models cooled per sid per hour (raised from 2), so one session cannot cool the catalogue; a failure over the cap is DENIED a cooldown beyond rung 0 but still marks the model at rung 0 (2 min); the former limit of 1 rung escalation per sid per hour is DROPPED (it left a failing model undemoted for about 47 minutes); there is NO multi-sid evidence requirement (a single-user machine has one sid). (2) The header alone is not enough for an agent with an agent id (I30; D-ak for billing-only agents). (3) The sid is LOWER-CASED for keys and file names (NTFS is case-insensitive, so `AbC` and `abc` are one session and one file). (4) Session files (`main-<sid>.json`, `agents-<sid>.jsonl`) exist only for sids that sent a MAIN request with an Agent tool (I35); other sids stay in bounded memory. (5) A STICKY HIT REQUIRES MEMBERSHIP IN THE WHOLE COMPILED ALLOWED SET (D-at): the recorded model must still be in `models[]` (the union over providers for the active toggles, I41, not only main's provider list) and resolvable (6.1 rule 4), else the request decides fresh; so a `/model` switch to another provider under `same-provider` does NOT re-decide RUNNING agents (no lost prompt cache), only entries whose model left the allowed set or is unresolvable are re-decided, and NEW agents follow the new main (under `inherit` see I40). (6) JOURNAL: a replay reads at most 256 KiB per request (a bounded tail), and the FIRST append per session starts with a newline so that a torn previous line cannot swallow the new one. (7) ROUTER MEMORY: CCR re-requires the router file on every request and each re-require leaks a Module record: measured 12.5 KB per request for v2 (515 MB after 30,000 requests) against 1-2 KB for v1 and s0b. The router body is therefore evaluated ONCE per process behind a stat-keyed `globalThis` wrapper (key: mtimeMs + size + ino of the router file; a changed key evaluates the new body and DROPS older keys, so a hot redeploy still loads new code). Module scope then holds exactly this wrapper (EXACTLY one `statSync` of the router's own file, one `globalThis` lookup and one GUARDED splice of the module from `module.parent.children` in try/catch; D-aw, contract pin 17); the section 4 module-scope rule is AMENDED to allow that and nothing else, and a source scan pins it. D-aw: the guarded `children` splice is APPROVED (measured residual growth 1.05 KB per request falls to 0.075 KB per require (0.009 KB per request over 30,000 requests, 0.26 MB total, final bytes)); the loader key includes the compiled source's identity (length plus an fnv hash of its head and tail), so a file swapped between Node's read and the `stat` cannot stay pinned; the state object carries a shape signature compared with `STATE_VERSION`. The proof is QB-11 (growth per request under 4 KB, measured by an `--expose-gc` loop).

I. PRODUCTION BYTES AND BUILT SHAPES (D-ao, D-ai). `seam.tamper` and the `drain()` timer are REMOVED from the production file (tests use a PATCHED COPY of the router, and a source scan asserts their absence; I31). The first synchronous status flush per worker is REMOVED (tests flush through a seam). The policy `contentHash` re-verification on a cache miss uses a pure-JS sha256 verified against `node:crypto` in tests: a cache-miss load costs about 9 ms for a 300 KB policy and 18-24 ms per policy change per worker was MEASURED (6.5). The BUILT `cooling.json` shape is recorded in 5.2 item (6) (`{"v":1,"models":{...}}`, times in epoch milliseconds on the router's monotonic base, at most 64 keys); the counters and warnings built are (5.4 item (6)): counters `retryHdr`, `retryLen`, `stickyJournalTorn`, `coolMark`, `coolProvider`, `coolDemote`, `overlayDemote`; warnings `COOLING`, `OVERLAY_UNREADABLE`, `LOG_DROPPED`.

### 6.1c Research facts about fallback, retries and free-tier limits (revision 10; recorded for the handoff and the cooldown design; each fact carries its confidence)

Confidence labels: [PLAN] agrees with a fact already verified in this plan; [RESEARCH] reported by the research notes of the owner's coordinator, NOT re-verified in this tree; [LIVE] measured on live rows by that research; [UNVERIFIED] stated as not known. X9 and the extended X3, X5 and X8 (12.2) test the ones the design leans on.
CCR `Router.fallback`:
- [RESEARCH] It is `{mode: off | retry | model-chain, models, retryCount}` with default `off`, and it is GLOBAL (one setting for every request, main-shaped ones included). A custom-router result is the FIRST policy and carries the global fallback.
- [RESEARCH] `model-chain` falls back on EVERY status >= 400; `retry` on 408, 409, 429 and >= 500.
- [RESEARCH] A fallback attempt does NOT re-run the custom router, so no banding, no fit check and no cooldown applies to it.
- [RESEARCH] CCR sleeps between attempts, even in `model-chain` mode: `Retry-After` clamped to 1 ms..60 s, or min(30 s, 1 s * 2^n) without it.
- [PLAN, V5] The fallback setting is hot-swapped without a gateway restart (`Router.*` is outside the restart predicate). [RESEARCH] agrees.
- CONSEQUENCE: the fallback chain stays OFF, for stronger reasons than P8: it is global, it bypasses the router's band, fit and cooldown logic, it adds sleeps, and it would act on main-shaped requests too.
Claude Code retry:
- [RESEARCH] Claude Code has its own retry loop plus the SDK retry: default 10 retries (cap 15, 300 with `RETRY_WATCHDOG`); it retries 408, 409, 429 and >= 500, but 529 only 3 times; backoff base 500 ms, cap 32 s, jitter 0.25; it honours `Retry-After` as max(retry-after, computed delay).
- [RESEARCH] IF THE DELAY EXCEEDS 60 s, Claude Code FAILS THE SUBAGENT IMMEDIATELY (`api_request_retry_after_too_long`), so NO retry reaches the router. Daily-limit 429s from Groq, SambaNova or OpenRouter may carry such values.
- [RESEARCH] A failed FOREGROUND subagent returns partial output marked incomplete, or "Agent terminated early due to an API error", to main, which may resume it or respawn it; a RESPAWN is a NEW agent id (so it gets a fresh decision, and the cooling and overlay hint are what steer it away).
- [RESEARCH] Claude Code's own `fallbackModel` NEVER triggers on 429 and lasts one turn.
- [LIVE] `x-stainless-retry-count` was `0` on 243 of 243 live rows: signal (a) of 6.1b E.1 PROBABLY NEVER FIRES, and `len` is the live signal.
- [RESEARCH] Request headers reach the router unchanged (the same object CCR logs).
HONEST BOUND for the quality bar (12.6 QB-5): the handoff is SEAMLESS for per-minute and short limits (the retry reaches the router within about 1 s); for DAILY-CAP exhaustion it degrades to ONE failure plus AVOIDANCE (cooling and the overlay hint), because no retry reaches the router when the delay exceeds 60 s.
Free-tier limit shapes (the sources are the official provider pages the research lane read, URLs as given by the research report; EVERY one is DOC-SUMMARY: it passed through a summarising fetch tool and was not read verbatim by this plan):
- Groq: RPM, RPD, TPM and TPD limits (an example of 10 RPM and 100 RPD), with `retry-after` and `x-ratelimit-*` headers. [RESEARCH, DOC-SUMMARY] https://console.groq.com/docs/rate-limits
- OpenRouter `:free` models: 20 RPM and 50 or 1,000 RPD (by account state); a mid-stream limit arrives as an SSE error on a 200 response. [RESEARCH, DOC-SUMMARY] https://openrouter.ai/docs/api-reference/limits
- Cerebras: RPM, RPH, RPD plus TPM, TPH, TPD as a token bucket (an example of 5 RPM and 1M TPH/TPD). [RESEARCH, DOC-SUMMARY] https://inference-docs.cerebras.ai/support/rate-limits
- SambaNova: 20 RPM, 20 RPD and 200k TPD. [RESEARCH, DOC-SUMMARY] https://docs.sambanova.ai/docs/en/models/rate-limits
- Gemini: daily quotas that reset at midnight Pacific time. [RESEARCH, DOC-SUMMARY] https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/rate-limits
- NVIDIA build: limits NOT stated on the page the research read (https://docs.api.nvidia.com/nim/docs/api-quickstart). [UNVERIFIED]
Design implications recorded here so they are not rediscovered: an OpenRouter mid-stream limit is an error INSIDE a 200, so the retry signal (a status-based retry) does not fire for it; per-day resets differ by provider (a model that failed on a daily cap stays unusable for hours, which is why the cooling ladder has a 6 h rung and the overlay hint has a 24 h age for `pay` and `auth`).
SOURCES the research lane used for the Claude Code and CCR facts above (recorded so each fact can be re-checked; the plan did not map each fact to one source, and the mapping is left to the research notes):
- Claude Code documentation, all DOC-SUMMARY (summarising fetch tool): https://code.claude.com/docs/en/env-vars , https://code.claude.com/docs/en/errors , https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents , https://code.claude.com/docs/en/model-config , https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks
- Claude Code issue: https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/68502
- Prompt caching (relevant to the handoff notice and the cache effects of 6.4): https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching (DOC-SUMMARY)
- BINARY EVIDENCE (not documentation, read from the installed programs by the research lane): `claude.exe` version 2.1.288, cited by offsets in that binary, and the CCR 3.0.22 bundle `cli.js`, cited as FUNCTION NAMES, re-found with `indexOf` (offsets move between builds, as section 3 already says for CCR).
- Confidence rule: [RESEARCH] means the fact came from these sources through the research report and was not re-verified in this tree; [LIVE] means measured on live request rows; X9 and the extended X3, X5 and X8 (12.2) are the experiments that re-verify the ones the design leans on.


### 6.2 `decide` for each toggle combination

```
decide(pol, main, want, asked, X, config):
  W = want
  const ok = sel => resolvable(sel, config);                       // THE one rule (cr-m7): provider in config.Providers with enabled !== false AND models.includes(rest). The compiler uses the same rule.
  const floor = Math.max(ctxOfAsked(pol, asked) ?? 0, 128000);     // cr-M1: never go below the asked model's context or 128k, EVEN under ctx any
  switch pol.owner.mode:
    case "inherit":                                                // toggle 1 IGNORED (I1: inheriting is by definition same-provider)
      if (isExempt(pol, asked))     return { act:"keep", model: asked, exempt: true };   // I8b: ONLY under inherit; haiku / small-fast env values are left alone (dynamic and free never exempt)
      if (!main)                    return { act:"unknown-main", model:null, why:"main model not learned", flags:["UNKNOWN_MAIN"] };   // serves asked, loud
      if (!ok(main.model))          return { act:"unresolvable", model:null, why:"main model not in Providers", flags:["UNRESOLVABLE_CANDIDATE"] };
      flags = [];
      if (pol.owner.ctx==="1m" && !main.beta1m && !ctxKnown1m(pol, main.model)) flags.push("INHERIT_BELOW_CTX");   // D-d: INHERIT ANYWAY, warn (status + decision log + `show`)
      if (isPremium(pol, main.model)) flags.push("INHERIT_PREMIUM_MAIN");           // D-b: Opus/Fable-priced main; warn, no cap
      return { act: main.model===asked ? "keep" : "inherit", model: main.model, flags };
    case "dynamic": case "free":                                   // "free" differs only in how the COMPILER built the lists
      S = pol.owner.source === "all-providers" ? pol.lists.all
                                               : (main ? pol.lists.byProvider[providerOf(main.model)] : undefined);
      if (pol.owner.source==="same-provider" && !main) return { act:"unknown-main", model:null, why:"main model not learned", flags:["UNKNOWN_MAIN"] };
      if (!S || S.length === 0)     return { act:"empty-set", model:null, why:`mainProvider=${providerOf(main?.model)}`, flags:["EMPTY_SET", ...(mode==="free" ? ["FREE_PROMISE_BREAK"] : [])] };
                                                                   // D-c: caller serves ASKED (never main's model, never blocks); logged once per session
      const askedOk = ok(asked);
      if (inSet(S, W) && ok(W) && fitsTokens(W, X, "honour") && fitsBytes(W, X))   // honour: only token and payload fit are checked, main chose deliberately; unknown c passes with CTX_UNKNOWN (note h)
         return { act: W===asked ? "keep" : "honour-tag", model: W, flags: ctxFlag(S, W, X.beta1m) };
      // not allowed, unresolvable, or does not fit: deterministic spread substitute
      pool = S filtered to rows with ok(row) AND row.c >= floor (known c only: c = 0 fails) AND fitsTokens(row, X, "substitute") AND fitsBytes(row, X);   // cr-M1, M3: the USABLE pool
      if (main && inSet(pool, main.model)) cand = main.model;      // least surprising: main's own model when allowed and fitting
      else { pool2 = pool restricted to main's provider if that leaves any, else pool;
             K = top 3 of pool2 (ties with the best on rank keys 1-4 first, then next in rank);   // cr-M6, section 5.3
             cand = K[ hash(X.agentKey) mod |K| ]; }
      if (cand) return { act: askedOk ? "substitute" : "reject-unresolvable", model: cand, requested: W,
                         why: inSet(S, W) ? "unresolvable-or-no-fit" : "not-in-set", dropTag: X.hasTag && !inSet(S, W),
                         flags: [...ctxFlag(S, cand, X.beta1m), ...(|K| < 3 ? ["FRAGILE_SET"] : [])] };
      return { act: askedOk ? "unresolvable" : "reject-unresolvable", model:null, why:"no resolvable candidate that fits", dropTag: X.hasTag,
               flags:["UNRESOLVABLE_CANDIDATE", ...(askedOk ? [] : ["UNRESOLVABLE_ASKED"])] };
```
Notes. (a) The substitute preference (main's own model if allowed and fitting, then the best-ranked of main's provider, then global rank, spread over the top 3 by agent hash) is the least-surprising deterministic choice and uses only section 5.3 inputs. (b) A tag value or `asked` outside the allowed set is NEVER honoured: this also bounds prompt injection (a hostile file telling main to spawn a subagent on a chosen model can only reach models the owner already allowed). (c) `ctxFlag` returns `CTX_UNDELIVERED` when the chosen row has `n: 1` and the request carries no `anthropic-beta` containing `context-1m` (the Anthropic relay serves 1M only with that header; Claude Code adds it from a `[1m]` model name that a bare alias value does not have). Whether the router may ADD the header is experiment E6b; until proven it does not, and the flag makes the gap loud. (d) Empty-set, unknown-main and unresolvable in `enforce` return `asked` (today's behaviour), never the anchor and never main's model (D-c). (e) `inherit` + `ctx: 1m` when main is below 1M inherits anyway and warns (D-d; the critic's opposite recommendation is recorded under Decisions). (f) Context-fit and payload-fit (cr-M1): `fitsTokens` compares `X.tokenCount * 1.1` with the row's `c` (F8: CCR provides `tokenCount` before the router runs; a row whose `c` is unknown (0) fails the substitute floor, and an absent `tokenCount` passes); `fitsBytes` compares the `content-length` header (E12: confirm CCR forwards it to the router; absent means pass and is counted `payloadUnknown`) with the row's `pb` when known. Counters `ctxSkip` and `payloadSkip` record what was filtered. The compiler-side payload field and its `set` warning are a PRECONDITION for enforcement (G3, section 12). (g) Rejecting an unresolvable `asked` explicitly (cr-m6): an unresolvable string returned from the router makes CCR log `custom-model-not-configured` and continue down its policy chain (custom router, then the tag, profile rules, `subagent-env`, `client-model`, `builtin-agent`), where an OUT-OF-SET TAG could be honoured; so in enforce the router never returns an unresolvable `asked` when a resolvable in-set substitute exists (act `reject-unresolvable`), and `dropTag` makes it clear `req.builtInSubagentModel` (the enriched request object is the same one CCR's chain reads, F2; E13 confirms in the sandbox). When no substitute exists the router must return `asked` anyway, raises `UNRESOLVABLE_ASKED` and the chain applies. (h) Unknown values and the promise break (M3). Semantics, one rule each: unknown context (`c = 0`) FAILS for a substitute (the 128k floor needs a known context) but PASSES for the honour path with the flag `CTX_UNKNOWN` (a tag main chose deliberately from the allowed list: failing it would silently replace a model main chose, and the owner was already told at `set` how many rows have unknown context; the check against `1.1 * tokenCount` is only made when `c` is known); unknown payload cap (`pb = 0`) PASSES in both paths and is counted (`payloadUnknown`), because the cap is known for very few providers and failing on unknown would empty almost every set. A FREE-MODE FALL-THROUGH IS A PROMISE BREAK: when `mode` is `free` and the router serves `asked` although `asked` is not in the free set (empty set, unknown main, no usable substitute, unresolvable), a paid model may serve a request the owner meant to keep free; the caller therefore adds the flag `FREE_PROMISE_BREAK`, increments `freeBreak`, writes the decision-log entry (once per session and reason, like `empty-set`), raises the status warning `FREE_PROMISE_BREAK` and the doctor WARN, and the `set` free report prints how many providers have `substitutable < 3` and how many are empty (section 7). It is never silent and it never blocks (D-c). (i) Helper calls (I8a, D-m): an `aux` request never reaches `decide` at all, in any mode; under `inherit` the exempt flag (I8b) keeps haiku and small-fast asks as asked; the plain statement for the runbook and the report is "free governs subagents with tools; background helper calls (titles, summaries, compaction, small-fast) still use the relay in every mode". (j) Revision 10, D-z: under `dynamic` and `free` a tool-carrying subagent whose DEFINITION deliberately says `model: haiku` is judged by the allowed set like any other and is OVERRIDDEN by the policy; only `inherit` exempts haiku (I8b). The escape hatch is mode `inherit`. (k) Revision 10, ar-17: the handoff path (6.1b E) acts after `decide` for an enforced `dynamic` or `free` subagent that sends a retry signal; it reuses `decide`'s fit and band rules and never runs for `aux`, `exempt`, `main` or `inherit`.

### 6.3 Main-model learning rules

1. Classification (cr-M5) and the hazard in BOTH directions. A request is `main` only when it has no agent-id and no billing marker; it TEACHES main's model only if it also has a session id and an Agent/Task tool. A request is `sub` only when it has an agent-id or the billing marker AND at least one tool; with an agent-id or marker and no tools it is `aux` and is passed through unchanged. Hazard 1 (the original one): Claude Code's background calls (small-fast haiku, title generation) have been seen WITHOUT an agent-id (5 of 28 live rows lack it; their tool content is E2) and must not overwrite "main": the Agent-tool test is the discriminator. Hazard 2 (the one revision 1 missed, and the opposite direction): a haiku or title call that DOES carry an agent-id (critic-reported 21 KB row, E14) would be classified as a subagent by `ag || bl` and then substituted or made to inherit an Opus main, turning a cheap auxiliary call into the most expensive model; the `tools.length > 0` requirement, the `aux` class (I8a) and, under `inherit` only, the `exempt` list (I8b) close it. The price of the rule is stated openly (M-d): a REAL subagent that carries no tools (a tool-restricted agent definition with an empty tool list) is also classified `aux` and bypasses the policy; the matrix counts it (cell `a1.*.t0.*`) and the accuracy protocol measures how often it happens (section 12.5). ALL enforcement modes (`dynamic`, `inherit` and `free`), not only `inherit`, are gated on E2/E3 AND on the measured classifier accuracy of section 12.5 (G3 precondition).
2. Key by session id (`request.sessionId`), so two concurrent Claude Code sessions never cross-contaminate; an unknown session id maps to `nosession`, which NEVER teaches (cr-m2) and for which `inherit` and same-provider `dynamic` return `unknown-main` rather than guess.
3. The in-memory map is primary (`globalThis.__uwSub.main`, survives the per-request `delete require.cache` of the entry file; verified in the sandbox, E7); `state/subagent/main-<sid>.json` `{model, beta1m, t}` is the durable copy, written with the inline write-temp-then-rename on change or every 60 s, read only on a memory miss, ignored beyond `main.ttlSec` (6 h). Windows rename over an open reader can fail with EPERM/EBUSY: the writer retries 3 times with backoff and a failed write is non-fatal (memory still holds the value). All `globalThis.__uwSub` maps (`main`, `agent`, `counters`) are created lazily and bounded (cr-m5): `main` at 64 sessions, `agent` at 256 entries, evict-oldest, TTL-pruned on every 50th call.
4. Provider = the prefix before the first `/` of main's wire model (I5); main's wire model has no `[1m]` (Claude Code strips it and signals 1M with the beta header, research C), hence `beta1m` is stored beside the model.
5. Garbage collection (revision 10: the sticky store is the journal `agents-<sid>.jsonl`, compacted in this hourly pass, 6.1b A): the router deletes `main-*.json` and `agents-*.json`/`agents-*.jsonl` older than 24 h, at most once per hour (one `statSync` of a `gc.stamp` file per request, bounded to 50 deletions per pass); `subagent-policy show` reports the count of session files.
6. A `/model` switch mid-session simply overwrites main's entry with the next main request. A subagent already in flight keeps its sticky model (section 6.1), and a NEW subagent decides against the new main (REVISION 10, D-at: a running agent also keeps its model across a switch to another provider under `same-provider`, as long as that model stays in the whole compiled allowed set). (Revision 1 said "a subagent already in flight keeps the old value" as an accident of timing; it is now a rule with a test.)

### 6.4 Agent-tool-description injection

What and where. D-g: the main agent learns the allowed list through the router writing it into the Agent tool description. Default OFF (`inject: off`), gated at G4, and only after the sandbox has confirmed that a router body edit reaches upstream (E4 PASS, S0). Only on main-role requests that contain an Agent/Task tool and only when `inject: on` and `mode !== inherit` (`inherit` has nothing for main to choose, so zero prompt change) and no rollback flag. For each tool whose lower-cased `name` (or `function.name`) is `agent` or `task` (CCR's own set `TUe`, F1) the router appends one block to `description` (or `function.description`), and one sentence to `input_schema.properties.prompt.description` when that property exists. No other tool, no system prompt, no messages are touched BY THIS INJECTION (revision 10 amends this for the whole router: the ONE other edit the router makes is the handoff notice appended to a SUBAGENT request's system prompt after a handoff, `handoffNotice`, 6.1b E; it never edits `messages` or `tools`). Workflow tools (`workflow`) are out of scope.

Text (rendered by the compiler, at most 2,048 bytes, ASCII, at most 20 entries; ids already passed the cr-m3 sanitiser):

```
[uw-subagent-policy v1 9f3a1c20b7d4]
UW subagent routing policy is active (mode dynamic, source same-provider anthropic, ctx any). When calling this tool, the prompt parameter MUST start with <CCR-SUBAGENT-MODEL>Provider/model</CCR-SUBAGENT-MODEL> on its own first line, replacing Provider/model with one ID from the list below. When the tool model field accepts exact strings, set it to the same ID. If the model field only permits built-in aliases, leave it unset and rely on the tag. CCR removes the tag before the agent runs. An ID outside this list is replaced by policy and the replacement is logged. Do not put the tag in description or subagent_type.
Allowed subagent models (13 of 13 shown; ordered by reliability then context, NOT by quality or price; columns: ctx, price per M tokens, tools verification, speed bucket, payload cap):
- anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5  1M  $3/$15  tools:UNVERIFIED  fast  cap:unknown
- ...
Notice: 13 of 13 listed models have UNVERIFIED tool support (not yet tested under Claude Code's tool payload); a subagent on one may fail on tool use. Premium-priced rows are marked $$. Pick for the task, not for position in this list.
```
Labelling rule (cr-m1): no string the router or CLI renders may call the order "best first" or imply quality; the compiler test greps the rendered strings for "best". Unverified surfacing (D-f): every row says `tools:UNVERIFIED` or `tools:verified`, the footer counts them, and the same count is in `set`, `show`, the doctor WARN and `status.policy.unverifiedAllowed`. Price (D-b) is on every row; premium rows are marked. EXACT ROW FORMAT (build ambiguity closed): `- <selector>  <ctx>  <price>  tools:<tier>  <speed>  cap:<cap>[  ALIAS][  $$]`, fields separated by exactly TWO spaces, in this column order, ASCII only. `<ctx>` is `1M`, `200k`, `128k` (rounded down to the nearest k, `1M` when at least 1,000,000) or `ctx?` when unknown; `<price>` is `$in/$out` per million tokens with trailing zeros trimmed (`$3/$15`, `$0.15/$0.6`), `free` when both are 0, `$?` when unknown; `<tier>` is `verified` (L3 passed or provenance), `small` (L1+L2 passed, tier `t`) or `UNVERIFIED`; `<speed>` is `fast`, `ok`, `slow` or `?` for TTFT bucket 0, 1, 2 or missing; `<cap>` is `<n>k` for a known payload cap in KB or `unknown`; `ALIAS` marks a pool alias (D-n, served model unknown); `$$` marks a premium-priced row (D-b). The row count is at most 20 and the block at most 2,048 bytes; if the cap cuts the list, the header line says "N of M shown".

Empty set text (same block, list replaced): "UW subagent policy WARNING: no subagent model is eligible for main provider groq under mode free / source same-provider. Subagents will run on the model they request, unchanged. Tell the user." This is the injected warning of D-c when injection is on; with injection off the same condition reaches the owner only through the status warning, the once-per-session decision-log entry, `show` and the doctor (section 10).

Idempotence. The block starts with the marker line `[uw-subagent-policy v1 <hash>]`. If the description already contains `[uw-subagent-policy`, the router truncates from the preceding blank line to the end and appends the fresh block (replace, never stack); this matters because `bodyOwnership === "owned"` edits the caller's object in place and a retry can re-route it (F2). If the description contains `<CCR-SUBAGENT-MODEL>` without our marker (CCR's native menu present) we still append ours with the extra sentence "This UW list replaces any list above" and raise `NATIVE_MENU_PRESENT`.

Cache effects. Tool definitions are the head of Anthropic's prompt-cache prefix, so the text must be byte-stable between turns: it contains no timestamps, ranks use TTFT buckets, and `contentHash` (an allow-list of routing content, revision 7 item 5) does not change when only counts or stamps change, so the marker line and the injected text stay byte-identical. A policy CHANGE (or a recompile that changes the shortlist) rotates the text once: running sessions pay one cache miss on their next turn (a cache write of the full tool prefix, tens of thousands of tokens on a paid main). `set` prints this as a note whenever `contentHash` changes with `inject: on`. Non-Anthropic mains have no comparable cost. Size: at most 2 KB against a request floor of 156,981 bytes (V19), under 1.3 percent, and only on main requests.

How main is told to use the tag: through that text, which copies CCR's own instruction (F1). Tag uptake by the main model is experiment E10 and gates `dynamic` enforcement (section 12). Reminder (F7): CCR strips the tag literal from main's own `system` and first two user messages too, so a project file quoting the tag is edited in flight; runbook note.

### 6.5 Per-request cost budget

Warm main request: one `statSync` of `policy.json`, one of `shadow.flag`, a `globalThis` hit, string checks, at most one memory write (file write only on change or 60 s). Warm subagent request: the same plus a sticky lookup, `resolvable()` (microseconds), the token and payload fit checks (arithmetic on `req.tokenCount` and one header, F8, no body stringify) and one `appendFileSync` of about 400 bytes. Cold (policy changed): one `readFileSync` plus `JSON.parse` of at most 1 MiB, plus building a `Map` of at most about 1,800 selectors. Targets and gates (FINAL; D-ai, D-av, the Phase B verifier; the older text of this section, which said that CI asserts p99 under 3 ms, that the enforce first decision of about 14 to 31 ms was an OPEN ITEM, that `slowReq` fires on every new agent and that the measured figures were pending, is SUPERSEDED): the strict QB-1 numbers are warm and shadow under 1 ms p99 and a NEW agent and a HANDOFF under 3 ms p99, at 1,800 rows over 60 providers, best of 3 batches, and they are checked by the STANDALONE `node test/perf/subagent-router-perf.mjs`, run by the verifier on a quiet machine. The test SUITE asserts only LOOSE ceilings (p99 about 10 ms; handoff p50 under 3 ms and p99 under 10 ms), because `node --test` runs files in parallel and the strict bounds flaked in 4 of 8 loaded runs. MEASURED strict results on the FINAL router bytes (Phase B verifier, quiet machine, 3 runs, best p99 of 3 batches, 1,800 rows over 60 providers): warm 0.47-0.54 ms, shadow warm 0.52-0.56 ms, shadow new agent 0.50-0.52 ms, enforce new agent 0.67-0.74 ms, handoff 1.35-1.55 ms (the executor measured warm 0.51-0.56, enforce new agent 0.73-0.75, handoff 1.23-1.37 ms). The sticky journal (ar-2) CLOSED the old enforce first-decision item: a NEW agent appends one line to an in-memory-authoritative store instead of a temp-file-plus-rename. Other measured costs: a policy change costs 18-24 ms once per worker (a pure-JS sha256 verified against `node:crypto`; 9 ms for a 300 KB policy on a cache miss), never on a warm request; the FIRST request per worker costs 19-23 ms (41-46 ms with 256 session files) and the hourly gc 20-22 ms with 256 files, so `slowReq` (5 ms) fires once per worker and once per policy change by design and is NOT a failure (the S3 verifier does not read it as one). The first synchronous status flush per worker is removed. The status writes of ar-5 and ar-6 are asynchronous and do not count against the request. The funnel timing of ar-14 (target 25-45 ms against the 150-290 ms measured before the memo) is reported by the S1r budget output.

### 6.6 Production router location and the legacy slot (D3 amended, D-e replaces D4)

D3 (amended). The production router stays at `spike/uw-router.cjs` for now. Reason: `CUSTOM_ROUTER_PATH` lives in `config.sqlite`, no UW code sets it (V3, V4), and moving it is a config write that buys nothing now; moving it to a supported folder is a later, separate change (follow-up F1). The owner-reported live value is `C:\Users\osami\.uw\spike\uw-router.cjs` (V26; not re-read here). Who wires: the owner, once, by hand as today. The doctor now FAILS when `CUSTOM_ROUTER_PATH` differs from the repo file (compared after `path.resolve` and case-folding) or when that file is missing (D-e, section 10), because the policy is inert otherwise and nothing asserts it today.
D-e (replaces D4). The legacy `claude-opus-5` slot hot-switch is RETIRED, in two steps. Step 1, NOW, as its own stage S0b (the live router is `spike/uw-router.cjs`, so this goes through the section 6.7 protocol and gate G2a): replace the substring test `asked.includes("claude-opus-5")` (V2) with an EXACT match, `asked === "anthropic/claude-opus-5" || asked === "claude-opus-5"`, make a corrupt or empty `slot.json` return `asked` (never `undefined`), and decide/learn from the ORIGINAL `asked` (the rewrite is a last step on the return path, `out()` in 6.1, not an input to any decision). `spike/slot.json` today pins the identity, so on today's traffic the only behavioural changes are that longer ids containing the substring (for example a `claude-opus-5-5` or a `[1m]`-suffixed spelling) are no longer rewritten to `anthropic/claude-opus-5`, and that a corrupt slot no longer falls to the anchor. THIS CHANGES LIVE BEHAVIOUR AND IS ANNOUNCED (stage text, runbook, issue): 60 distinct snapshot ids contain the substring `claude-opus-5` (V29; for example `openrouter/anthropic/claude-opus-5.5` and `anthropic/claude-opus-5-5[1m]`), and every one of them is silently rewritten to `anthropic/claude-opus-5` TODAY; after S0b they are served as asked. Anyone relying on that accidental downgrade to opus-5 will see a different model, which is the intended fix. Step 2, at the first ENFORCED stage (G3): delete the slot code, `spike/slot.json` and the `repoint` writer in `spike/uwmodels-web.mjs`, and the doctor slot WARN; `SLOT_ERROR` is removed. The differential test (section 9.1) carries the explicit "intended differences from git 6edfdee" list.

### 6.7 Deploying to the live router file (cr-B1)

Why this section exists. `spike/uw-router.cjs` is not a staging file, it IS the router: CCR re-requires it on every request (V21), and ANY load-time failure (syntax error, module-scope throw, a missing `require`, a non-function export) is caught by CCR's wrapper, logged as `Failed to load custom router`, and turned into `undefined`, so EVERY request then falls through the policy chain toward the anchor (the #47 failure, for all traffic at once, in every Claude Code session, with no restart needed to cause it and none that would fix it). The router's own try/catch cannot help. Therefore the policy-aware router is developed at `router/uw-router.next.cjs` (never wired), and the live file changes only at G2a (hotfix), G2 and G3 (policy router), each through this protocol, implemented once in `harness/deploy-router.mjs --deploy yes --candidate <path>` (the candidate flag is required: S0b deploys `router/uw-router.s0b.cjs`, the patched copy of the current live file; G2 deploys `router/uw-router.next.cjs`; G3 deploys `router/uw-router.next.cjs` as rebuilt without the slot) and announced to the owner ("applying now, no restart") before it runs:
1. `node --check <candidate>` on the exact candidate file; a non-zero exit stops the deploy.
2. Require smoke test on the EXACT BYTES to be deployed (the script reads the candidate once, writes those bytes to a scratch tree laid out as `tmp/spike/uw-router.cjs` so `__dirname`-derived state is scratch, and requires THAT copy with `createRequire`): the module loads without throwing, `typeof module.exports === "function"`, and a synthetic call `await route({body:{model:"anthropic/claude-sonnet-5"}, headers:{}}, {Providers:[]}, {})` returns the string `"anthropic/claude-sonnet-5"` (the exported function is `async`, so every smoke call is AWAITED; comparing an un-awaited Promise would prove nothing, M-f; the smoke script also asserts the awaited value `typeof` is `string`); a call with an empty model returns `undefined`; a call with a hostile object (null body, tools as a string) returns without throwing. The script then asserts the sha256 of the scratch copy equals the sha256 of the candidate.
3. Backup: copy the current live file to `spike/uw-router.cjs.bak-pre-subpolicy-<yyyymmdd-hhmm>` (never overwritten; a second deploy in the same minute adds a suffix) and verify the copy's hash.
4. Write the candidate bytes to `spike/uw-router.cjs.tmp-<pid>` in the SAME directory, hash-verify it, then atomic rename over `spike/uw-router.cjs`. On Windows a rename over a target that a reader holds open fails with EPERM (CCR reads the file once per request, so the window is tiny): retry with 10, 30, 90, 270 and 810 ms backoff, then abort with the temp file removed and the live file untouched. Never write the live path in place (a torn write is a load-time failure).
5. Post-check: re-hash the live file and `node --check` the live path, then print the one-command restore. (The post-check does NOT execute the live file: its state directory is `__dirname`-derived and would be the real one.)

Rehearsal and smoke-test rules as built (revision 9 item 6). Rehearsals accept only paths under the OS temp dir (an allow-list), so a rehearsal starts with a COPY-FIRST step: copy `router/uw-router.s0b.cjs` to a path under the temp dir and rehearse from the copy (the runbook note says the same). UNC paths and the `\\?\` and `\\.\` spellings are refused. The smoke child runs with a MINIMAL environment and its verdict is read from its stdout with a nonce. The candidate is the owner's reviewed file and runs with the owner's privileges: a hostile candidate is out of the threat model.
Restore (one command, in the runbook section 5b and printed as the script's final line): `node harness/deploy-router.mjs --restore yes` works only when there is exactly ONE backup `spike/uw-router.cjs.bak-pre-subpolicy-*`; with two or more it exits 1 `E_AMBIGUOUS` and `--from <backup>` is MANDATORY (`--restore yes --from <backup>`); it runs steps 1, 2 and 4 on the chosen backup and replaces the live file. A deploy prints the ORIGINAL (oldest) backup path and a restore command that already carries `--from`, and refuses with `E_NOOP` when the live file already equals the candidate (revision 9 item 6). Independent of node and of any UW code, the PowerShell fallback is `Copy-Item spike\uw-router.cjs.bak-pre-subpolicy-<stamp> spike\uw-router.cjs.tmp-restore; Move-Item -Force spike\uw-router.cjs.tmp-restore spike\uw-router.cjs`. A policy rollback that does NOT need a file swap is `subagent-policy rollback` (section 7). The deploy needs no CCR, keysync or gateway action and touches no config.

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 7. The CLI

`node keysync/key.mjs subagent-policy <show|set|clear|explain|rebuild|rollback> [flags]` (REVISION 10, D-s: the S1d UX wave adds the commands `status`, `last`, `report`, `preset`, `help`, `pause`, `resume`, `undo`, `why`, `wizard`, `selftest` and the flags `--banded yes|no`, `--handoff-notice yes|no`, `--detail yes`, `--no-rebuild yes` to this list: section 7.2; the S1r router work adds only the two `set` flags `--banded` and `--handoff-notice`, and NO `--canary` flag; the fourth delivery adds `--ctx prefer-1m` (D-ag) and the explicit `--live yes` that every WRITING command on REAL paths now requires, D-aj: `set` without `--dry`, `rebuild` and `clear` exit 1 `E_USAGE` naming the real paths without it; D-ar: `rollback` and its alias `pause` are EXEMPT, so the emergency command stays one word; 7.2 and item 21 of revision 10; D-aw: the CLI performs the UNC-path check BEFORE `realish()`). Library in `keysync/subagent-policy.mjs` (the I/O shell; EVERY count, funnel stage and refusal verdict comes from the pure `funnel` in `menu/subagent-funnel.mjs`, which the picker toggle pane of S4b imports too, so `set`, `show`, `explain` and the pane can never disagree, D-o); key.mjs only parses and prints (as `default-model`, V13). EVERY flag takes an explicit value (V14), never a bare boolean: `--dry yes`, `--allow-empty yes`. BOOLEAN VOCABULARY (build ambiguity closed): the only accepted boolean values are `yes` and `no` (lower-case, exact); `no` is the same as the flag being absent; a trailing flag with no value (`--dry`), a value that is itself a flag (`--dry --source`), and any other word (`--dry foo`, `--dry true`, `--dry 1`) is a usage error, exit 1, naming the flag and listing the accepted values, so the V14 "swallow the next token" behaviour can never turn a typo into a silent choice; an unknown flag is also exit 1. Enumerated flags (`--mode`, `--source`, ...) reject any value outside their list the same way.

| Command | Flags | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| `set` | `--source same-provider\|all-providers`, `--mode dynamic\|inherit\|free`, `--free-scope models\|providers\|providers+deposit` (default `providers`; only with `--mode free`, D-j), `--ctx any\|128k\|200k\|256k\|512k\|1m\|prefer-256k\|prefer-512k\|prefer-1m`, `--enforce shadow\|enforce`, `--inject off\|on`, `--unverified allow-warn\|allow-t\|pin-only` (S5), `--allow provider/model` (S5; give the flag once per pin; an owner pin makes an unverified model eligible, a failed model additionally needs `--force yes`), `--dry yes`, `--allow-empty yes`, `--min-set N` (default 3; the FRAGILE warning threshold ONLY: warn when a provider list has fewer than N USABLE candidates, M-h; it never causes a refusal, because the refusal threshold is the fixed value 0 of the rule below), `--policy-file P`, `--state-dir D`, and the test-only inputs `--providers-file`, `--snapshot-file`, `--bench-file`, `--observed-file`, `--default-model-file`, `--registry-file`, `--key-choices-file`, `--vault-providers-file`, `--settings-file`, `--tool-fidelity-file` (section 4 safety rule as built, revision 9 item 2: fixture mode when any file flag is present, then `--policy-file`, `--state-dir` and `--providers-file` are all required for `set`) | Validate flags (`--mode fixed` is rejected with "renamed to inherit"), merge with the existing owner file, COMPILE, print the report below; refuse (exit 2, nothing written) per the refusal rule below; otherwise write owner file then compiled file (atomic, retry on rename; also removes `shadow.flag` once the compiled file carries the owner's enforcement). Needs a loadable snapshot (else exit 4 with `E_SNAPSHOT:<reason>`, the `loadSnapshot` reason) and refuses `--enforce enforce` unless `providersLive` is true (a gateway-down compile cannot prove resolvability) and unless the enforcement preconditions of G3 hold (context and payload fields compiled, E2/E3 recorded). Under `--mode inherit` it prints "source ignored under inherit" and does not require `--source` after the first set. |
| `show` | `--providers-file` etc. as above | Owner file, compiled summary (`compiledAt`, `contentHash`, counts, age, `emptyProviders[]`, from S4b the `NO ROWS: <list>` line from `noRowProviders` and `NO ROWS FOR MAIN` when main's provider is in it (revision 8 item 1), the router's effective mode and any gate (revision 8 item 5), the free-scope funnel: probe-ok models and providers under `free models`, `free providers` and `free providers + deposit` whatever scope is chosen, D-j and D-l), `status.json` summary (router last seen, counters, warnings, per-session mains with their price class), staleness lines, the unverified count, the premium-main warning when `inherit` is set and a learned (or the likely) main is Opus- or Fable-priced (D-b), `ROLLBACK FLAG PRESENT` when `shadow.flag` exists, the one-line rollout reminder. Exit 0. |
| `clear` | | Removes owner file, compiled file, `shadow.flag`, `main-*.json` and `agents-*.json`; prints that the router reverts to exact legacy behaviour on its next request. Exit 0 even if absent. |
| `rollback` | `--state-dir D` | COMPILE-FREE incident flip (cr-m8): writes `state/subagent/shadow.flag` and sets `enforcement: shadow`, `inject: off` in the owner file WHEN ONE EXISTS (with no owner file it writes ONLY `shadow.flag` and creates no owner file, M-e), with no snapshot, bench, `getConfig` or compile involved, so it works when the snapshot is unreadable, the gateway is down or the compiled file is corrupt. The router stats the flag on every request and acts as shadow (asked returned, stickiness bypassed) from the very next request. `set` (which compiles) is the only thing that clears the flag. Exit 0. (`set --enforce shadow` also works but RUNS the compile and can fail on its inputs, which is exactly when an incident flip must not.) |
| `explain <provider/model>` | `--tool-fidelity-file F` and the test-only flags | One model: found? chat-capable? in live `Providers`? bench status, age, TTFT, observed overlay; `tools` claim and tool tier from the tool-fidelity record (read through `--tool-fidelity-file`, default `state/tool-fidelity.json`, so offline tests and acceptance never touch the real file); key tier, its source (registry) and the full-length key id; `ALIAS` flag and the pool rule that matched (D-n); price (`$in/$out`, free verdict, premium verdict, `PRICED_BUT_BADGED`); ctx and its basis, `substitutable` verdict; payload cap and band; each toggle and each free-scope verdict (`free models` / `free providers` / `free providers + deposit`) pass/fail with the reason including `deposit-strict-skipped`; rank position and the rank keys that decided it; shortlist membership; "would be chosen as the substitute for any request when main is X". Exit 3 if the id is unknown. |
| `rebuild` | `--quiet yes`, `--if-stale yes` (S4) | Recompile from the current owner file. Run it MANUALLY after every bench sweep and every keysync run from S2 onward (cr-M8; data-driven emptiness exists from the first shadow day, so waiting for the S4 hook would leave a stale compiled file); S4 adds the automatic non-fatal call. WHEN a free-provider model flips to `pay` (D-j): a live 402 is recorded as `pay` by the live observer overlay (`state/observed.json`), which the router never reads, so the compiled lists keep the model until the next compile. The sequence is: (1) the doctor and `show` compare the overlay with the compiled free lists and raise `FREE_LIST_STALE` naming the models that are now `pay` or `auth` (section 10); (2) the owner (or the S4 hook) runs `subagent-policy rebuild`, which excludes them (responding-provider rule: excluded from the policy only, never pruned from routing or the picker); (3) `rebuild --if-stale yes` (S4) recompiles ONLY when the overlay or bench is newer than `builtFrom` or a listed model changed status, and is a cheap no-op otherwise, so a scheduled call is safe; the plan creates no schedule (owner decision, as in phase 6). Until the rebuild, agents already running keep their sticky model (section 6.1) and new agents may still be handed the flipped model, which the top-3 spread limits to a third of the fan-out per model; the window is the time between the flip and the rebuild, hence the S2 acceptance note that data-driven emptiness exists from the first shadow day. Also `rebuild` after every bench sweep, every keysync run and every registry tier change (`retier`, `add`). Idempotent; never changes the owner file; exit 2 on an empty set (it writes the compiled file with `empty: true` and the router reports `EMPTY_SET`, never silently enforcing). |

`set` report (and `--dry yes` prints the same without writing). The figures below are LIVE-DATA figures (critic replay, 2026-10-03), informational and date-stamped; the acceptance commands in section 12 run on the fixture trio instead (cr-M9):
```
subagent policy (DRY): source=all-providers mode=free free-scope=models ctx=1m enforcement=shadow inject=off
funnel (denominator = 6,432 snapshot routes, snapshot rebuilt 2026-10-03, schema 9; the first four stage figures below are the 2026-09-30 counts, NOT re-counted; live-data figures 2026-10-03
        except the Providers stage, which the compiler measures live; the snapshot's own `routable` flag is true on all 6,432):
  chat-capable, not pool row ........ 6,080
  in live Providers .................. (measured at compile time)
  bench ok (fresh or any age) ........ 1,868   [of the 6,080 chat-capable: pay 2,339, auth 198 = account state, responding, not pruned; not re-counted in revision 2]
  tools != false ..................... 1,790   [UNVERIFIED 1,790 = 710 tools unknown + 1,080 catalogue claims; verified 0]
  id-rejected (cr-m3) ................ 0
  free scopes (probe-ok models, then providers; names per D-l; tier from the vault registry, D-k; LIVE REPLAY 2026-10-03, V29, informational, BEFORE the tools stage):
    free models .................... 112 (badge or bare-id free tag; 14 of them >= 1M); strict rule on paid and deposit tiers: 0 rows skipped
    free providers ................. 1,687 on 46 providers (860 unknown ctx, 772 >= 128k; 392 positive-priced, 30 price 0)
    free providers + deposit ....... 1,687 (equal to free providers: no probe-ok free-tagged model on a paid or deposit tier)
    ALIAS (D-n, counted separately)  6 probe-ok pool aliases in the whole universe; flagged ALIAS, never above tier u, ranked below non-alias at equal keys
  chosen scope: free models ......... 112
  ctx >= 1M .......................... 14     [free models AND >= 1M is NOT empty on live data]
ALLOWED: 14 models (before the tools stage; the compiler measures the rest).
SUBSTITUTABLE: 14 (every one has a known ctx >= 1M); providers with substitutable < 3: listed per provider by the compiler.
free governs subagents with tools; background helper calls (titles, summaries, compaction, small-fast) still use the relay in every mode (D-m).
(An empty-set refusal example, exit 2, is in the fixture acceptance of section 12 S1, not a live figure.)
```
Warnings the report can add (each a line, each tested on fixtures): `UNVERIFIED: N of M allowed models have unverified tool support (D-f)`; `PREMIUM: N allowed models are Opus- or Fable-priced (price shown per row; no cap, D-b)`; `INHERIT PREMIUM MAIN: --mode inherit would give every non-exempt subagent main's model, and the likely/learned main <id> is Opus- or Fable-priced ($in/$out)`; `INHERIT BELOW CTX: --ctx 1m with --mode inherit: main <id> is below 1M; subagents inherit it anyway (D-d)`; `FRAGILE: fewer than 3 usable candidates for <provider>: a fan-out concentrates on N model(s) (cr-M6)`; `PAYLOAD: N allowed models have a known payload cap below 1,000,000 bytes (REVISION 11, sa-A1: observed subagent bodies exceed 1 MB in 71 of 337 classified sub requests, 21%; the earlier "largest 919 KB" is superseded); M have no known cap` (cr-M1); `EMPTY for N other providers: <list from emptyProviders[]>` (D-c, saved anyway); `1M basis listing-only for N relay rows (n:1)`; `this policy changes prompt text; running sessions pay one prompt-cache miss` (only when inject=on and hash changed); `source ignored under inherit`; free-scope lines (D-j): `FREE PROVIDERS: the tier is YOUR label from the vault registry; a provider labelled free that bills will bill (N providers listed)`, `CREDIT: free providers have token or credit limits; price-0 models rank first, then cheapest`, `PRICED_BUT_BADGED: N models carry a free badge but a non-zero listed price` (scope `models`), `DEPOSIT STRICT: N probe-ok models on paid or deposit providers were skipped for lacking a free tag (listed by explain)`, `TIERS: registry unreadable (exit 4 `E_TIER_UNREADABLE`): free scopes providers and providers+deposit refused, scope models falls back to the STRICT rule everywhere with this warning` (never a fallback to the snapshot key id, D-k); `TIER_MISMATCH: snapshot key id says X, registry says Y for provider P (registry used)` (M-a); `ALIAS: N rows are pool aliases, flagged ALIAS, never above tier u` (D-n); `FREE PROMISE: N providers have substitutable < 3 and M have 0; when a free-mode request cannot be served from the free set the model it ASKED for (possibly paid) serves it, visibly (counter freeBreak, FREE_PROMISE_BREAK)` (M3); `HELPER CALLS: free governs subagents with tools; background helper calls (titles, summaries, compaction, small-fast) still use the relay in every mode` (D-m, printed on every `set` and `show` for any enforcement or mode).
Refusal rule (R2, D-c). Exit 2, nothing written, ONLY when (a) the SUBSTITUTE pool (`substitutable`, section 5.2; M3) is empty for EVERY provider (for `all-providers`: the union pool is empty; for `same-provider`: every provider's pool is empty), or (b) under `same-provider` the substitute pool is empty for main's CURRENT provider (a set whose rows all have unknown context counts as empty here, because substitution is impossible): the provider of the most recently learned main in live `status.json` (`mainBySession`, newest `t`, under the 6 h TTL) when the router has been seen recently, else the provider of the likely main (the default model, `keysync/default-model.mjs load()`); the report names which source it used and lists any other learned mains whose provider is empty as warnings. `inherit` and `dynamic`/`all-providers` can only hit (a). Rule (b) is UNCHANGED by the `noRowProviders` ruling (Q5, revision 8 item 1): it still refuses at `set` time, zero rows included; `noRowProviders` is the standing signal after the policy is saved and the owner later switches main (picker note, pane, `show`, doctor), and it never causes a refusal itself. Otherwise a set that is empty for SOME other providers is saved with a warning listing them (compiled `emptyProviders[]`). `--allow-empty yes` overrides the refusal deliberately (the router then reports `EMPTY_SET` at runtime, serves the model each subagent ASKED for, and logs it once per session). The refusal text names the stage that emptied the set and the single flag change that would not (for example "free removed all 13 of anthropic's candidates; `--source all-providers` gives N, `--free-scope providers` gives M, `--mode dynamic` gives 13").


Exit codes (complete, build ambiguity closed; the first output line of a non-zero exit is `E_<CODE>: <message>`):

| Code | Meaning | First-line code and cases |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Success, including warnings, any `--dry yes` that WOULD succeed, `show`, `clear`, `rollback`, `rebuild --if-stale yes` with nothing to do | none |
| 1 | Usage or validation error: unknown or malformed flag, boolean not `yes`/`no`, value outside an enumeration, `--mode fixed` (message says renamed to `inherit`), `--free-scope` with a non-free mode, a blank or whitespace test-file-flag value, a fixture-mode path under `~/.llmkeys`, `~/.claude`, `~/.uw/state` or `~/.uw/catalog` (revision 8 item 4), an invalid tier for `add` or `retier`, a refused `retier` (unknown id, same tier, new id exists), `--enforce enforce` with an unmet precondition (gateway not live, fit fields missing, classifier gate unrecorded) | `E_USAGE`, `E_TIER_INVALID`, `E_PRECONDITION` |
| 2 | Empty refusal: `set` refused by the substitute-pool rule (nothing written), or `rebuild` on an empty result (compiled file written with `empty: true`) | `E_EMPTY` (names the stage and the flag change that would not) |
| 3 | `explain` of an unknown id | `E_UNKNOWN_MODEL` |
| 4 | An INPUT is unreadable or ambiguous: corrupt owner file, unloadable snapshot or bench (`E_SNAPSHOT:<reason>`), registry or vault-providers file missing or corrupt, key-choices file present but corrupt, or `chooseKeys` throws "multi-key provider with no deliberate choice" (provider scopes only; the `free models` scope never exits `E_KEY_AMBIGUOUS`: it applies the strict rule and warns `KEY_AMBIGUOUS` / `NO_TIER`, revision 7 item 8) | `E_OWNER_CORRUPT`, `E_SNAPSHOT`, `E_TIER_UNREADABLE`, `E_KEY_AMBIGUOUS` |
| 5 | A write failed (owner file, compiled file, flag, state directory) after the retries | `E_WRITE` |

KEY-CHOICES AND AMBIGUITY (M6): the library reads `key-choices.json` itself and strictly, NOT through `loadKeyChoices`, because that function goes through `readJsonOr`, which turns a corrupt file into `{}` (`menu/atomic.mjs:39-43`, `keysync/keysync.mjs:86-88`) and would then fail only later and confusingly for a multi-key provider: an ABSENT file means no choices, a PRESENT but unparsable file is `E_TIER_UNREADABLE` (exit 4). `chooseKeys` (`keysync/keysync.mjs:90-111`) is called on the filtered registry exactly as keysync does; when it throws for a multi-key provider with no deliberate choice, the compile reports `E_KEY_AMBIGUOUS` (exit 4) naming the providers and the existing message, which is a DIFFERENT code from `E_TIER_UNREADABLE` because the data is readable but does not say which key is wired; a multi-key provider with no choice therefore never gets a guessed tier and `models` scope applies the strict rule to it with the same warning. The registry and vault-providers pair is loaded by a library function `readVaultFiles({registryFile, providersFile})` that parses the two files the way `loadVault` does (`keysync/keysync.mjs:51-55`, BOM-tolerant JSON; `loadVault` itself hard-codes `os.homedir()/.llmkeys`, `keysync/keysync.mjs:29`, and is NOT called) and builds the providers `Map` for `filterRegistry`; the default paths are `~/.llmkeys/registry.json` and `providers.json`. To simulate an unreadable registry an acceptance command passes a non-existent file path to `--registry-file`.

Staleness of inputs. Snapshot older than 7 days (`BENCH_OUTDATED_DAYS`, `menu/bench-data.mjs:257`) or bench older than the snapshot: WARN with the age and proceed (staleness is a warning, never a prune); missing, unreadable or wrong-schema snapshot: refuse (cannot compute). Live `Providers` unreadable (gateway down, `rpc` returns `undefined`): fall back to the snapshot's `routable` flag ONLY when `routableAsOf` is under 7 days old, stamp `providersLive: false`, WARN, and refuse `--enforce enforce`.

### 7.1 Key tier vocabulary, `add` validation and `retier` (stage S1c, D-k)

Vocabulary, defined ONCE as data in `menu/tiers.mjs` (the compiler, `add`, `retier`, the doctor and the docs all read it; nothing re-lists it):

| Tier | Meaning | Free scope it feeds (D-j) |
|---|---|---|
| `free` | Free key with limited credit or tokens | every probe-ok model: `free providers` and `free providers + deposit` |
| `free-deposit` | Free allowance unlocked by a deposit (the owner is considering relabelling deposit providers from `paid` to this) | only free-tagged probe-ok models: `free providers + deposit` |
| `paid` | Pay per use | only free-tagged probe-ok models: `free providers + deposit` |
| `subscription` | Flat-rate route (the Anthropic relay) | none, in no free scope |
| `management` | Admin keys | none, excluded from keysync and bench exactly as today (`keysync/keysync.mjs:63`, `refresh/cli.mjs:50`) |

Nothing in current code depends on the tier value except `management` (V28), so adding `free-deposit` and `subscription` changes no existing behaviour; the free scopes are the first consumer.

`key.mjs add <bucket> <provider> <tier>` validates `<tier>` against the vocabulary BEFORE it calls PowerShell: a typo (`freee`, `Paid`, `deposit`) exits 1 with nothing written and a message that lists the valid tiers (today the tier goes through unchecked, V28, and a typo would silently create a key no scope recognises).

`key.mjs retier <id> <new-tier> [--dry yes]` (PLANNED, S1c; M9 redesign). Today the relabel from `paid` to `free-deposit` is a re-add, because the id embeds the tier (`bucket.provider.tier`) and the secret lives under `LLMKEY:<id>`; `retier` removes the retyping. WHY THE SECRET MUST NEVER REACH NODE (verified, V28 and `~/.llmkeys/ApiKeyVault.ps1`): `Add-ApiKey` reads the secret with `Read-Host ... -AsSecureString` (`:162`), so node cannot drive it non-interactively; and passing a secret as a command-line argument leaks it to the process list and to PowerShell script-block logging. The plan therefore moves the credential INSIDE ONE PowerShell process that only ever receives ids: a here-string script (run by the same `runInherited`-style runner `add` uses, no edit to the vault script) dot-sources `ApiKeyVault.ps1`, then in that single process: (1) `[Win32CredManager]::Read("LLMKEY:<old id>")` (`:192` pattern), (2) `[Win32CredManager]::Write("LLMKEY:<new id>", <value>)` (`:166` pattern), (3) reads the new credential back and compares it with `-ceq` INSIDE the process (a mismatch aborts before anything else changes), (4) updates the registry THROUGH THE VAULT OWN WRITER: `Get-KeyRegistry`, replace the one row (`id`, `tier`, `envVarName`; `added`, `bucket`, `provider`, `notes` kept), `Save-KeyRegistry` (`:137-140`, which writes `ConvertTo-Json -Depth 10 | Set-Content -Encoding UTF8` through a temp file: the encoding carries a UTF-8 BOM under Windows PowerShell 5.1, which node readers already tolerate, `keysync/keysync.mjs:30` `replace(/^\uFEFF/, "")`, and node NEVER rewrites `registry.json`, so there is a single writer; `Remove-ApiKey` also re-saves the file, `:222-225`, which is another reason for one writer). The new `envVarName` follows the existing rule: `LLM_` plus the id upper-cased with every non-alphanumeric turned into `_` (`free-deposit` is valid). The secret value is a PowerShell variable that never leaves the process, never reaches stdout, a log or a file. Node then (5) updates `key-choices.json` through `menu/atomic.mjs` `writeAtomic` when it maps this provider to the old id (node already owns that file: `key.mjs add` writes it), and only THEN (6) a second one-line PowerShell process deletes `LLMKEY:<old id>` with `[Win32CredManager]::Delete` directly (not `Remove-ApiKey`, so the registry is not re-saved). Validation first: `<new-tier>` in the vocabulary, `<id>` in the registry, new id not present, else exit 1.
RESUME BRANCH (idempotent; a crash between any two steps is recoverable by running the same command again): if `<id>` is no longer in the registry but the new id is, with the requested tier, then (a) if `LLMKEY:<old id>` still exists or `key-choices.json` still names the old id, the command FINISHES the move (re-check the new credential, update `key-choices.json`, delete the old credential); (b) if neither holds it prints "already retiered, nothing to do" and exits 0. States after a crash: between (3) and (4) both credentials exist and the registry names the old id (a re-run repeats the copy and continues); between (4) and (5) the registry names only the new id while `key-choices.json` still names the OLD id: DANGLING CHOICE. Its outcome is LOUD, never silent: for a multi-key provider `chooseKeys` finds no entry equal to the stored choice and throws the existing "multi-key provider(s) with no deliberate choice" error (`keysync/keysync.mjs:90-111`), which the compiler reports as `E_KEY_AMBIGUOUS` and keysync itself reports as it does today; for a single-key provider the choice is unused and nothing breaks; the resume finishes it. The old secret is removed last, so a secret is never lost. Follow-ups `retier` does NOT perform: a keysync run to propagate the new env var name into the configuration (owner-authorised, never run by `retier`), and `subagent-policy rebuild`. `--dry yes` prints the plan and touches nothing. The vault is reached ONLY through an injected seam `{retierCopy(oldId, newId, newTier), deleteCredential(id), exists(id)}`; the production implementation runs the two PowerShell scripts above, and the tests inject an in-memory fake (never the real Credential Manager); a separate static test asserts the production script text passes ids only (no secret variable on any command line).

### 7.2 UX wave, report, self-test and extended auto-rebuild (revision 10; stage S1d, S2a, S2c; owner decisions D-s, D-t, D-v, D-w, D-x)

Built AFTER G1 is requested or run, because none of it changes router bytes (the one sentence `status.json.policy.headline` is part of router v2, ar-10) and `keysync/subagent-policy.mjs` is NOT an executed file of G1. Every new command follows the existing flag rules (explicit values, `yes`/`no`, V14, exit codes of section 17 item 7 unchanged, fixture mode as built, revision 9 item 2). User-facing text never shows an internal id (`D-x`, `cr-x`, `n:1` and the like): those stay in `explain` and `why`.

1. `status` (S1d). ONE pure function `verdict(owner, compiled, status, flag, now)` in the shared menu module `menu/subagent-verdict.mjs` (no I/O, imports nothing) feeds `show`, `status`, the doctor, the picker banner and the pane, so they can never disagree (the same discipline as the shared funnel, D-o). States: OFF, SAVED-NOT-COMPILED, NOT-WIRED, SHADOW, ENFORCING, PAUSED, DEGRADED(code). Each state prints ONE sentence and ONE fix command. The word stays `shadow` (the owner did NOT choose the TRIAL rename) and the SHADOW sentence says it "logs what it would do, changes nothing".
2. `last [N] [--since <when>] [--json yes]` (S1d; its `--json` form is S2a). Reads `agents.jsonl`; prints, per agent, "ran on" (what the router returned) versus "policy says" (what it would choose), the decision and why, and any HANDOFF lines through `formatHandoff` (6.1b E). It states in words that the Claude Code transcript shows the REQUESTED model, not the served one (the served model is visible here, in the doctor and in CCR's `request_logs`).
3. `preset [name]` (S1d): `follow-main`, `any`, `free`, `free-1m`; sugar over `set`. With no name it LISTS the presets with live counts (from the shared funnel). Plan's reading of the expansions, to be confirmed at the S1d review and always printed by `preset <name> --dry yes` before anything is saved: `follow-main` = `--mode inherit`; `any` = `--mode dynamic --source all-providers --ctx any`; `free` = `--mode free --free-scope models --source all-providers --ctx any`; `free-1m` = the same with `--ctx 1m`.
4. A real `help` and a no-argument usage (S1d) that prints the owner's toggle map (toggle 1 source, toggle 2 mode, toggle 3 ctx, with the CLI flag of each, section 1) and the command list.
5. `E_EMPTY` (S1d) DIAGNOSES (which stage emptied the set) and offers RUNNABLE cures (for example the preset or flag that would make the set non-empty), instead of only naming the stage.
6. Verdict-first `set` and `show` (S1d): the first line is the verdict, then a DELTA line (what changed against the previous saved policy); the funnel moves behind `--detail yes`.
7. `pause` / `resume` / `undo` (S1d). `pause` is the compile-free `shadow.flag` write of `rollback` (rollback stays as an alias; both are EXEMPT from the `--live yes` guard, D-ar); `resume` re-asserts enforcement through the SAME gate checks as `set --enforce enforce` (it never bypasses a precondition) and also clears an AUTO_ROLLBACK flag (ar-10); `undo` restores the one previous generation `~/.llmkeys/subagent-policy.json.prev` (one generation only, written by the CLI on every `set`).
8. `why <CODE>` (S1d) over ONE warning-code table in `menu/subagent-codes.mjs`, the single home of code text (what it means, the plain wording, the fix command); the status warning list of 5.4 and the doctor read it.
9. `wizard` (S1d, D-t): readline, at most 3 questions, TTY only; the DEFAULT FIRST ANSWER is FREE MODELS ONLY (I17); it shows a PREVIEW (the verdict and delta of the would-be policy), saves with enforcement `shadow` (the owner's "trial"; nothing is enforced by a wizard) and prints the equivalent flags; without a TTY it REFUSES and prints the preset list.
10. Plain wording table (S1d; user text, never the internal term): "eligible" (in the allowed set; what the pane counts, R-pane-1) versus "usable" (substitutable and fits the request); `THIN` (a provider with fewer than 3 usable rows, `thinProviders`); `FREE NOT GUARANTEED` (the `FREE_PROMISE_BREAK` condition); "costs $15+/M output" (the badge on a row whose listed output price is $15 per million tokens or more). Flagged: the $15 badge threshold is the owner's wording for DISPLAY and is DISTINCT from `PREMIUM_RULE` (family `opus` or `fable`, or output >= $20, Q3), which drives the D-b warning and is NOT changed.
11. Runbook section 5b (S1d) carries all of the above, the handoff statement (14 note n) and the toggle map.
12. SELF-TEST AND DRIFT DOCTOR (D-v; S2c for the self-test, S4 for the doctor). `subagent-policy selftest` runs a headless Claude Code through the SANDBOX (never the live gateway), with the SAME ceremony as G1: plan print, typed approval of the plan hash, tripwire, tree-wide isolation proof and retained redacted evidence (revision 9 item 4). It spawns a subagent, asserts the stub upstream received the model the policy chose, and asserts a helper call stayed on `asked`. The doctor adds WARNs for: a Claude Code or CCR version different from the one the classifier record and the last sandbox run saw (T6, 12.5), the Agent tool's name changing (`Task` versus `Agent`), `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` being set (and `_FORCE`, item 7(b) of revision 10), and a stale classifier record (`accuracy.json` older than 30 days or bound to other bytes).
13. REPORT WITH SAVINGS COUNTERFACTUAL (D-w; S2a builds it, S4 wires the doctor inputs). `report [--since <when>] [--session <sid>] [--json yes]` and `last --json yes`: per agent and in total, "asked -> served", why, what WOULD HAVE happened (the shadow `would`), and an input-token cost estimate versus inherit, always labelled "estimate, input tokens only, snapshot prices"; EVERY figure carries its denominator (for example "of 212 subagent requests in 3 sessions since <date>"). The optional `--outcomes yes` is a READ-ONLY join to CCR `request_logs` for ground truth, run by the CLI on demand, NEVER by the router and never on a timer. The `--json` schema is FROZEN (a schema version field and a fixture test). The same data feeds the doctor.
14. EXTENDED AUTO-REBUILD (D-x, extends D-p; S4). After `key.mjs add`, `remove` and `retier`, a default-model `set`, a keysync run and a bench sweep, the CLI runs the compile-only `subagent-policy rebuild --if-stale yes --quiet yes --live yes` (D-aj: the explicit `--live yes` every writing command on real paths now needs) NON-FATALLY and prints ONE impact line, for example `subagent policy: +1 free provider (groq, 12 models) now in scope; rebuilt`. It never enforces and never restarts anything. The flag `--no-rebuild yes` skips it. The picker-exit rule of D-p (hash-gated, only when the owner file changed in that session) is UNCHANGED. The hot-file rules of 12.1 S4 apply (`keysync/run.mjs`, `keysync/key.mjs`: single writer).
15. TESTS (S1d/S2a/S2c): `verdict` for every state and a table test that `show`, `status`, doctor, banner and pane print the same sentence for a fixture; `last` and `report` against fixture `agents.jsonl` (including a HANDOFF line); the frozen `--json` schema; `preset` expansions; `wizard` refusal without a TTY and the three-question cap; `undo` one generation; `resume` refuses when a gate precondition is unmet and clears AUTO_ROLLBACK; `E_EMPTY` cures are valid commands; no internal id appears in default output (a grep test); the auto-rebuild extension runs once, non-fatally, and `--no-rebuild yes` runs nothing; the selftest's plan print and approval gate (the run itself is a gated owner act like G1).
16. MAIN-AGENT SPAWN WARNING (D-bo; S1d wording, S4b picker hook, S5 data). When the selected or live MAIN model has `sp` = no, the surfaces show: "This agent can't summon subagents, so all subagent functionality doesn't apply."; when `sp` is untested: "Not yet tested for summoning subagents." SURFACES: (a) the picker, at the moment a main model is selected (a note beside the pick, like the empty-provider note of section 10), and in the toggle pane; (b) `subagent-policy status` (one extra line after the verdict, not a new verdict state); (c) `show`; (d) the doctor (a WARN for the live main model, read from the router's learned main in the status file and from `sp` in `state/tool-fidelity.json`). CODE-TABLE ROWS in `menu/subagent-codes.mjs` (the plan's proposed codes `MAIN_CANNOT_SPAWN` and `MAIN_SPAWN_UNTESTED`): plain wording as above, a fix text ("pick another main model, or run the tool sweep for this one"), and NO plan ids (`D-`, `cr-`, `sa-`) in default output. The `status` verdict function takes the spawn state as one more input and prints the line, so `show`, `status`, the doctor, the banner and the pane agree. Tests: every state of `sp` for a fixture main model prints the matching sentence on every surface, a hostile model id is sanitised, and a grep test finds no plan id in default output.

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 8. Candidate computation (exact filters)
