The writer stores the derived `t` beside the section 11 fields, because S1 reads `{schema, generatedAt?, models:{"provider/id":{t, alias?}}}`; fields are only ever ADDED, nothing is renamed or bumped. `t` is never set by hand (parity test, F).
 One ADDITIVE field, `lvr` (ADDED by the owner's answer CQ1 = YES): a per-level result string of exactly four characters, one per level L1..L4 (`p` passed, `f` failed, `n` not run), written by the S5 probe on every record. It exists so that the picker cell is exact: section 11's `lv` collapses L1 and L2 into the value 1 and cannot say "L1 passed, L2 failed" or "L4 passed, L3 failed". It changes no section 11 field and no derivation of `t` (the compiled class is unchanged and may be derived from `lv`/`ok` as section 11 says, or from `lvr`; the parity test pins that both give the same class on every combination `lvr` can express). `cleanFidelity` knows `lvr` (a missing or malformed value makes the record invalid: the picker draws `-` and the compiler reads `u`, I28); there is NO coarse fallback from `lv`.
C. THE CELL (the picker maps it from the per-level string `lvr`; the rule is exact, with no coarse fallback). The digit is the HIGHEST CONTIGUOUS LEVEL PASSED: the largest k in 1..4 such that levels 1..k ALL passed. Rule when levels are not monotone: a pass above a failed or unrun level is IGNORED for the digit (example: L4 passed but L3 did not shows `2`, and `show` prints "L4 passed out of order"). Source of the per-level facts: the four characters of `lvr`. Exact reading: first character `f` gives `x` (L1 failed); first `p` with the second not `p` gives `1` (L1 passed, L2 did not, including L2 `f`); first two `p` and the third not `p` gives `2`; first three `p` and the fourth not `p` gives `3`; all four `p` gives `4`; first character `n` (nothing run) gives `-`. An out-of-order pass (a `p` after a non-`p`, for example `ppfp`, L4 passed with L3 failed) is ignored by the digit (`2` for `ppfp`) and REPORTED by `show` ("L4 passed out of order"). The compiled class follows section 11 and the table below: `x` and `1` give `x`, `2` gives `t`, `3` and `4` give `v` (with a `*` in the picker and the class still `v` when `fx` is outdated, D-ap), so `ppfp` gives `t`.
 The corrected cell table (the cell, what it means in section 11's terms, the compiled class it must agree with):

| Cell | Meaning | Compiled `t` |
|---|---|---|
| `x` | L1 failed: no valid forced tool call | `x` |
| `1` | L1 passed, L2 (the `tool_result` round trip) did not | `x` (section 11 needs L1 AND L2) |
| `2` | L1 and L2 passed; L3 not passed or not run | `t` |
| `3` | L1, L2, L3 passed (the ~157 KB fixture; "verified at N KB") | `v` |
| `4` | L1, L2, L3 and L4 (parallel calls) passed | `v` |
| `P` | known good by provenance (the Anthropic relay), not probed | `v` |
| `~3` (dim) | REVISION 11, D-bc: an INHERITED upper bound (a pass at another provider for the same model); never a pass at this key | `u`, never `v` |
| `c` (dim) | REVISION 11, D-bc: the catalogue `tools` claim only, a prior; never eligibility | `u` |
| `-` | not probed (never a guess) | `u` |
| digit followed by `*` | tested against an OLDER tool-set fixture (the record's `fx` is outdated against the current fixture); re-sweep recommended; the digit is the last result | the LAST class (`x`, `t` or `v`), NOT `u` (D-ap) |

 A catalogue `tools: false` model that passes the probe shows its REAL digit. There is NO age-based state: no blank row and no expiry (D-ap). ALIAS and pool rows show their digit but their compiled class stays capped at `u` (D-n: the model behind such an id can change). The PROBE DATE stays visible in `show`, `explain` and the picker detail, and the header stamp `tooled MM-DD HH:MM` stays. The cell is the digit or letter plus an optional `*` (width 2). Colours come from the existing palette bands and the ASCII or Unicode glyphs from the existing `g` glyph set (no new palette or glyph table). The header carries the stamp `tooled MM-DD HH:MM` (the file's `generatedAt` when present, else the newest `at`, local time): the time of the last sweep.
D. PICKER WIRING (stage S5c). ONE lazy read on the first model screen through an injected `toolsOf(target)` (the pattern of `benchOf` and `routableOf`, `menu/uwpick.mjs:372-390`), so the picker import graph and the 300 ms first-frame budget are unchanged (`test/bench-startup.mjs`); the column goes through `layoutFor` with the header and the rows sharing ONE rule, fits the 78-column floor (`FRAME_MIN`, `menu/style.mjs:33`) and DROPS like the other optional columns when the terminal is narrow; it works in the default view AND in the bench view. The toggle pane's verified/unverified split, `explain` and `show` read the SAME reader (`menu/tools-data.mjs`, which imports only `fs` and the sanitiser), so the cell, the pane count and the compiled `t` class cannot disagree. A missing file means every cell `-` and the default view is byte-identical to today; a corrupt file means every cell `-` with a doctor WARN, never `x`. Hot files `menu/style.mjs`, `menu/pick-state.mjs`, `menu/uwpick.mjs`, `menu/legend.mjs`: single writer, serialised after S4b. The column can be BUILT EARLY on a fixture file before the first real sweep; the sweep itself is the owner gate G7 (S5). NOT added unless the owner asks: a filter "tool level >= N" and a sort by tool level.
E. LEGEND (D-ab). A `TOOLS` section in `menu/legend.mjs` built with the existing `entry(key, term, text)` helper (`TERM_W` 11, `LINE_MAX` 72, wrapped), one line per symbol in section 11's wording:

| Symbol | Legend line (plain words; wrapped at 72 characters) |
|---|---|
| `4` | passes everything below, and two parallel tool calls with streamed arguments |
| `3` | handles a realistic Claude Code tool set: the large synthetic fixture (about 157 KB) |
| `2` | a forced tool call and the tool_result round trip both work |
| `1` | a forced tool call works, the tool_result round trip does not (counts as failed) |
| `x` | cannot make a valid forced tool call: failed |
| `-` | not probed, nothing guessed |
| `*` | tested against an older tool set; re-sweep recommended |
| `P` | known good by provenance (the Anthropic relay), not probed |
| `~3` | dim: an upper bound inherited from another provider's pass for the same model, not tested at this key |
| `c` | dim: the catalogue says tools work; nothing was tested |

 A closing note in the same section: this measures tool USE, not quality, and the verdict column's `t` is the CATALOGUE's claim, which can be wrong, while TOOLS is what the model actually did. The last sweep time is shown in the section, the bench glossary page mentions the column, and the toggle pane's `?` opens the same legend.
F. TESTS (S5c). The pure cell formatter as a table test over EVERY digit, state and fixture-version combination (current, outdated, absent) in ASCII and Unicode, including the non-monotone cases (L4 passed with L3 failed shows `2`; L1 passed with L2 failed shows `1`; every `lvr` string over `p`/`f`/`n` of length 4 is covered; a record with a missing or malformed `lvr` is invalid and draws `-`; `show` reports an out-of-order pass such as `ppfp`); PARITY: the cell maps to the compiled class exactly as the table in C says (`x` and `1` to `x`, `2` to `t`, `3`, `4` and `P` to `v`, `-` to `u`, a `*` cell to the LAST class, an ALIAS row capped at `u`; every age-based test of the first text is now a FIXTURE-VERSION test); layout at 78, 100 and 132 columns (every row exactly the frame width, the column dropped when narrow, header and rows from one rule); the default view byte-identical when the file is absent; a 6,000-row toggle cost test; the startup budget unchanged; a hostile id or reason sanitised; the legend obligation test (every symbol the column can draw has a legend entry); every legend line at most 72 characters in ASCII and Unicode; the existing legend lines unchanged apart from the added section, `LEGEND_LENGTH` updated deliberately, scrolling works; QA steps added to `docs/qa-interactive-protocol.md`.
G. WHAT CHANGED AGAINST THE FIRST TEXT, AND WHAT STAYS OPEN. (1) The ladder, the record fields (`lv`, `why`, `at`, `fx`, `ok`, `maxBytes`, `alias`) and the `t` derivation are section 11's; the earlier mismatch list M1-M6 is WITHDRAWN, in particular the loosening that counted a level-1-only model as `t` is DROPPED (a model that passes only L1 is `x`, as section 11 says). (2) AGE-BASED STALENESS IS REMOVED (D-ap, replacing section 11's 14-day FRESH window and 30-day retention): the only invalidation signals are a re-sweep and an outdated fixture version (`*`). (3) The relay is `P` in the cell and tier `v` with basis `provenance` in the compiler, as section 11 says. (4) CLOSED, the only real question: CQ1, whether to add the additive `lvr` field (B) so that the cell can show `1` and the non-monotone cases exactly: the owner answered YES, so `lvr` is added, the cell reading in C is exact and no coarse fallback exists.
H. HONEST CAVEAT (D-ap). Tool capability can change when a provider swaps the model behind an unchanged id or changes its gateway; the policy does NOT detect that except through a re-sweep or through live handoffs (6.1b E). Health keeps its own last-probed dating (a separate mechanism: a provider may remove or modify listings). Tool results never expire by age.
I. REVISION 11 ADDITIONS (D-ba, D-bb, D-bc, D-bd, D-be). (1) The cell grammar of C is unchanged for OWN-tested keys (digits by `lvr`, `x`, `-`, `P`, `*`); two DIM cells are added for keys with no own test: `~3` (an inherited upper bound, D-bc) and `c` (a catalogue claim only), each mapping to the compiled class `u` and never to `v` or `t`. (2) A `v` row is described in the picker detail and in `show` as "verified at N KB" (sa-A4), where N is the size the L3 and `big` steps actually sent (`maxBytes` is a lower bound and never sets `pb`). (3) New record fields, additive and never renaming a section 11 field: `big` (`p`/`f`/`n`, the about 400 KB step, kept out of `lvr`), `capBelow` (bytes just under a failed fixture, used by the compiler for `pb`), a strike count and an inconclusive-reason code (the plan's proposals for the S5 fix round); a size refusal never makes `x`, and a rate, 429, 5xx or timeout stays inconclusive (D-ba). (4) `show` and `explain` print the COVERAGE LEDGER (D-bd): tested, pending (with the reason) and excluded (with the reason), the three counts summing to the candidate count. (5) Tests added to F: the `~3` and `c` cells and their dim rendering in ASCII and Unicode, the parity of both with `u`, the ledger partition assertion, and a hostile inconclusive reason sanitised. (6) The owner's message listed the own-tested cells as 1/2/3/x; digit 4 is kept here (item 6 of revision 11).
K. SPAWN ABILITY AND THINKING-FIRST MARKS (D-bn, D-bp; DESIGN DIRECTION, the final layout is the owner's choice after the post-sweep report, D-bq). The model-level menu gets two more marks beside the tier cell, the `~3` and `c` marks and "verified at N KB": (1) a SPAWN mark from the record field `sp` (yes, no, untested; the relay by provenance yes): it highlights which models can summon subagents when used as the MAIN model; (2) a THINKING-FIRST mark for models that stream reasoning or thinking only within a small budget, from the bench thinking-only flag (392 of 1,787 ok routes, 21.9%, at 96 tokens) and from the probes' `reasoning-budget` pending reason. Working names for the columns are `SPAWN` and `THINK` (the plan's proposal). Both marks go through `layoutFor` (header and rows share one rule) and drop like other optional columns at narrow widths, keeping the 78-column floor. The legend (`?`) explains each mark from the user's point of view with an example (for example: a model marked as unable to spawn can still be a subagent, but if you pick it as the main model, no subagents will start; a thinking-first model may answer with thoughts only if the budget is small). Parity: the spawn mark equals the stored `sp` and the thinking-first mark equals the stored flag and reason; both are covered by a legend obligation test. The L6 `spawn` level is a separate probe, not part of the 4-character `lvr` (D-bn).
L. SWEEP SCOPE AND PAID TIERS ON THE CELL (D-br, D-bt). The ladder as swept splits L3 into L3a (constructs-only pre-check) and L3b (the 157 KB request, with the L4 parallel calls inside it) and adds the fields `af`, `nm`, `cc`, `br`, `er`, `sp` and `big`, all OUTSIDE `lvr`; the third `lvr` character is the L3 verdict as a whole. The TOOLS cell of a PAID, free-deposit, management or unlabelled provider shows UNTESTED (`-`) with the note "not probed (paid tier)" in the picker detail and in `show` (D-bv: such providers are skipped for the tool probe at every level); the cell never claims a level that did not run and never draws `~3` from another provider for such a key as a pass. Tests: the skipped-provider cell and note in ASCII and Unicode, and parity with the compiled class (`u`).

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 11. R3: why `tools` is untrustworthy, and the fix (a separate later phase, S5)

Root causes (re-verified):
1. One source, and it is a claim, not a measurement: `capsOf` returns `c.toolCalling ?? null` from catalogue capabilities (V7). Of the snapshot's 6,432 routes: `tools` true 2,947, false 423, absent 3,062 (re-counted today).
2. Provenance `listing-verified` proves only that the id was listed by the provider, nothing about tools.
3. Pool-style and test-model rows have `tools: null` (`menu/catalog.mjs:762`); relay rows are hard-coded `tools: true` (`:826`).
4. The bench probe has no tools (V8), so a model that cleanly answers "say hello" and chokes on a Claude Code tool schema is recorded `ok`. Measured failures that PASS the bare probe: `groq/openai/gpt-oss-20b` and `cohere/command-r7b` (400 through `keysync/verify-cli.mjs`), `aihubmix/coding-glm-5.1-free` (#118, Read schema), `aihubmix/xiaomi-mimo-v2.5-pro-free` (#119, Artifact schema), `orcarouter/deepseek-v4-flash-free` (#109, 408 KB request refused; payload cap about 100 KB on some free tiers).
5. The live observer cannot see it: 400, 413 and 422 are ignored by design (V9), so tool-bearing failures never reach status; there is also a 128-tool cap on some providers (#116).
6. Both polarities are unsafe: `tools: false` can wrongly exclude a working model (the owner's interim rule excludes it anyway), `tools: true` wrongly admits failing ones.

Fix (extends open issue #121, "[FEAT] Bench: tool-bearing probe", which asks for exactly this probe). Do NOT edit the catalogue flag; add a measured field and key policy on it:
- Ladder: L0 bare chat (exists); L1 one forced tool call returns a well-formed `tool_use` with valid JSON arguments; L2 `tool_result` round trip yields a final answer with no 400; L3 a versioned, redacted, SYNTHETIC fixture of N realistic Claude Code tool schemas including the awkward constructs (maximum 9007199254740991, `anyOf`, `const`, a lookahead regex) plus a roughly 9 KB system prompt, fixture size recorded; L4 two parallel tool calls with streamed arguments. Never replay real logged bodies (privacy); the fixture is hand-built and versioned (`fixtureId`).
- Storage (cr-M3, REDESIGNED; revision 1 stored `tf` on the bench record, which would have lost it): tool fidelity lives in its OWN file `state/tool-fidelity.json`, keyed by `benchKey`, with its own cleaner (`cleanFidelity`), writer (write-temp-then-rename with retry), NO age-based retention (D-ap: tool-test results never expire; an entry leaves the file only when a re-sweep replaces it or, for capacity, when its model has left the catalogue, I37) and a size cap. Record: `{lv, why, at, fx, ok, maxBytes, alias}` where `lv` is the highest level passed (`0` none, `1` L1 and L2 passed, `3` L3 passed, `4` L4 passed; a failed level is `why` plus `ok: false`), `ok` the boolean verdict (renamed from `v`, which collided with the tier letter `v`), `alias` true for a pool alias whose result is advisory only (D-n), `why` the failure reason (redacted, clipped), `at` ISO time, `fx` the `fixtureId`, `maxBytes` the request size the L3 test actually sent, and a tier `v` is DESCRIBED AS "verified at N KB" (never as plain "verified": a model verified at 157 KB can still fail at 900 KB, V27). WHY NOT the bench record (all re-verified, V24): `FIELDS` in `refresh/bench-store.mjs:35` is a whitelist and `toStored` (`:38`) would discard a tool-fidelity field on write; `mergeRecord` (`:82`) returns the WHOLE newer record, so a fresher probe record without the field would overwrite an older one that has it; `compact` (`:237`) rewrites from the same shape; and on read `loadBench` runs `cleanRecord` (`menu/bench-data.mjs:91`) on the overlay winner (`:222-235`), which discards unknown fields again. Four places would have needed coordinated changes in two shared files, each a silent-loss failure. The plan's earlier reference to `cleanApplies` is gone: no such identifier exists in the plan or the repo (0 hits); the real names are `cleanRecord` and `FIELDS`. NO snapshot schema bump: `loadSnapshot` rejects any other schema with reason `schema` (`menu/snapshot.mjs:358-361`), so a 10 would blank every consumer until a keysync run, and keysync runs are not allowed now. The picker's later display of tool fidelity (a follow-up) reads `state/tool-fidelity.json` too; the policy compiler reads it directly, joined to snapshot routes by `benchKey`. Required tests: `loadBench`, `compact`, `mergeRecord` and a keysync-style snapshot rebuild leave `tool-fidelity.json` byte-identical; `cleanFidelity` rejects unknown fields and out-of-range values; a corrupt file means all `u` with a loud doctor WARN, never `x`.
- Scope and cost control (D-i; cr-M3 L3 sizing). FIRST PASS = ALL probe-ok models: L1 and L2 run for EVERY probe-ok model regardless of the catalogue `tools` flag (1,875 on the 2026-10-03 replay, including the 77 with `tools: false`: the false claim is as untrusted as the true one, root cause 6). The 13 Anthropic relay models are known-good by provenance and are NOT probed (subscription route, tool use proven daily, and the live observer never confirm-probes that route either); they enter the compiler as tier `v` with basis `provenance`. If the 1,875 already includes them the probed set is 1,862 (confirm at the dry run). The 175 probe-ok models on paid-tier providers go through the bench EXISTING price ceilings and spend caps unchanged (no new money rule is invented); free-tier models need none. L3/L4 are NOT part of this pass: they run only (a) for models that are policy candidates under the owner toggles, (b) for models the owner pins, or (c) as a separate opt-in pass capped per provider (`--l3 yes`). Because the default `free providers` scope makes about 1,686 models candidates, (a) would be about 1,686 x 40,000 = roughly 67M tokens of free-tier traffic if run for all of them, so L3 is run on the policy CANDIDATES (REVISION 11, D-az: the compiled allowed set filtered to known ctx >= 128,000, plus `--allow` pins), ordered by the policy rank and bounded by per-provider token caps and spend caps, NOT limited to the top 3 per provider; the earlier lazy top-3 rule is SUPERSEDED. Cost table (input tokens; research figures, informational, dated 2026-10-03):

| Level | Scope | Requests | Input tokens | Money |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L1+L2 | all probe-ok models (1,875; 1,862 if the relay is inside it) | about 3,750 | about 2.5M | only the 175 paid-tier models, inside the existing ceilings and caps |
| L3 | policy candidates, owner pins, or opt-in per-provider-capped pass | 1 per model | about 40,000 per request (the real payload is about 157 KB); about 40M for 1,000 passers | free tiers: rate-limit time; paid tiers: the existing caps |
| L4 | same models as L3, with L3 | 1 per model | the same order again (tens of millions for 1,000 models) | as L3 |

The dry run prints per-provider request and token estimates and refuses to start L3 or L4 without `--l3 yes` and a named provider subset or a spend cap, so the estimate is always shown before anything is spent. `maxBytes` is recorded per entry so a later, larger fixture version (`fixtureId`) invalidates `v` honestly. Opt-in command through the existing bench scheduler (per-provider pacing `--per-provider 2`, cool gap, spend caps, `skip` reasons), never automatic; gate G7 (section 12) approves the L1+L2 spend.

BENCH CAPS ARE OUTPUT-BASED, SO G7 NEEDS A NEW ESTIMATOR (M10, verified): the existing caps are $0.10 per row and $5 per invocation plus a 150-minute wall clock, charged from the OUTPUT tokens each provider reports (`refresh/bench-cli.mjs:54-57`, defaults `maxSpend: 5, maxRowCost: 0.1` at `:103`, the ESTIMATE note at `:350`). The bare probe is output-dominated (a five-word answer), but L1/L2 carry tool schemas and L3/L4 are about 40,000 INPUT tokens per request, so the existing estimator would undercount them by orders of magnitude and a cap that looks safe would not be. Therefore G7 is NOT requestable until S5 ships an INPUT-TOKEN-AWARE estimator for the tool-fidelity pass: per planned request `cost = inTokens * pin + outTokens * pout` from the row listed prices (an unknown price on a paid-tier row is charged at the highest listed price among paid rows, the conservative default), `inTokens` = fixture bytes / 4 (about 40,000 for L3/L4, a few thousand for L1/L2), `outTokens` = the probe max; the dry run prints per-provider and total estimates in tokens AND dollars; the per-row ceiling and the total cap are applied to THIS estimate BEFORE each request is sent (not after reading provider-reported usage); the pass refuses to start when the estimate exceeds the cap.
Per-provider cap for the free-tier first pass (M-g): free-tier providers cost no dollars but have token or credit limits, so the all-models L1+L2 pass gets its own cap, `--tf-max-tokens-per-provider N` (default 150,000 input tokens per provider per invocation) on top of the existing pacing `--per-provider 2`; a provider that reaches it is recorded `skip` with reason `tf-provider-cap` for its remaining models and resumes on the next invocation, so a provider with hundreds of models is covered over several runs instead of draining its allowance at once.
NO AGE-BASED STALENESS (D-ap, REPLACING the former FRESH and retention paragraph of M10: a 14-day FRESH window and a 30-day retention that turned an old result into `u`): a tool-test result NEVER expires; it changes only when a re-sweep replaces the record. The ONLY invalidations are (1) the re-sweep, (2) an OUTDATED fixture version: the record's `fx` differs from the current fixture (the Claude Code tool set), which marks the picker cell `*` ("tested against an older tool set, re-sweep recommended") while the compiled class STAYS the last class (not `u`), and (3) the ALIAS cap (an ALIAS or pool row stays at `u`, D-n). `cleanFidelity` caps the file at 1 MiB (about 8,000 records of about 120 bytes); beyond that, records of models that have left the catalogue go first, then the oldest (capacity, not expiry; I37). RE-SWEEP RECOMMENDATIONS, never expiry: a status flip to ok, and a new `fixtureId` (a Claude Code tool-set change or a sanitizer rule-set change, `keysync/ccr-plugins/schema-rules.mjs`, revises the fixture). Newly discovered probe-ok models are queued for the L1+L2 probe automatically inside the bench caps and the G7 spend rules (D-aq). The live-traffic lane (a 4xx on a tool-bearing real request) is ADVISORY only: it may demote a model to 'failed (live)' in the explain output and raise a warning, never prune (responding-provider rule).
- REVISION 10 (D-aa, D-ab; CORRECTED: nothing in this section is superseded): the picker shows this section's ladder (L1 forced tool call, L2 `tool_result` round trip, L3 the ~157 KB fixture, L4 parallel calls) as the `TOOLS` column (S5c, 10.3), mapping the cell from `lv` as the highest CONTIGUOUS level passed, `x` failed at L1, `-` not probed, `*` for an outdated fixture version (D-ap: tool results never expire by age), `P` for the relay's provenance. The record fields and the `t` derivation (`v` = L3 pass, `t` = L1+L2 pass, `x` failed, `u` not probed) are unchanged, except that ALL age-based staleness is REMOVED (D-ap). One ADDITIVE field `lvr` (a four-character per-level result string, `p` passed, `f` failed, `n` not run, one character per level L1..L4) is ADDED to the record by the owner's answer CQ1 = YES (closed in section 16), so that the cell is exact where `lv` cannot say (it collapses L1 and L2 into 1); it changes no field and no derivation above, and the S5 probe writes it on every record (10.3 B, C).
- REVISION 11 (D-ay..D-bd; sa- fixes): PURPOSE: accuracy of categorization first (D-ay). HYBRID PROBING (D-az): L1+L2 on ALL probe-ok keys (including the 74 `tools: false` claims, 4.0% of 1,862); L3, L4 and the about 400 KB `big` step on every candidate (the compiled allowed set filtered to known ctx >= 128,000, plus `--allow` pins), ordered by rank, bounded by per-provider token and spend caps, the tail `pending`; a stratified pilot of about 60 models (about 2.4M tokens) measures the L3 failure rate among L1+L2 passers and decides promotion to all probe-ok models (pre-authorised if needed). TWO-STRIKE (D-ba): `x` needs two failed tries; a thinking-only stream at `max_tokens` is inconclusive (retry with a larger budget, then inconclusive with a reason, sa-T1); a tools-unsupported 404/400 or a schema or construct 400 at L3 is `x` after two strikes; SIZE refusals never make `x` and record `capBelow` (the compiler's `pb` upper bound); rate, 429, 5xx and timeout stay inconclusive. `big` (`p`/`f`/`n`, fixture `cc-tools-big-1` (bumped to `cc-tools-big-2` by D-br)) runs only for L3 passers, stays out of `lvr`, and with L4 is a rank key inside `v` (D-bb). INHERITANCE (D-bc): a pass never propagates as a pass; `likely-x` at a reseller key after a verified fail elsewhere, an upper bound `~3` after a pass elsewhere, the catalogue claim a dim prior `c`, keyed on provider plus family, never excluding. COVERAGE LEDGER (D-bd): every candidate ends tested, pending or excluded, the partition asserted and printed in every sweep report and dry run. The remaining sa- items (sa-A6 seed `x` for #109/#118/#119 rows, sa-T2 re-check policy as the open question CQ4, sa-T4 stored inconclusive reasons) are in the revision 11 table. The age-based rules removed by D-ap stay removed.
- REVISION 11 ADDENDUM (D-bm..D-bq): (1) `state/tool-fidelity.json` gains a top-level `pending` map, a SIBLING of `models`: `{key:{r:reason, n:runs, at}}` (reason code, number of runs tried, time). It is BOOKKEEPING, not a result: a `pending` entry never sets a class, a cell digit or a rank, it only lets the coverage ledger say why a model is untested (sa-T4). `PENDING_MAX` is 5,000 entries (oldest dropped beyond it) and the map is COUNTED in the 1 MiB size cap of the file. (2) The record gains the 1-character field `sp` (`p`/`f`/`n`, D-bn), kept out of `lvr`. (3) The candidate set, the priority queue P1-P5 and the tier exclusions are in D-bm. (4) Reasoning-budget stalls (thinking-only streams) get one escalation retry and then `pending: reasoning-budget`.
- The 3-tier switch (S5; until it exists, D-f applies: unverified models are allowed WITH a warning surfaced in `set`, `show`, the injected list, a standing doctor WARN 'N unverified models allowed' and a status field): compile tiers `v` verified (L3 pass, 'verified at N KB', with a `*` marker when `fx` is outdated, D-ap; or known-good by provenance for the 13 Anthropic relay models), `t` tools work at small payload (L1+L2 passed, L3 not run, D-i), `u` unverified (not probed, or an ALIAS or pool row, D-n; NEVER because of age, D-ap), `x` failed. The standing warning (D-f) counts `u` and `t` separately. Policy `unverified: allow-warn` (default until the probe exists), `allow-t` (`v` and `t` eligible, `u` only by pin: the realistic strict setting after the all-models pass, M10) or `pin-only` (`v` only; collapses most sets unless L3 has been run widely) (after S5, flipped by the owner with a printed 'this removes N models and empties these providers' diff, because the flip can empty sets and the D-c rules apply). Pins: `set --allow provider/model` (an `allow` entry makes an unverified model eligible; a failed model needs an additional `--force yes`). Failed (`x`) is excluded in every case.

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 12. Rollout stages, gates, acceptance, rollback

Experiments that gate stages (the UNCONFIRMED table) are in 12.2 (revision 10 adds X1-X9); the helper-call classifier accuracy protocol is 12.5. REVISION 10: the execution order of 12.0 is replaced (router v2 first, G1 on its exact bytes); the gates themselves, their numbering (G1, G2a, G-read, G2, G3, G4, G6, G7) and their wording are unchanged, and no new gate is added: the self-test of D-v reuses the G1 ceremony (typed approval of the plan hash) and the handoff of ar-17 is part of G3 (enforcement), not a gate of its own; D-ac adds a G3 PRECONDITION (10), the PASS of the end-to-end scenario suite S2d, and the quality bar of 12.6 states what "ready" means. Owner gates, in the order they are asked (execution order, 12.0): G1 start a second 3.0.22 sandbox daemon beside the live one; G2a deploy the S0b slot hotfix to the live router file (D-e; hot, announced "applying now", no restart; section 6.7); G-read read-only `getConfig` for the live compile; G2 deploy the policy router in shadow (same protocol, new backup); G3 `--enforce enforce`, which also deploys the router WITHOUT the slot code (D-e step 2; there is no separate slot-retirement gate, the old G5 is folded into G3) and is BLOCKED until the classifier matrix of 12.5 meets its thresholds; G4 `--inject on` (D-g: only after E4 PASS in the sandbox); G6 flip `unverified` to `allow-t` or `pin-only` after S5; G7 approve the S5 all-models L1+L2 probe pass (D-i: about 3,750 requests and 2.5M input tokens, spend only on the 175 paid-tier models and only inside the bench price ceilings and spend caps AND the input-token-aware estimator of section 11), and separately any L3/L4 pass beyond the lazy first step. REVISION 10, D-aq: once G7 is approved, later INCREMENTAL probe runs for newly discovered probe-ok models (L1 and L2; L3 and L4 lazily as decided) follow the existing bench caps without a fresh approval each time (I38). REVISION 11, D-bq: the POST-SWEEP REPORT is a deliverable of the G7 run. The numbering is therefore G1, G2a, G-read, G2, G3, G4, G6, G7: "G1..G7" is a range in which G5 is unused. S2d. END-TO-END SCENARIO SUITE (revision 10, D-ac; built AFTER G1; each RUN is an owner act; its PASS is G3 precondition (10); the standards it proves are the quality bar of 12.6). `harness/subagent-scenarios.mjs` runs in the SAME isolated sandbox as G1: the same ceremony every run (plan print with hash, typed approval, tripwire, tree-wide isolation proof, retained redacted evidence, refusal on a dirty scratch tree), a REAL headless Claude Code (`claude -p`) against the stub upstream, on the exact router bytes that G1 passed. The suite never touches the live gateway, `~/.llmkeys`, `state/`, `~/.claude` or the live router (QB-8, hash-compared before and after). Each scenario states WHAT IT MUST PROVE and the suite prints PASS or FAIL per scenario with the run count (QB-10):
 1. SPAWN-AND-SERVE: a spawned subagent is served the model the policy chose (the stub's received model equals `ret`), and `last` shows it. (The S2c self-test is the one-run form of this scenario and shares its code.)
 2. FREE MODEL RETURNS 429: the stub returns 429 for the chosen free model; Claude Code retries, the router HANDS OFF to a different eligible model that fits (6.1b E), the task COMPLETES, and the handoff is in the log and in `last` (QB-5). If X3 and X5 show that no retry signal reaches the router, this scenario reports FINDING (degraded honestly), not PASS.
 3. ALL FREE MODELS LIMITED: every eligible model returns 429; the agent FAILS GRACEFULLY (no hang, no storm of retries), the cooldown ladder steers the NEXT agent away from the cooling models, `handoffNone` is counted, and routing is never blocked.
 4. TEAM AGENTS: agents whose ids carry `@` (for example `ccr-logs@session-f49cde2f`) are classified as subagents, not `main` (ar-1, X4).
 5. `/model` SWITCH MID-SESSION: a switch changes main for NEW subagents and leaves a running subagent on its sticky model, EVEN under `same-provider` when the new main is on another provider (D-at: the model must stay in the whole compiled allowed set), the one exception being a handoff.
 6. CORRUPT, MISSING AND NEWER POLICY: each serves `asked` with the matching warning (`POLICY_CORRUPT`, `POLICY_ABSENT_SKIPPED` silent, `POLICY_NEWER`) and no request fails (QB-2).
 7. WORKER RESTART: after the router state is torn down and rebuilt, the sticky journal replays the agent's model (including a handed-off model) (ar-2).
 8. HELPER CALLS: title and compaction calls are never rewritten (`ret == asked` on every aux and exempt request; T2).
 9. ROLLBACK MID-RUN: `pause`/`rollback` takes effect on the VERY NEXT request and the gateway pid and `service.json` mtime are identical before and after (QB-3).
 10. 20-SUBAGENT FAN-OUT: twenty concurrent subagents are spread across the models of the lead row's band (banded spread, D-u), none outside the band, none on a cooling model.
 11. DAILY-CAP 429 (research, 6.1c; the honest-bound scenario): the stub returns a 429 whose `Retry-After` is larger than 60 s; Claude Code is expected to fail the subagent AT ONCE with NO further request reaching the router; the scenario proves the DEGRADED behaviour the quality bar promises: ONE failure, then AVOIDANCE (the failed model is marked cooling or demoted by the overlay hint, and the NEXT agent, a respawn with a new agent id, is steered to a different eligible model); it is reported as that, never as a seamless handoff.
 CHAOS CHECKS (same sandbox): a WALL-CLOCK JUMP (forward and back; TTLs, cooldowns and throttles behave, ar-16); a TORN JOURNAL (a partial last line is ignored, nothing throws); ENOSPC on the log and journal (the request is still served, `logDropped` counts, ar-7); TWO WORKERS on one state directory (they converge: same sticky model, cooling visible to both, merged status, ar-2, ar-6, X2).
 Acceptance (the BUILD, offline): `node harness/subagent-scenarios.mjs --plan` prints the scenario list, what each must prove and the plan hash, performs no I/O and starts no process; a run without an approval file exits 1; the assertion code is unit-checked against fake transcripts and a fake stub (a scenario FAILS when the served model is wrong, when a helper call is rewritten, when a handoff is missing from the log). Acceptance (a RUN, a gated owner act): every scenario PASS (or the marked FINDING for scenario 2), the chaos checks PASS, and the isolation proof shows zero live-state writes. A PASS records the router sha256, the Claude Code and CCR versions and the date, and expires on a router byte change or a Claude Code or CCR update (T6).

Order of the real run: free-tier providers first (no spend), then paid tier under the caps. Acceptance: ladder unit tests with fake streams; `state/tool-fidelity.json` is untouched by `loadBench`, `compact` and `mergeRecord` (a failing-first test of the cr-M3 failure mode); no snapshot schema change (`git diff` shows `menu/snapshot.mjs` untouched); a dry run reports request and token estimates before spending (L1+L2 about 3,750 requests and 2.5M tokens for the full set, L3 and L4 about 40,000 input tokens per request) and the relay models as "not probed (provenance)"; the probe set contains the `tools: false` models (a fixture with a `tools: false` model that passes L1+L2 compiles as `t`); first real run limited by the scheduler's caps on a named provider subset; G6 flip prints the removed-model diff; `x` models absent from every list in the compiled file. COMPLETENESS (owner remark, QB-12) AND AUTOMATIC INCREMENTAL PROBING (D-aq): the first sweep covers ALL probe-ok models (1,875 at the 2026-10-03 replay), so after S5 an untested row is only a NEWLY DISCOVERED one (results never expire by age, D-ap); a newly discovered probe-ok model is QUEUED automatically for the tool probe, about 2 small requests per model (L1 and L2 first; L3 and L4 lazily as already decided), inside the existing bench caps and the G7 spend rules; the FIRST real run still needs the G7 owner approval, later incremental runs follow the caps (I38); the owner will notice a small automatic quota spend after discovery and the new model's `TOOLS` cell filling in. The sweep report prints the counts (probe-ok, with a record, with a record against the CURRENT fixture version). Tests: the queue is the probe-ok models with no record, a record against an outdated fixture is NOT re-queued (a `*` is only a recommendation), incremental runs stay inside the per-provider cap `--tf-max-tokens-per-provider`, and a model with a record is never re-probed by the incremental pass. CQ2 and CQ3 are CLOSED (section 16).
S5 REVISION 11 (D-ay..D-bd, sa-A1..sa-T5; replaces the lazy L3 plan above where it differs). SCOPE: L1+L2 on ALL probe-ok keys including the 74 `tools: false` claims (4.0% of 1,862); L3, L4 and `big` on every candidate; a stratified pilot first (about 60 models, about 2.4M tokens) to measure the L3 failure rate among L1+L2 passers and to decide whether L3/L4 are promoted to all probe-ok models. RULES: two-strike `x`; thinking-only at `max_tokens` inconclusive and escalated (sa-T1); size refusals record `capBelow` and never make `x`; rate, 429, 5xx and timeout inconclusive with the reason stored (sa-T4); `big` (`p`/`f`/`n`) kept out of `lvr`; inheritance never propagates a pass (D-bc, sa-T5); seed `x` for the known-failing rows of #109, #118 and #119 (sa-A6). THE COVERAGE LEDGER (D-bd): each candidate ends tested, pending or excluded and the partition is asserted and printed in every dry run and sweep report. ACCEPTANCE ADDED: fake-stream tests of two-strike (one fail then a pass is not `x`; two fails are), of the thinking-only escalation, of a 413 that records `capBelow` without `x`, of a 429 that stays inconclusive, and of the partition assertion; the dry run prints the pilot estimate and the ledger; inheritance tests (a pass elsewhere gives `~3` and never `v`; a verified fail elsewhere gives `likely-x` and never excludes); `maxBytes` never sets `pb` (a failing-first test); the 74 `tools: false` rows are in the L1+L2 probe set. STATUS: the S5 fix round is running (review findings `s5-F1`..`s5-F8`, the audit items `s5-R1`..`s5-R8`, the candidates, the ledger and the inheritance helper).
S5 ADDENDUM (D-bm..D-bq). CANDIDATES: every probe-ok model on a FREE-TIER provider (1,683 free-tier keys of 1,870 probe-ok keys, 45 providers; 1,639 candidates plus 44 ctx-too-small-for-fixture; dry run 2026-10-05, D-bm), regardless of known ctx, in the priority order P1-P5 of D-bm; paid (175 keys, 7 providers), free-deposit, management and unlabelled providers are SKIPPED ENTIRELY at every level, L1+L2 included (D-bv, default-deny, enforced in the engine; the ledger reason is `excluded: not-free-tier (skipped for now)`); `--include-tier` with an explicit `--levels`, `--live`, a printed per-tier estimate and an explicit `--max-spend` lifts it (a lifted tier may get any listed level, 1 and 2 included); L3 is skipped for a known-too-small ctx and the `big` step below about 200k known ctx. `--sample N` (default 60) is the stratified pilot and prints the L3 failure rate among L1+L2 passers per provider family. A thinking-only stream gets one escalation retry with a larger budget, then `pending: reasoning-budget`. The `pending` map (section 11) holds the reasons. SPAWN (L6, D-bn): the real Agent tool schema from a Claude Code fixture, `tool_choice` auto, a task that warrants delegation; PASS is a valid Agent `tool_use` with a non-empty `prompt` and a recognised `subagent_type`; about 3k tokens per model; field `sp` (`p`/`f`/`n`, out of `lvr`); relay by provenance; two-strike; run for every free-tier probe-ok model that passed L1+L2. ACCEPTANCE ADDED: fake-stream tests of a valid spawn, of an empty `prompt` (fail), of an unrecognised `subagent_type` (fail), of a text-only answer (fail, two strikes), and that `sp` never appears in `lvr`; the candidate queue order P1-P5 on a fixture; paid, deposit, management and unlabelled providers are skipped entirely (no request is ever sent for them) and appear in the ledger with `excluded: not-free-tier (skipped for now)`, and no environment variable, config file, onboarding call or incremental call can lift the rule (a failing-first test of each entry point); the `pending` map respects `PENDING_MAX` and the size cap; `--sample` prints the per-family rate. POST-SWEEP REPORT (D-bq): after the sweep the agent writes the report (tier distribution, spawn counts, thinking-first counts, size-cap findings, provider-family patterns, failures by cause, each with its denominator) with a mock layout at 78 and 132 columns for the owner to choose from.
S5 SWEEP SCOPE AND OPTIMISATION (D-br, D-bs, D-bt). LADDER AS SWEPT: L1 under `tool_choice` auto (forced only as a diagnostic fallback); L2 with a tool_result of about 20 KB carrying the fact at the END plus a small `is_error` case; L3a constructs-only pre-check (about 6 KB: `anyOf`, `const`, `maximum` 9007199254740991, a lookahead regex, `additionalProperties` false, `uniqueItems`, `enum`, an MCP-style long name, `cache_control`); L3b the 157 KB request (41 tools, L4 parallel calls inside it); the about 400 KB `big` step (101 tools) for L3 passers; L6 `spawn` (`sp`); A1 argument fidelity (`af`, rides in L1/L2), A2 long MCP-style names (`nm`), A3 `cache_control` tolerance (`cc`), A4 big result and error result (`br`, `er`). The new 1-character fields stay out of `lvr`; fixtures `cc-tools-2` and `cc-tools-big-2`; about 150k tokens per fully tested model. OPTIMISATION: L3a before L3b; A1, A4 and L4 folded into existing requests; cancel the stream once the needed content is complete; minimal `max_tokens` per level (L1, L2, L3b about 256; L6 about 512) with the single escalation; timeouts 15 s, 60 s, 90 s per class; per-provider concurrency 1 for the WHOLE run when the run includes any request >= 100 KB (the code is stricter than the per-request rule and is safe), else 2, global 8, round-robin interleave, `Retry-After` backoff, a pause on repeated 429; a canary per provider; a resume skips confirmed levels; `--order l3-first|big-first` decided by the pilot pass rates; telemetry of actual against estimated tokens and seconds. REJECTED: skipping the rest of a provider after a few passes, copying a pass across providers, sampling the `big` step. PAID-TIER HARD RULE (D-bt, TIGHTENED by D-bv): NOTHING, L1+L2 included, runs by default for a provider whose vault key tier is not `free`. ACCEPTANCE ADDED: fake-stream tests of each level and field (`af`, `nm`, `cc`, `br`, `er`) and of the cancel-when-complete behaviour; the engine sends NO request, at any level, for a paid, deposit, management or unlabelled fixture provider without all of `--include-tier`, an explicit `--levels`, `--live` and an explicit `--max-spend`, and the tier it uses equals the compiler's effective tier for the chosen key (key-choices; the most restrictive only when the choice is unresolved); the dry run prints the per-tier cost and the dollar caps; the telemetry line compares actual and estimated tokens and seconds; a resume re-runs no confirmed level.
S5 ROUND RESULTS (revision 11): the security round (sec-s5) and the policy code review (cr-s5) are in the table after the sa- audit table; D-bu (priced models on a free key are probed, costed at the listed price, `--max-spend` required, rows over `--max-row-cost` are `pending: priced-over-row-cap`) and the hard 12-requests-per-model ceiling (`pending: request-cap`) apply to every S5 run. Acceptance added: a priced fixture row is probed and costed at its listed price; a run with any priced entry and no `--max-spend` refuses; a row over the ceiling is `pending: priced-over-row-cap`; a cache_control retry loop stops at 12 requests with `pending: request-cap`; `resolveTierRows` agrees with the compiler on the tier of every fixture provider (management key, conflicting tiers).
S6. KEY ONBOARDING PIPELINE (revision 11, D-bf; NEW; after S5 is verified and G7 has proved the mechanism). The default onboarding of any new key runs, in order: models discovery, the response (bench) probe sweep for that provider, the tool-capability sweep (L1+L2 on its probe-ok models, then L3/L4/`big` on its candidates), then `subagent-policy rebuild` with a one-line impact print. PAID-TIER RULE (D-bt, D-bv): a new key whose vault tier is not `free` gets discovery and the response (bench) sweep ONLY, NO tool probe at any level; nothing deeper ever runs from onboarding. It uses the same caps and the free-first order, prints a dry-run estimate first, and `--no-probe yes` opts out; an inconclusive step leaves the models `pending` and NEVER blocks adding the key; `retier` re-queues L3 failures. It generalises D-aq (incremental probing of newly discovered models) and D-x (auto-rebuild). HOT files `keysync/key.mjs` and `keysync/run.mjs` are serialised with S4 and S4b (single writer). Acceptance (a fixture vault seam, no real key): adding a fixture key runs the four steps in order with the estimate printed first; `--no-probe yes` runs none; a failing step leaves `pending` and the key is still added; a `retier` re-queues exactly the L3 failures; a fixture paid key gets discovery and the response sweep only and no tool probe; the impact line carries its denominators; no secret in any output (QB-8).
