### 12.3 Rollback (never a restart)

Revision 10: `pause` is the new name of the same compile-free flip (`rollback` stays as an alias), `resume` re-asserts enforcement through the same gate checks, `undo` restores the one previous owner-file generation, and the router's tripwire (ar-10) can perform the flip by itself during the first 200 enforced requests per worker (`shadow.flag` content `auto:<code>:<iso>`, warning `AUTO_ROLLBACK`; only `set` or `resume` clears it). Fastest to slowest: (1) `node keysync/key.mjs subagent-policy rollback` (compile-free: writes `shadow.flag` and flips the owner file, effect on the very next request, works with a broken snapshot, a down gateway or a corrupt compiled file); (2) `... set --enforce shadow` (same effect but it runs the compile, so it can fail on its inputs); (3) `... clear` (removes policy files, router returns to exact legacy behaviour); (4) restore the router file when the ROUTER is the problem (load failure, bug): `node harness/deploy-router.mjs --restore yes` (or the PowerShell fallback in section 6.7), which re-runs `node --check` and the smoke test on the backup before the atomic rename; (5) delete the compiled file by hand. None writes CCR config, none needs `ccr` or keysync. R1 is also the rollback property: the same mechanism applies and removes the policy. Rollback is never sticky (section 6.1): a subagent in flight is served `asked` from the next request on.

### 12.4 Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Router error falls open to the anchor (opus-5) silently, the #47 class | Every path returns `asked`; `resolvable()` check; fuzz and differential tests; `ROUTER_ERROR`, `unresolvable` counters; S4 doctor scans recent traces for `custom-model-not-configured` |
| Router FILE fails to load (syntax error, module-scope throw): CCR catches it and EVERY request goes down the policy chain to the anchor (cr-B1, V21) | Develop at `router/uw-router.next.cjs`; deploy only via section 6.7 (`node --check`, exact-bytes require smoke test, temp plus atomic rename, backup); no module-scope side effects (source scan); one-command restore; doctor FAILS on a missing or differing `CUSTOM_ROUTER_PATH` file |
| A decision changes under a running subagent (main switch, policy edit, bench move) | Per-agent sticky map, first enforced decision final (cr-B2; revision 10: a 6 h sliding TTL with a 24 h cap, in memory plus an append-only journal, and ONE deliberate exception, the retry-triggered handoff of ar-17, R-v2-8), rollback bypasses it; required test |
| Auxiliary calls (title, summary) under an agent-id treated as subagents and made to inherit or substitute | `aux` class (no tools, never rewritten), `exempt` haiku/small-fast values under `inherit`, E14, E2/E3 gate on ALL enforcement modes (cr-M5) |
| Substitute cannot hold the request (context or payload) | Floor `max(asked ctx, 128k)` even under `ctx any`, `tokenCount` and `content-length` fit, compiled `pb`, `set` warnings, enforcement precondition (cr-M1) |
| Per-request file reads | stat + `globalThis` cache; budget test; 1 MiB cap |
| Stale policy (bench or Providers moved on) | `rebuild` manually from S2 and automatically from S4; `providersHash`; doctor staleness; router validates each returned selector against live `config.Providers` |
| Background-call pollution of "main's model" | Agent-tool + no-agent-id + session-id rule; per-session keys; TTL; E2; E15 for compaction |
| Claude Code or CCR version drift (header renamed, Agent tool schema or tag handling changed, signature changes) | contract test pins; DETECTOR_DRIFT; re-run sandbox and re-verify E1/E3 after every Claude Code or CCR update; `routeRequest`/`BUe`/`OUe` offsets re-found each time |
| Shared router file with the opus-5 slot (substring bug rewrites longer ids today) | S0b exact-match hotfix now, differential test with intended-differences list, slot removed at G3 (D-e) |
| Injected text changes main's behaviour and the prompt cache | separate `inject` switch (default off, G4); byte-stable text; cache note in `set`; size cap |
| Prompt-injected tag choosing a model | tag honoured only if in the owner's allowed set; `dropTag` clears an out-of-set tag |
| FREE? rows that still bill | `set` prints FREE and FREE? separately; observed `pay` demotes |
| `inherit` hands every subagent an Opus- or Fable-priced main model (cost; no cap by D-b) | warning at `set` and `show` and a status warning `INHERIT_PREMIUM_MAIN`; price on every row and decision line; haiku/small-fast exempt (I8); `--max-price` is a later idea |
| Unverified tool support (D-f) | allowed with a warning everywhere (`set`, `show`, injected list, standing doctor WARN, status field) until S5 |
| Windows rename-over failures (EPERM) on state files | writers retry with backoff and skip a flush rather than throw; readers retry; no timers (cr-m4) [revision 10, ar-5: no blocking sleep on the request path; the retry runs on a request-scoped `unref`'d timer on the failure path only, X7; "no timer outside a request"] |
| Credit drain on free-tier providers: a fan-out of subagents lands on free keys with token or credit limits (the default `free providers` scope is about 15 times the badge scope) | price-0 first then cheapest inside free-tier providers (key 2b); top-3 spread by agent hash; price on every row; shadow tally of `would` by provider before enforcement; `rate`/`pay` demotion on the next `rebuild`; `FREE_LIST_STALE` WARN |
| A provider labelled `free` in the registry actually bills (the tier is the owner own label, trusted by `free providers`) | tier visible in `explain` and the `set` provider list; the bench `pay` status excludes and the doctor flags a free-labelled provider whose models mostly show `pay`; `key.mjs add` validates tier names and `retier` makes correcting a label cheap; the strict free-tag rule protects only paid and deposit labels, not a wrong `free` label |
| A deposit unlocks paid models that look free (listed price 0 or a FREE? badge) | strict free-tag rule inside `free-deposit` and `paid` providers: untagged models are skipped whatever their price or badge; no override list; tagged rows must still be probe-ok and never `pay` |
| Tier source drifts: the snapshot key id is clipped (issue #148) or the registry changes after compile | tier read only from the vault registry; `tiersHash` in the compiled file; doctor WARN on mismatch or unreadable registry; `TIER_UNREADABLE` refusal instead of a snapshot fallback |
| Hot-file edit collisions | single writer per hot file, lanes by file (section 13) |
| Picker-exit auto-rebuild (D-p) runs when it should not, fails silently, hangs the exit, or is mistaken for something that changes routing | runs only when the owner-file hash changed in that session (zero spawns otherwise, tested); quiet and non-fatal with a timeout; a failure writes `state/subagent/autorebuild.json`, shown by the next picker open and the doctor (`AUTOREBUILD_FAILED`); it only compiles (never an enforcement flag, never `ccr`, never a gateway restart); the permanent AUTO-APPLY rows in the pane and the legend tell the owner exactly what exit will run; a CLI edit made during the picker session also changes the hash and triggers one harmless rebuild |
| R-v2-1 (rev 10) Team-agent ids such as `ccr-logs@session-f49cde2f` were rejected by the old `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}` rule and misclassified as `main` (policy bypass, and such a request could teach main's model) | ar-1: charset `^[A-Za-z0-9_@.:-]{1,128}$`, `agentIdOdd` counter and a doctor WARN when it is non-zero, X4 records the real shapes and the charset is widened before G2 if one fails |
| R-v2-2 (rev 10) NULL-MODEL STICKINESS: a "serve asked" outcome (empty set, unknown main, unresolvable) was stored and then pinned the agent to `asked` for hours even after the cause was fixed | ar-3(a): null-model decisions are never stored; a test that the same agent, after the cause is fixed, is decided fresh |
| R-v2-3 (rev 10) BLOCKING SLEEPS on the request path (rename retry backoff of 10-810 ms) add latency to every request under contention | ar-5: asynchronous writes with an in-flight guard, no blocking sleep, a request-scoped `unref`'d timer on the failure path only; a source scan; X6 and X7; the budget test |
| R-v2-4 (rev 10) LOG RETENTION AND LOSS: unbounded volume, or silent loss when limits are hit (and rate-limit drops make the classifier accuracy samples incomplete) | `agents.jsonl` current file plus 3 rotated generations (up to 4 MiB), sampling (first hit, then 1 in 50), token buckets, ENOSPC backoff, `logDropped` counter and `LOG_DROPPED` warning; residual: dropped `classify.jsonl` lines shrink the 12.5 sample, so the accuracy protocol takes its denominators from the counters and refuses a verdict when `logDropped` is non-zero in the window (plan's reading, pinned by an S2 test) |
| R-v2-5 (rev 10) MULTI-WORKER DIVERGENCE: workers hold different memory, overwrite one status file, or serve a stale main model | journal tail reads (ar-2), per-worker `status-<w>.json` merged by readers (ar-6), `main-<sid>.json` re-stat every 5 s (ar-9), `cooling.json`; X2 measures the worker count; residual: up to about 5 s of stale main model and a cooling mark visible to another worker only after its next stat |
| R-v2-6 (rev 10) VERSION SKEW between the router file and the compiled file (a newer compiled file served by an older router, or the reverse; a file edited by hand) | `ROUTER_VERSION` against compiled `minRouter` (`POLICY_NEWER`), `ino` in the cache key, `contentHash` re-verified on a cache miss (`POLICY_HASH`); both serve `asked`; deploy order router first, compiled file second |
| R-v2-7 (rev 10) WALL-CLOCK JUMPS (NTP, sleep, manual change) extend or kill a TTL, a cooldown or a throttle | ar-16: a monotonic base for durations and a clamp of file timestamps in the future; seam test; residual: a file written under a different clock is clamped, not corrected |
| R-v2-8 (rev 10) HANDOFF (ar-17) misfires: (a) a retry NOT caused by the model (network, 5xx) triggers an unneeded switch and a cooldown on a healthy model; (b) a mid-life model switch breaks thinking-signature continuity or the tool dialect (E16, P5); (c) a cascade: the handoff lands on the next free model and cools that too; (d) the `len` signal false-positives on a normal turn (X5); (e) flapping between two models; (f) the notice edit does not reach upstream (X8) or alters the cache prefix for that one request | handoff only in enforce, `dynamic` and `free`; at most 3 handoffs per agent per hour and a trail rule (never back within 30 min unless it is the only usable row); the cooling ladder DEMOTES rather than removes; banded spread and the fit checks stay in force; `handoffNone` and `handoffCap` are counted and visible; the notice is best effort and its failure is counted `noticeFail`; the runbook states that a non-model retry can cause a harmless switch; residual: when every free row is cooling the agent keeps failing and the owner sees HANDOFF lines then `handoffNone`, which is the honest outcome (the CCR fallback chain stays OFF) |
| R-v2-9 (rev 10) TRIPWIRE misreads: an automatic rollback flips enforcement to shadow and nobody notices, or a stuck `auto:` flag keeps the policy off | warning `AUTO_ROLLBACK`, `show` prints "AUTO-ROLLBACK (reason)", the status headline and the doctor carry it; only an explicit `set` or `resume` clears it; the trip window is the first 200 enforced requests per worker only; residual: enforcement stays off until the owner acts, by design |
| R-v2-10 (rev 10) BANDED SPREAD concentrates a fan-out when a band is thin (K' = min(K, bandSize) can be 1) | `FRAGILE_SET` on the decision, the existing `set` K < 3 warning, and the owner switch `banded no`; the owner decision D-u makes it default ON knowingly; the band is defined by D-ag (tool tier + health + context preference class + price class, recency classes only ORDER inside a band, so the top-3 choice stays calendar dependent when a band has more than 3 rows) |
| R-v2-11 (rev 10) The UX wave and its automation surprise or hide state: the extended auto-rebuild (D-x) runs after more events than the owner expected; the self-test (D-v) touches something live; a verdict sentence disagrees between surfaces | the rebuild is compile-only, non-fatal, prints one impact line, never enforces or restarts, and `--no-rebuild yes` skips it; the self-test runs only through the sandbox with the G1 ceremony and the isolation proof; ONE pure `verdict` feeds every surface and a table test pins that they agree |
| R-v2-12 (rev 10, D-aa) The `TOOLS` column is misread: as a QUALITY ranking, as current when it is old, or as agreeing with the catalogue `t`; a model that passes only L1 shows `1` while its compiled class is `x` | the legend says this measures tool USE not quality, that `1` counts as failed and that the catalogue `t` can be wrong; `*` for an outdated fixture version (no age expiry, D-ap); the header stamp `tooled MM-DD HH:MM`; `show` prints the level and any out-of-order pass; the parity test of the cell against the compiled `t` (10.3 F); the additive `lvr` field (CQ1 = YES, closed) makes the cell exact, so `1` and out-of-order passes are distinguished |
| R-v2-18 (rev 10, D-an) ROUTER MEMORY GROWTH: CCR re-requires the router file on every request and each re-require leaks a Module record (v2 measured 12.5 KB per request, 515 MB after 30,000 requests; v1 and s0b 1-2 KB) | the router body is evaluated ONCE per process behind a stat-keyed `globalThis` wrapper (mtimeMs + size + ino, older keys dropped, so a hot redeploy still loads new code); QB-11 (growth per request under 4 KB by an `--expose-gc` loop); a source scan of module scope; residual, MEASURED on the final bytes: after the splice the leak is 0.075 KB per require (0.009 KB per request over 30,000 requests, 0.26 MB total), not the 1-2 KB the older text assumed |
| R-v2-19 (rev 10, D-an) HANDOFF ABUSE: one session (or a forged header) cools many models or the whole catalogue, or a case variant of a sid creates a second session and file | a per-sid cap of 4 distinct models cooled per hour (D-as: raised from 2; the per-session rung-escalation limit is DROPPED because the ladder lengths already pace it and the limit left a failing model undemoted for about 47 minutes; a denied failure still marks rung 0), the header alone never triggers a handoff for an agent with an agent id, the sid lower-cased, session files only for sids that sent a main request with an Agent tool, a sticky hit requires membership in the whole compiled allowed set (D-at), journal replay capped at 256 KiB with a leading newline on the first append |
| R-v2-20 (rev 10, D-aj) A CLI WRITE REACHES REAL STATE BY ACCIDENT (incident: an empty fixture variable made `set` and `rebuild` write the real owner and compiled files) | the explicit `--live yes` on every writing command on real paths (exit 1 `E_USAGE` naming the real paths without it), a test that an empty flag set never writes, the picker auto-rebuild and keysync passing `--live yes`; D-ar: `rollback` and its alias `pause` are EXEMPT from the guard (one word must work in an emergency; they only move toward safety and create no policy files when none exist), so every printed rollback command is plain `rollback`; residual: `resume` and `undo` still need `--live yes` (I36) |
| R-v2-21 (rev 10, D-ap; REFRAMED: the stale-result risk of the first text is gone because tool results never expire by age) A tool-fidelity result stays in force although the model's real tool behaviour changed (a provider swapped the model behind an unchanged id or changed its gateway), or a result was recorded against an OLDER fixture version | the policy does not detect a swap except through a re-sweep or live handoffs (stated honestly, 10.3 H); an outdated `fx` marks the picker cell `*` ("tested against an older tool set, re-sweep recommended") while the class stays; ALIAS and pool rows stay capped at `u` (D-n); newly discovered models are probed automatically (D-aq) and a re-sweep replaces records; the probe date is visible in `show`, `explain` and the picker detail |
| R-v2-22 (rev 11, sa-A1) THE BYTE GATE IS INERT: `pb` is 0 on all 110 allowed rows, while 317 of 337 (94%) classified sub requests are over 200 KB and 71 (21%) over 1 MB, so a substitute can be handed a body it cannot take | G3 precondition (11): `pb` filled from `capBelow` and gateway-log refusals before G3; the unknown-`pb` ranking is a router-bytes-DEFERRED change that needs a new G1 run; size refusals record `capBelow` and never make `x` (D-ba); E24 measures free-tier tolerance |
| R-v2-23 (rev 11, D-bc) INHERITANCE MISLEADS: a pass at one provider is read as a pass at a reseller key, or a verified fail elsewhere excludes a model that works at this key | a pass never propagates as a pass (`~3` is a dim upper bound), `likely-x` and the catalogue claim `c` never exclude, inheritance keys on provider plus family; evidence of disagreement in 3 of 3 cases checked (D-bc); the ledger shows why a model is pending |
| R-v2-24 (rev 11, D-bn, D-bo) SPAWN ABILITY IS MISJUDGED: a main model that can summon subagents is flagged as unable (a false `sp` = no from a probe that was too strict or a budget stall), or one that cannot is shown as able (a probe that does not match the real Agent tool use) | the real Agent tool schema from a Claude Code fixture, a task that warrants delegation, two strikes before `sp` = no, a thinking-only stall is `pending: reasoning-budget` and never `f`, the relay verified by provenance; the warning wording says "not yet tested" for untested and never claims more; the post-sweep report counts `sp` yes, no and untested with denominators |
| R-v2-25 (rev 11, D-bt) THE DEEP SWEEP SPENDS MONEY ON A PAID KEY: an entry point (onboarding, the incremental queue, an environment variable or a config file) sends any tool-probe request (L1 and above, since D-bv) for a paid, deposit, management or unlabelled provider | default-deny enforced IN THE ENGINE, lifting needs `--include-tier` plus an explicit `--levels` plus `--live` plus a printed per-tier estimate plus an explicit `--max-spend`; no env, config, onboarding or incremental path can lift it (a failing-first test per entry point); the dry run prints the per-tier cost first; residual: the label is the only guard, so a mislabelled paid key is probed deeply and `--include-tier paid` lifts every paid provider at once (D-bt) |
| R-v2-14 (rev 10, D-ad) COOLING misfires: a transient failure cools a healthy model for hours (the 6 h rung), a provider key cools because two unrelated models failed close together, or everything cools and the agent keeps failing | the 6 h rung only after 3 consecutive failures of the same model within 24 h; reset after the cooldown plus 1 h of quiet; provider-key cooling needs two DIFFERENT models within 5 min; DEMOTION ONLY (a cooling row is still used when nothing else in the band is usable, so routing is never blocked); `COOLING` warning and `status` list the entries and the time left; `cooling.json` is capped at 64 and fail-open; residual: when everything is cooling the honest outcome is repeated failure visible in `last` |
| R-v2-15 (rev 10, D-ae) The OVERLAY HINT is wrong or lags: the observer runs only when the picker or keysync triggers it, so `rate`/`pay`/`auth` marks can be hours old or missing, and a model may be demoted for a limit that already reset, or used after one began | age caps (2 h `rate`, 24 h `pay`/`auth`); a hint only (ignored when stale, absent or corrupt, never a veto, never moves a sticky agent); the cooling ladder is the fast signal; residual: a lag of hours is accepted by the owner (D-ae) |
| R-v2-16 (rev 10, D-af) The HOOK misbehaves: it prints the wrong agent's handoff (`agent_id` differs from the header), prints twice, blocks a tool call, floods, or modifies `settings.json` without approval | X9(g) must pass before the hook is offered (agent_id equals the header `aid`); a per-agent last-printed marker; exit 0 on every path and silent on error; a bounded tail read; install only through keysync with a printed diff and the owner's typed approval; `hook remove` and `hook status`; tests in S1e |
| R-v2-17 (rev 10, research 6.1c) The retry signal never reaches the router: `x-stainless-retry-count` was `0` on 243 of 243 live rows, and a Retry-After above 60 s makes Claude Code fail the subagent at once, so handoff is not seamless for daily caps; a Retry-After above 60 s or an OpenRouter mid-stream limit (an SSE error inside a 200) produces no retry at all | `len` is the expected live signal (X5); QB-5 states the honest bound; ONE failure plus AVOIDANCE by cooling and the overlay hint; S2d scenario 11; the fallback chain stays OFF (6.1c); X3 and X9 measure what Claude Code and CCR actually do before G3 |
| R-v2-13 (rev 10, D-ac) The scenario suite S2d touches live state, passes by accident (a stub that always agrees) or expires unnoticed after a Claude Code or CCR update | the G1 ceremony every run (typed approval, tripwire, tree-wide isolation proof, evidence), hash comparison of live paths before and after, assertion code unit-checked against fake transcripts that MUST fail (wrong served model, rewritten helper call, missing handoff line), and a PASS that records the router sha256 and the Claude Code and CCR versions and expires on a router byte change or an update (T6) |

SECOND-REVIEW FINDINGS of router v2 (D-aw; the summary the owner asked to be recorded): a FUZZ of 100,000 requests produced 0 non-string and 0 out-of-set returns (QB-2); MUTANT testing killed 31 of 38 mutants on the first pass (the survivors were reviewed and tests added); the DEPLOY REHEARSAL on the new bytes passed 5 of 5 steps twice and refused with `E_NOOP` on the third run, as designed (6.7). Fixes from the review that are recorded in the text: the tested-tier rule for every handoff (D-au), the loader hardening and guarded splice (D-aw), the cross-worker file index (6.1b A), the loose latency gates with a standalone strict check (D-av) and the UNC check before `realish()` in the CLI (D-aw).

PHASE B VERIFIER SUMMARY (router v2 final bytes, verified 2026-10-05; recorded as reported): fingerprints identical; the five router and policy suites 287 of 287; the sandbox suite 81 of 81; the S0b hotfix suite 45 of 45; the full suite 2,424 with only the 2 known `checkHandoff` failures; the leak 0.075 KB per require; the deploy rehearsal on the new bytes 5 of 5 steps twice, `E_NOOP` on the third run, and a working restore; the CLI-1 checks (the `--live yes` guard of D-aj) passed; 14 of 14 mutants killed; the secret scan was clean. The strict latency numbers on these bytes are in 6.5. This summary follows the SECOND-REVIEW FINDINGS above and supersedes their interim figures where they differ.

Pre-mortem (twelve ways this fails after launch; P13 and P14 follow in the picker-pane risks). P1 enforcement silently serves everything from the anchor because a router bug or a selector spelling made every substitute unresolvable (mitigations above; the signature is a flood of `unresolvable` plus anchor-priced requests; the doctor and `report` surface it, and the S3 gate requires zero `custom-model-not-configured`). P2 `inherit` mode inherits the wrong model because "main" was learned from a background call or another session (signature: `UNKNOWN_MAIN` or `inherit` lines whose `main` differs from the session's real model; E2 and the per-session key prevent it; the S2 shadow log compared against the owner's own `/model` history is the acceptance). P3 a Claude Code update renames or drops `x-claude-code-agent-id` and the billing block, so every subagent request is classified as main and the policy looks "off" while appearing healthy (signature: `main` counter grows, `sub` stays 0; DETECTOR_DRIFT in doctor and `show`; contingency C1 hint headers). P4 ROUTER LOAD-TIME FAILURE: a bad deploy (syntax error, module-scope throw, wrong export) makes CCR log `Failed to load custom router` and send every request to the anchor, for every session at once (signature: `request_route_traces` show `customer.custom-router` with no target and anchor-priced requests; mitigations: section 6.7 protocol, restore command, doctor, S0b rehearsal). P5 MID-LIFE SWITCHING: main changes model or the policy is edited while subagents run, and a subagent continues on a different model (lost thinking-signature continuity, different tool dialect or window; signature: one agentId with two different `ret` values; mitigation: per-agent stickiness, E16). P6 CONTEXT OVERFLOW: a substitute with a smaller window or a free-tier payload cap receives a 200-900 KB subagent body and answers 400/413 (signature: `ctxSkip`/`payloadSkip` counters stay 0 while errors rise; mitigations: floor, fit checks, compiled `pb`, enforcement precondition). P7 AUXILIARY MISCLASSIFICATION: title or summary calls under an agent-id are made to inherit an Opus main or are substituted (signature: `aux` cell non-empty with `act` other than `aux`; mitigations: `aux` class, `exempt`, E14). P8 FREE-TIER 429 STORM: a fan-out of N subagents all lands on the same one or two free models and is rate-limited (signature: bench/observed `rate` flips, 429s in `request_logs` for the chosen models; mitigations: top-3 spread by agent hash, K < 3 warning, `rate` demotion on the next `rebuild`; the CCR fallback chain stays OFF on purpose, so there is no silent retry on a paid model). P9 CLAUDE CODE UPDATE CHANGES THE AGENT TOOL SCHEMA OR TAG HANDLING: the tool is renamed, the `model` field changes, or CCR/Claude Code stop passing the tag, so injection silently has no effect or the tag is no longer stripped (signature: DETECTOR_DRIFT, a `tag` field that is always null with injection on, or a literal tag visible in a subagent's prompt; mitigations: contract test pins, re-run of the sandbox after every Claude Code or CCR update, E9/E10 re-measured). P10 CREDIT DRAIN ON FREE PROVIDERS: with `free providers` as the default scope about 1,686 models on 45 providers are eligible, and a fan-out of subagents (or a long-running agent team) concentrates on the few price-0 top-ranked models of a provider and exhausts its token or credit allowance, after which every subagent on it gets 402/429 (signature: a rising share of `pay`/`rate` outcomes for one provider in `request_logs`, `FREE_LIST_STALE`, the shadow `would` tally dominated by one provider; mitigations: price-0 then cheapest ranking, top-3 spread, shadow tally reviewed before G3, `rebuild --if-stale yes`, the CCR fallback stays off so there is no silent retry on a paid model; residual risk: the window between a flip to `pay` and the rebuild). P11 A DEPOSIT UNLOCKS PAID MODELS THAT LOOK FREE: the owner makes a deposit on a `free-deposit` or `paid` provider, its catalogue then lists many models with price 0 or a FREE? badge, and a looser rule would route subagents to them and bill the deposit (signature: spend on a deposit provider with no free-tagged traffic; prevention: the strict free-tag rule skips every untagged model there, and a tagged row must still be probe-ok and never `pay`; test: the PAID-PROVIDER RULE test with price-0 and badge fixtures). P12 A WRONG TIER LABEL: a provider is labelled `free` (or relabelled by `retier`) while it bills, or the registry is edited after the compile so the compiled lists describe old tiers (signature: `pay` outcomes on a free-labelled provider, doctor tier WARNs; mitigations: tier shown in `explain` and `set`, `tiersHash` mismatch WARN, `key.mjs add` validation, `rebuild` after every `retier`).

Risks of the toggle pane (revision 5, D-o). R-pane-1: the pane counts differ from what the router will serve (the pane shows policy ELIGIBILITY using the snapshot `routable` flags, the compile uses live `Providers`, the router uses `config.Providers` per request): mitigated by the label "eligible", the `compiled says N (as of T)` line and the doctor (section 10.1 item 6). R-pane-2: a save looks live but is not (the router reads only the compiled file): the banner and doctor say `policy not applied`, the automatic post-exit rebuild of the wrapper (D-p) or a manual `rebuild` applies it, and the pane save text and the permanent AUTO-APPLY rows say so. R-pane-3: a keypress changes live routing: prevented, the pane has no mode toggle and cannot enable enforcement, and a save is inert until compiled under the existing gates. R-pane-4: the pane regresses picker startup or breaks the import-graph rule: dynamic import on first `ctrl+t`, the 300 ms check and the static-graph assertions in 9.4 item 6. R-pane-5 (revision 6, D-p): the automatic rebuild on picker exit surprises the owner, runs when nothing changed, fails unseen or slows the exit: the permanent AUTO-APPLY warning (pane and legend, never hideable), the hash gate (no change, no process), quiet non-fatal timeout-bounded execution, `autorebuild.json` shown on the next open and by the doctor, and 9.4 items 12-15; it can only compile, never enforce or restart.
Pre-mortem additions (revisions 5 and 6). P13 THE PICKER SAYS ONE THING AND THE ROUTER DOES ANOTHER: the owner flips a toggle in the pane, sees "1,687 models included" and saves, but the rebuild never ran or failed, so the router keeps serving the old policy while the picker implies the new one (signature: owner file newer than compiled, `policy not applied` banner, doctor FAIL; mitigations: banner, doctor, the automatic post-exit wrapper rebuild (D-p) with its failure record `autorebuild.json` shown on the next open, the pane save text and the permanent AUTO-APPLY rows, parity tests). P14 THE PANE COUNT IS TRUSTED AS A PROMISE: the owner reads "eligible 1,687" as "1,687 will serve", but 860 have unknown context and are not substitutable, a live overlay flip moved some to `pay`, and live Providers drop some (signature: `compiled says N` differs, `FREE_PROMISE_BREAK`, `emptyProviders[]` non-empty; mitigations: the split shown in the pane itself (not substitutable, ALIAS, unverified), the STALE row, and the label "eligible"). P15 (revision 6, D-p) THE AUTO-REBUILD ON PICKER EXIT MISBEHAVES: (a) it runs although the settings did not change (a hash computed on different bytes, a rewrite of identical content treated as a change, a CRLF or BOM difference, or a hash taken after the picker started instead of before) and spawns a process the owner was promised would not run; (b) it fails (timeout, missing node, a locked compiled file, a snapshot problem) and the owner believes the new policy is live; (c) it hangs the picker exit or the ctrl+g handoff; (d) it compiles from stale or partial inputs and publishes a wrong compiled file; (e) a future edit makes it pass an enforcement flag or restart something. Signatures: a stub or process-start count above zero for an unchanged session; `autorebuild.json` with `ok: false` or older than the owner file; `policy not applied` persisting after exit; a slow exit; a compiled file newer than the owner file with different `counts`. Mitigations: hash over the file BYTES taken before the picker starts, equality means zero spawns (9.4 item 12, including rewrite-same-bytes and change-then-revert); failure writes `autorebuild.json`, shown on the next open and by the doctor, and `policy not applied` stays visible (item 15); timeout and non-fatal exit code (item 13); `rebuild --if-stale yes` is the existing atomic compile with its own input checks, and a compile failure must leave the previous compiled file in place (an S1 requirement of the atomic compile writer, already tested there); a source scan pins that the wrapper step contains only the one fixed argument list.

### 12.5 Helper-call classifier: measured reliability and accuracy (D-m; BLOCKS G3)

Why. The class decides which request is rewritten, and a wrong class is costly in BOTH directions: a real subagent classified `aux` is a policy BYPASS (a paid model serves work the owner meant to constrain), and a helper call classified `sub` is an UNWANTED REWRITE (a title, summary or compaction call sent to a free model, or made to inherit an Opus main). The inference rule (agent-id header or billing flag, and `tools.length`) is plausible but unmeasured (E1-E3, E14), so the owner requirement is: it is MEASURED before enforcement.
What is under test: class `main` = no agent id and no billing flag; `sub` = agent id or flag, and at least one tool; `aux` = agent id or flag and NO tools (helper call, I8a); `exempt` = a `sub` under `inherit` that asks a haiku or small-fast env value (I8b).
Inputs. In shadow mode the router writes the classification log of section 5.5 for EVERY request (agent-id present, `builtInClaudeCodeSubagent` flag, `tools.length`, system-prompt size bucket, body model, `x-claude-code-request-class` and `x-claude-code-agent-type` when present, byte-size bucket, token count; NEVER a body) together with its class.
Ground truth (labelled sets; a label is what the HARNESS or the SPAWNER caused, never what the router decided):
- Sandbox set (E17): a real Claude Code process run headless (`claude -p`) with its base URL pointed at the SANDBOX gateway and the claude-code profile key (section 9.2), against the stub upstream. The stub scripts the assistant: on a main request that carries the Agent tool it answers with an Agent tool call of a chosen subagent type whose prompt carries a marker `UWGT:<label>:<n>`; the stub (sandbox only; bodies never leave it) finds the marker in the resulting subagent requests and writes `(request time, sid, label)` to a labels file. Helper triggers are run in otherwise idle windows and labelled by trigger: first-prompt title generation, `/compact`, a Bash-permission prefix check (small-fast), session summary. Real-subagent labels: `sub-explore`, `sub-executor`, `sub-restricted` (an agent definition with a restricted but non-empty tool list), `sub-team` (team agent `name@session`), `sub-workflow`, `sub-toolless` (an agent definition with an empty tool list, E19); helper labels: `aux-title`, `aux-compact`, `aux-smallfast`, `aux-summary`; `main`.
- Live set: a labelled window of the live shadow log: the owner or an executor agent spawns KNOWN subagent types and triggers KNOWN helper events in a live session and records `(start, end, sid, label)` in `state/subagent/gt-labels.jsonl`; labels join to `classify.jsonl` by `sid` and the time window plus or minus 2 seconds.
Minimum samples (N per class, all must be met or the verdict is INSUFFICIENT, not PASS): 40 requests for each of `sub-explore`, `sub-executor`, `sub-restricted`, `sub-team`, `sub-workflow` (and at least 30 distinct agents across them); 10 for `sub-toolless`; 40 for each of `aux-title`, `aux-compact`, `aux-smallfast`, `aux-summary` (at least 200 helper requests in total, since T2 is stated on 200); 200 `main` requests over at least 2 sessions including one `/model` switch; at least 3 sessions and 2 different calendar days overall.
Confusion matrix: rows are truth (`sub-*`, `sub-toolless`, `aux-*`, `main`), columns are the router class (`sub`, `exempt`, `aux`, `main`). The cells that matter: truth `sub-*` classified `aux` (BYPASS rate), truth `aux-*` classified `sub` or `exempt` (UNWANTED-REWRITE count), truth `main` classified anything else (learning pollution).
Thresholds (ACCEPTED by the owner as proposed, revision 6, Q2 closed; all must hold):
- T1 at least 99% of real TOOL-CARRYING subagent requests (all `sub-*` except `sub-toolless`) are classified `sub`, AND the one-sided 95% Wilson lower bound is at least 97% (with 200 requests and no miss the bound is about 98.7%; with one miss in 200 it is about 97.8%).
- T2 ZERO helper calls (`aux-*`) are classified `sub` (or rewritten) in a sample of at least 200 helper requests (rule of three: the true rate is under 1.5% with 95% confidence).
- T3 `sub-toolless` bypass is REPORTED, not gated; the owner is told if tool-less real subagents exceed 5% of real subagent requests (then I8a bypasses meaningfully).
- T4 ZERO `main` requests classified `sub` or `aux`, and ZERO `main-learn` events caused by a non-`main` request.
- T5 the detector disagreement rate (`ag != bl`) is at most 1% of non-main requests and every disagreement is explained in the review. REVISION 11 (sa-A9): restated on BUILT-IN subagents only, because teammates carry an agent id and no billing flag (298 of 310 sub requests disagree in the live shadow, and 292 of those 298 are teammates), so T5 as written cannot hold on all sub requests.
- T6 the record states the Claude Code and CCR versions and the date; a Claude Code or CCR update, or an older than 30 days record, invalidates it (`CLASSIFIER_UNMEASURED`).
Procedure. (1) In S2, with `classLog` on, run the sandbox set and the live set. (2) `harness/classifier-eval.mjs --log state/subagent/classify.jsonl --labels <labels> --out state/subagent/accuracy.json` (new, read-only apart from its output file, S2) computes the matrix, the intervals and the verdict `PASS`, `FAIL` or `INSUFFICIENT`. (3) Every off-diagonal cell is reviewed one request at a time by a reviewer agent who did not write the evaluator; a wrong label is corrected and the correction recorded. (4) `accuracy.json` holds `{ccVersion, ccrVersion, at, minSamples, matrix, thresholds, verdict}`.
Gate. G3 is BLOCKED until `accuracy.json` has verdict PASS with matching versions and an age under 30 days; revision 7 item 6: AS BUILT S1 checks only the verdict `PASS`, an age under 30 days and live Providers, NOT the version match (T6), E2/E3 results, the fit-fields check, or a binding of the file to measured evidence (hash of the measured `classify.jsonl` window plus the compiler `contentHash`, from `harness/classifier-eval.mjs`, S2), so until that binding exists `accuracy.json` is forgeable and G3 is an explicit OWNER ACT, never inferred from the file; `set --enforce enforce` refuses without it (`E_PRECONDITION`) and the doctor raises `CLASSIFIER_UNMEASURED` if enforcement is on without it. Enforcement USES the classifier; a rollback path ALWAYS exists (`subagent-policy rollback`, compile-free, next request) and S3 watches the first 200 enforced requests for any rewritten helper call.
Is `CLAUDE_CODE_GATEWAY_HINT_HEADERS=1` the authoritative answer? Evaluated as an OPTIONAL improvement (E18, never a gate). With it Claude Code adds `x-claude-code-request-class` (`main|subagent|workflow|compaction|auxiliary`) and `x-claude-code-agent-type`; those would make the class authoritative for exactly the cases inference finds hard (compaction and auxiliary versus a tool-less real subagent, and `workflow`). Costs: Claude Code reads it at startup, so a Claude Code restart and an env change (the settings env is keysync-owned) are needed, which require OWNER APPROVAL and are NOT part of this plan; it depends on a header that can change (E8); and CCR must pass it to the router (E18 checks that `rc` appears in the log). What the plan does WITHOUT it (the default): inference from agent id, billing flag and tools, measured by this section, with `rc` logged as null. If the owner later enables it: the router would use `rc` as primary when it is in the closed set and fall back to inference when absent, a counter `rcDisagree` measures it against inference for a week before `rc` becomes primary, and this protocol is re-run.

### 12.6 QUALITY BAR (QB) (revision 10, D-ac)

Owner goal: the most advanced, seamless, optimized, smooth and reliable subagent experience. Each standard below is MEASURABLE: it names its denominator (the population the number is true of, so a figure is never restated about another population) and the test that proves it. Where a standard cannot be proved offline it is proved by the sandbox suite S2d (12.1) or by the gated live stage named, and until then it is a TARGET, not a claim. A standard that is measured and unmet is reported with its measured value and the owner decides; the number is never relaxed silently (QB-10). The scenario suite S2d is the main proof; its PASS is G3 precondition (10). Numbering note (recorded because a message once called it otherwise): QB-4 is CLASSIFICATION and the HANDOFF standard is QB-5; no renumbering is needed.

| ID | Standard | Denominator | Proving test |
|---|---|---|---|
| QB-1 SPEED | The router adds under 1 ms p99 for SHADOW decisions and for WARM (sticky-hit) decisions, and under 3 ms p99 for a NEW agent and for a HANDOFF, at 1,800 rows over 60 providers, best of 3 batches. REWORDED by D-ai: NO sleep, NO synchronous rename and NO retry loop on the request path; bounded single appends (journal, logs) are allowed (the synchronous appends are part of the design); the bounded `statSync` cache checks and the cache-miss policy read remain (I19). A policy change costs a MEASURED 18-24 ms once per worker (a pure-JS sha256 verified against `node:crypto`), never on a warm request. GATES (D-av): the STRICT numbers are checked by the standalone `node test/perf/subagent-router-perf.mjs`, run by the verifier on a quiet machine; the test SUITE asserts only loose ceilings (p99 about 10 ms; handoff p50 under 3 ms, p99 under 10 ms) because parallel `node --test` files flaked the strict bounds in 4 of 8 loaded runs. The first request per worker (19-23 ms, 41-46 ms with 256 session files) and the hourly gc (20-22 ms with 256 files) are NOT covered by the warm bound and are not failures. Supersedes the 3 ms shadow assertion of S1 acceptance 13 and 6.5 (I20). | 10,000 calls per path (3 batches, best printed), with rows, providers and path printed | `node test/perf/subagent-router-perf.mjs` (strict, quiet machine), the loose suite assertions through the `now` seam, and a router source scan (no sleep, no synchronous rename, no retry loop on the route path) |
| QB-2 NEVER BREAKS | Fail-open on 100% of a fuzz of at least 10,000 inputs: no throw, no hang, and the asked model returned on error; a corrupt, missing or NEWER policy serves `asked` plus a warning. | the number of fuzz inputs and the number of failures (must be 0) | the S1r fuzz test with a per-call hang watchdog, and S2d scenario 6 |
| QB-3 NO RESTART | The gateway pid and the `service.json` mtime are IDENTICAL before and after every `set`, `pause`, `resume`, `undo` and `rollback`; rollback is effective on the VERY NEXT request; `rollback` and its alias `pause` need no `--live yes` (D-ar), so the emergency command is one word. | every command invocation in the run | S2d scenarios 1 and 9, the live G2 and G3 before/after fingerprints (R1), and a source scan that these commands never call `saveConfig`, `ccr` or a spawn of the gateway |
| QB-4 CLASSIFICATION | T1 at least 99% (Wilson lower bound at least 97%), T2 zero helper calls rewritten in at least 200, T4 zero main requests misclassified, exactly as 12.5. | the labelled sample sizes of 12.5 | the 12.5 protocol (`harness/classifier-eval.mjs`) and S2d scenarios 4 and 8 |
| QB-5 HANDOFF | After a retry signal, at least 99% of scenario runs move the agent to a DIFFERENT eligible model that fits (I21: at least 100 runs, so at most one failure); 100% of handoffs appear in the log; NOTICE COMPLIANCE (the new model printing the handoff line) is MEASURED and REPORTED, never promised; the feature DEGRADES HONESTLY (a FINDING, not a PASS) if X3 and X5 show that no retry signal reaches the router. HONEST BOUND (research, 6.1c): the handoff is SEAMLESS for per-minute and short limits (the retry reaches the router within about 1 s); for DAILY-CAP exhaustion it degrades to ONE failure plus AVOIDANCE (cooling and the overlay hint), because Claude Code fails the subagent at once when the `Retry-After` delay exceeds 60 s and no retry then reaches the router; this standard says so, and S2d scenario 11 proves the degraded behaviour rather than a handoff. D-as: there is NO per-session escalation limit (the cooldown lengths pace the ladder, so a failing model is demoted at its first failure instead of staying undemoted for about 47 minutes); D-au: a handoff follows the toggles (it never leaves the allowed set, no price cap, free mode only free models, `handoffNone` when every free model is cooling) and the tested-tier rule applies to every handoff whatever `--banded` says. | the number of scenario runs and the number of handoffs | S2d scenarios 2 and 3 and the S1r handoff tests |
| QB-6 VISIBILITY | Any state is answered by ONE `status` command in under 1 s; every warning code has a fix command; every handoff shows in `last`. | the number of states (7), of warning codes and of handoffs in the fixture | the S1d tests: a timing test of `status` on fixtures, a table test that every code in `menu/subagent-codes.mjs` has fix text, a `last` test with a HANDOFF line |
| QB-7 SETUP | A first WORKING trial in under 60 s and at most 3 questions. | one scripted wizard run on fixtures (the wall time includes the compile) | a scripted-TTY test of `wizard` with a timer and a question counter |
| QB-8 SAFETY | ZERO live-state writes in any sandbox run, by hash comparison; ZERO secrets in any output, by scan. | the paths fingerprinted (`~/.llmkeys`, `state/`, `service.json`, `~/.claude`, the live router file) and the number of output and evidence files scanned | the isolation proof of every G1, S2c and S2d run, and a secret scan with fake secrets assembled at runtime from split pieces (the GH013 rule) over all outputs, logs and evidence |
| QB-9 FRESHNESS | HEALTH is dated by its last probe (separate mechanism, the bench and overlay rules). TOOL-CAPABILITY results NEVER expire by age (D-ap): the cell and the compiled class come from the last recorded result, the probe date is visible in `show`, `explain` and the picker detail, and `*` marks only a record tested against an OLDER tool-set fixture (the class stays the last class). The doctor reports a STALE policy (compiled file older than its inputs or its owner file). | the number of cells and fixtures per state (current, outdated, absent fixture version) | the S5c cell table test over every fixture-version combination and a doctor fixture test |
| QB-10 HONEST NUMBERS | Every printed figure names its population (for example "of 212 subagent requests in 3 sessions since <date>"); targets are labelled "target" and measurements "measured"; estimates are labelled estimates. | every count line in `set`, `show`, `status`, `last`, `report` and in the S1r budget output | format tests that every count line carries a denominator, and a reviewer checklist item on every new document |
| QB-11 MEMORY (D-an) | Memory growth per request stays UNDER 4 KB (v2 before the fix: 12.5 KB per request, 515 MB after 30,000 requests; v1 and s0b: 1-2 KB), because the router body is evaluated once per process behind the stat-keyed wrapper. | N requests in the loop (printed), the heap delta divided by N | an `--expose-gc` loop through the real per-request require path, in the S1r budget test, with a redeploy step proving a changed file still loads new code; measured after the guarded splice (D-aw) on the final bytes: 0.075 KB per require, 0.009 KB per request over 30,000 requests, 0.26 MB total (1.05 KB per request before the splice) |
| QB-12 COVERAGE (S5 completeness, D-ap, D-aq) | The first tool-fidelity sweep covers ALL probe-ok models (1,875 at the 2026-10-03 replay; 1,862 without the relay, confirmed at the dry run); after S5 an untested (`u`) row is only a NEWLY DISCOVERED one (results never expire by age); newly discovered probe-ok models are QUEUED for the tool probe automatically (about 2 small requests per model, inside the bench caps and the G7 spend rules), so the untested set stays small; the owner notices a small automatic quota spend after discovery. | probe-ok models, models with a record, models with a record against the CURRENT fixture version, models queued (all printed) | the S5 dry run and the sweep report, the incremental-queue tests of S5, and `show` printing the counts |

MEASURED RESULTS (D-av; best of 3 batches, 1,800 rows over 60 providers, the standalone perf script on a quiet machine): p99 warm 0.47-0.54 ms, shadow warm 0.52-0.56 ms, shadow new agent 0.50-0.52 ms, enforce new agent 0.67-0.74 ms, handoff 1.35-1.55 ms (final router bytes, Phase B verifier, 3 runs, best p99 of 3 batches), so the strict QB-1 bounds hold; first request per worker 19-23 ms (41-46 ms with 256 session files); hourly gc 20-22 ms with 256 files; memory growth about 0.009 KB per request over 30,000 requests (0.26 MB total; 0.075 KB per require) after the guarded splice (QB-11). Second-review results are in 12.4.

Where each standard is first proved: QB-1 and QB-2 at S1r (before G1); QB-6 and QB-7 at S1d; QB-4 in S2 (12.5); QB-3, QB-5 and QB-8 in S2d and again live at G2 and G3; QB-9 at S5c; QB-10 at every stage; QB-11 at S1r; QB-12 at S5 (with the automatic queue of D-aq). The scenario list, chaos checks and ceremony are in 12.1 S2d.

## 13. Agent roles, lanes and serialisation

Every Agent call below carries an EXPLICIT `model: sonnet` (or `haiku` for pure lookups), never opus or fable (memory `subagents-never-above-sonnet`). D-b scopes that rule: it binds UW's helper agents, the ones that build this feature, and NOT the models the policy hands out. The planner, architect and critic agent types default to Opus, so a call that omits `model` silently runs them above sonnet: EVERY call states `model: sonnet` (cr-m10). Roles must also match their tools: `architect` has no Write or Edit tool, so any step that WRITES a file (the S1.0 fixtures) goes to planner or executor, and architect stays a read-only reviewer. Writers on hot files are strictly serialised; read-only reviewers run only on a frozen, committed tree (memory `no-interfering-concurrent-agents`); lanes are split by FILE, not directory.

| Stage | Role | Files owned (exclusive) | Parallel with |
|---|---|---|---|
| S0 | executor | `harness/stub-upstream.mjs`, `harness/subagent-e2e.mjs`, `harness/config.mjs` (one constant) | nothing live-touching; `Explore` (haiku) may re-verify V1-V27 read-only |
| S0 | test-engineer | reviews the isolation protocol against the three memories and the enricher gate (V22, V25) before G1 | after executor, before run |
| S0 | verifier | runs the experiment script after G1 and checks the before/after fingerprints | serial |
| S0b | executor | `harness/deploy-router.mjs`, `test/router-slot-hotfix.test.mjs`; at G2a also the live `spike/uw-router.cjs` (single writer, only through the script) | nothing |
| S0b | code-reviewer then verifier | read-only: the 3-line semantic diff and the deploy script's step order; verifier runs the deploy at G2a and checks the fingerprints | serial |
| S1.0 | planner or executor (NOT architect: no Write tool) | `test/fixtures/subagent/{snapshot,bench,providers,registry,vault-providers,key-choices,observed,default-model,settings}.json`, `test/fixtures/subagent-flags.mjs`, `test/fixtures/router-slot-v29-ids.json` | must finish first; lanes consume it |
| S1.A | executor A | `menu/subagent-funnel.mjs`, `keysync/subagent-policy.mjs`, `test/subagent-policy.test.mjs` | S1.B (different files) |
| S1.B | executor B | `router/uw-router.next.cjs`, `test/subagent-router.test.mjs`, `menu/cc-contract.mjs` and `menu/ccr-client.mjs` ADDITIVE constants only, `menu/pool-rule.mjs` (new) with the one-line import in `menu/style.mjs`, `test/contracts.test.mjs` (`router` added to a directory list), `test/subagent-contract.test.mjs` | S1.A |
| S1.C | executor C (after A and B merge) | `keysync/key.mjs`, `test/subagent-cli.test.mjs`, `docs/runbook.md` 5b | serial, hot files |
| S1 | code-reviewer then security-reviewer | read-only on the frozen tree: router (per-request file reads, session-id and agent-id to filename, log redaction, size caps, tag as an injection channel, module-scope side effects, bounded maps), CLI (flag swallow, write paths, no-touch guard), deploy script (atomicity, backup, restore) | after C, one at a time or both read-only in parallel |
| S1 | verifier | full `node --test test/*.test.mjs`, S1 acceptance commands (on fixtures) | serial |
| S1c | executor (tiers) | `menu/tiers.mjs` first (with the S1.0 fixtures, before S1.A); then `keysync/retier.mjs`, `test/keysync-tiers.test.mjs` and the `add`/`retier` cases in `keysync/key.mjs` (HOT, single writer, only after S1.C) | `menu/tiers.mjs` before S1.A; the rest serial |
| S1c | test-engineer then code-reviewer | read-only on the frozen tree: the fake-vault seam (the production vault must be unimportable from the tests), the crash-injection order (old secret removed last), the no-secret-in-output assertions | after S1c executor |
| S2 | executor (evaluator) then a DIFFERENT reviewer agent | `harness/classifier-eval.mjs` (read-only apart from `state/subagent/accuracy.json`) and the labelled-set scripts; the reviewer inspects every off-diagonal cell (12.5), authoring never approves itself | after S1 and the sandbox run |
| S2, S3 | executor (deploy) then verifier | live router file swap through `harness/deploy-router.mjs` and the CLI `set` calls, under G2, G3 | serial, announced |
| S4 | executor D | `menu/doctor.mjs`, `menu/pick-state.mjs`, `menu/uwpick.mjs`, optional `keysync/run.mjs` hook | serial per hot file |
| S4b | executor E (pane), then test-engineer, then code-reviewer and verifier | `menu/subagent-state.mjs`, `menu/subagent-pane.mjs`, and the hooks in `menu/pick-state.mjs`, `menu/uwpick.mjs`, `menu/legend.mjs` (all HOT picker files: single writer, serial), `docs/qa-interactive-protocol.md`, `test/subagent-pane.test.mjs`; `menu/uwpick-run.ps1` and `test/uwpick-autorebuild.test.mjs` (D-p: Q4 answered yes; S4b only, never S1) | after S1 and S4; never parallel with another writer on the same picker file; the reviewer checks the startup budget and the import graph |
| S5 | executor, test-engineer, code-reviewer, verifier | `refresh/tool-fidelity.mjs` (new), `state/tool-fidelity.json` writer; NOT `menu/bench-data.mjs`, `refresh/bench-store.mjs` or `menu/snapshot.mjs` (cr-M3) | serial |
| S1r (rev 10) | executor (router v2) | `router/uw-router.next.cjs`, the compile-side `menu/subagent-funnel.mjs` and `keysync/subagent-policy.mjs` changes, the two `set` flags in `keysync/key.mjs`, the router and v2 tests; ONE writer per file, lanes by file (the router file and the compiler may be two executors only if their files are disjoint and the shared compiled-file shape is frozen first) | nothing that touches these files; S1d, S2a, S2c, S4 wait |
| S1r (rev 10) | code-reviewer, then security-reviewer, then verifier | read-only on the frozen tree, in this order, one at a time or the two reviewers in parallel on the frozen tree (memory `no-interfering-concurrent-agents`): the journal and torn-line handling, filename construction from agent id and session id, the notice edit of the system prompt, the cooling file, log redaction and rate limits, module-scope side effects, the timer rule; the verifier runs the S1r acceptance and prints the MEASURED numbers | serial after the executor |
| harness update (rev 10, 12.0 step 3) | executor, then a DIFFERENT reviewer, then verifier | `harness/probe-router.cjs`, `harness/subagent-e2e.mjs`, `harness/subagent-sandbox-spec.mjs`, `harness/stub-upstream.mjs` and the evaluators for X1-X9, the shape test, the plan-hash refresh | after S1r is reviewed and verified; before the commit and G1 |
| S1d (rev 10) | executor (UX), then test-engineer, then code-reviewer and verifier | `menu/subagent-verdict.mjs`, `menu/subagent-codes.mjs`, the new commands in `keysync/subagent-policy.mjs`, and, SERIALISED with S4, the hot files `keysync/key.mjs`, `docs/runbook.md` 5b, `menu/doctor.mjs`; must not touch `router/*` (acceptance checks the router hash against the G1 evidence) | after G1 is requested; never parallel with S4 on a hot file |
| S1e (rev 10, D-af) | executor, then security-reviewer (the `settings.json` install path), then verifier | `keysync/subagent-handoff-hook.mjs`, `test/subagent-hook.test.mjs`, the `hook` subcommand in `keysync/subagent-policy.mjs`; the settings write goes ONLY through keysync's existing writer (keysync-owned, never edited directly); must not touch `router/*` | after G1 and only if X9(g) passed; serialised with S1d and S4 on shared hot files |
| S2d (rev 10, D-ac) | executor, then a DIFFERENT reviewer (test-engineer), then verifier | `harness/subagent-scenarios.mjs`, its spec file and `test/subagent-scenarios.test.mjs`; it reuses, and does not edit, `harness/stub-upstream.mjs` and the orchestrator's gates; each real run is an owner act with the G1 ceremony | after G1; before G3; never parallel with another writer on `harness/*` files |
| S5c (rev 10, D-aa, D-ab) | executor, then test-engineer, then code-reviewer and verifier | `menu/tools-data.mjs`, `test/tools-data.test.mjs`, `test/fixtures/tool-fidelity.sample.json`, and the hooks in the HOT picker files `menu/style.mjs`, `menu/pick-state.mjs`, `menu/uwpick.mjs`, `menu/legend.mjs`, `docs/qa-interactive-protocol.md` | serialised AFTER S4b; one writer per picker file; the reviewer checks the startup budget, the import graph and the legend obligation |
| S6 (rev 11, D-bf) | executor, then test-engineer, then code-reviewer and verifier | the onboarding pipeline in `keysync/key.mjs` and `keysync/run.mjs` (HOT files, single writer, serialised with S4 and S4b) and its tests on a fixture vault seam; never the real vault | after S5 is verified and G7 has proved the mechanism |
| S2a, S2c (rev 10) | executor, then a DIFFERENT reviewer, then verifier | `report` and `last --json` (S2a); `selftest` (S2c) and its sandbox spec; each real selftest run is an owner act with the G1 ceremony | after S1d on shared files; the run after G1 and before G3 |

Authoring never approves itself: each stage's output is reviewed by a different agent (reviewer or verifier) in a separate pass.

## 14. Docs and issues

`decisions.jsonl` is no longer written, I12), status is per worker and merged by `show`, `enforcement`, `classLog` and `handoffNotice` do not rotate the prompt-cache marker, `banded` is default ON (`set --banded no` restores the old spread), there is no canary flag, and an AUTO-ROLLBACK (the router's own tripwire in the first 200 enforced requests per worker) is cleared only by an explicit `set` or `resume`; ## 15. Decision record (ADR)

Decision. Enforce the three toggles (`dynamic`, `inherit`, `free`; D-a) in the existing CCR custom router, driven by a compiled JSON policy file written by a CLI, learn main's model from traffic, make each subagent's first enforced decision sticky for 6 hours (revision 10: a 6 h sliding TTL, 24 h cap, with the retry handoff as the one exception), tell main the allowed list through an idempotent Agent-tool-description injection that reuses CCR's own tag grammar (router-written, default off, G4; D-g), and roll out shadow first with enforcement and injection as separate owner switches (D-h). The router is developed at `router/uw-router.next.cjs` and deployed to the live `spike/uw-router.cjs` only through a verified atomic protocol with a one-command restore.
Drivers. R1 no restart; R2 a prompt warning on an empty candidate set (through the exact paths listed, not all instant); R3 verified tool fidelity (in its own store); UW pillars (new data by CLI with no source edits, failures loud, never silently substitute, responding provider never pruned); evidence that the hot lever (router) and the design vocabulary (tag, injection) already exist in CCR; and the finding that the router file is live, so a deploy mistake is an all-traffic incident.
Alternatives considered. (a) Repoint the six tier aliases per policy: global, four slots, semantically wrong, and writing `Providers`/profile risks restarts (phase 7 doc). (b) `Router.rules` or script rules: inert here and unable to read files. (c) CCR's native `modelDescriptions` menu: any change restarts the gateway. (d) Claude Code settings (`CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL`, agent frontmatter, `availableModels`): env is read at startup (restart of Claude Code), the first hard-overrides everything, and none can express free/ctx/same-provider. (e) Put the logic in the schema-sanitizer plugin: plugin code is loaded once, so any logic change restarts the gateway. (f) Other tools' tier/role mapping (free-claude-code, claudish, routatic, OmniRoute, cc-switch): none filters by free, same-provider and ctx per subagent; routatic's SelectCheapest is the closest and is a scenario router, not an owner policy. (g) Hint headers for subagent detection: needs a Claude Code restart; kept only as contingency C1. (h) For `inherit` + `ctx 1m` with a sub-1M main, TREAT AS EMPTY (serve `asked`, warn), the critic's recommendation, which follows the owner's text "Toggle3 contingent on toggles 1 and 2" literally: not adopted, the owner chose to inherit anyway with a warning (D-d). (i) A price cap on the policy output (never above sonnet, or `--max-price`): not adopted (D-b), a later idea. (j) Keep the legacy opus-5 slot through S3 as a pure rewrite (revision 1's D4): superseded by D-e (exact-match hotfix now, removal at G3). (k) Store the tool-fidelity field on the bench record with a snapshot schema 10 bump (revision 1): rejected, it would be lost by four code paths and blank the snapshot loader (cr-M3). (l) Edit `spike/uw-router.cjs` in place during development (revision 1): rejected, it is the live router (cr-B1). (m) Read the free-scope key tier from the snapshot `keyId`: rejected (clipped at 30 characters, issue #148; the registry is the source of truth, D-k). (n) Keep `free` = badge or zero price as the only meaning: rejected as the default (D-j makes `free providers` the default; the badge meaning stays as `free models`). (o) Treat zero price or a FREE? badge as a sufficient free signal inside paid and deposit providers: rejected, a deposit can unlock paid models that look free; only the strict free tag admits them (D-j). (q) Let the picker pane count with its own code, or shell out to the CLI for counts (revision 5): rejected, a second implementation can disagree with the CLI and a shell-out is forbidden on the picker path (import-graph rule, no `child_process`); ONE pure function in `menu/` is shared instead (D-o). (r) A mode (`shadow`/`enforce`) toggle inside the pane: rejected, enforcement is a gated CLI act. (p) Probe only the models that look usable (`tools !== false`) or all levels for everyone: rejected (D-i): L1+L2 for ALL probe-ok models, L3/L4 lazily. REVISION 10 alternatives: (s) build router v2 AFTER G1: rejected (D-q), any router change after G1 would force a sandbox re-run, so G1 must run on the final bytes; (t) a canary rollout feature and `--canary` CLI: not adopted, the compiled field is inert; (u) overflow rescue (substitute a bigger-context model when a context is outgrown): not adopted, sticky stays final except for the retry handoff; (v) failover by the CCR fallback chain: kept OFF (a free-tier 429 would be hidden or become a paid retry); the ar-17 handoff re-routes the NEXT request instead; (w) a response-side detector, SQLite reads or a daemon for handoff: not built (ar-17 item 5); (x) a per-agent-type rule set and a status-line display of the served model: deferred (revision 10 item 6).
Why chosen. It is the only combination that is restart-free, enforceable per request, expressive enough for all three toggles, reversible in one command (compile-free `rollback`), and mostly built from vocabulary CCR already ships; the deploy protocol removes the one way this design could take all traffic down.
Consequences. The router becomes policy-aware production code (so it gets differential, fuzz, budget, stickiness and sandbox tests); the owner learns that same-provider `free` is empty for a subscription main (the relay is in no free scope) and that under `free providers` 51% of the probe-ok models have unknown context and cannot be substituted (V29), while `free models AND >= 1M` is NOT empty on live data (14 rows); helper calls (titles, summaries, compaction, small-fast) stay on the relay in every mode (D-m); enforcement waits for a measured classifier (12.5); `inherit` follows main's price with no cap; prompt-cache rotation on policy change when injection is on; the router lives at a path named `spike` for now; policy is only as good as bench freshness until S5 and every unverified model carries a standing warning; detection depends on a documented but not guaranteed header and on CCR's enricher gate; the native `/model` command is outside the feature; the default `free` scope (`free providers`) is about 15 times larger than the badge scope, so free-tier credit is the new thing to watch (P10) and the tier label becomes a trusted owner input (P12); closing the picker after a saved change now triggers an automatic quiet `rebuild` through the hot `menu/uwpick-run.ps1` wrapper (D-p; hash-gated, non-fatal, compile only, permanent warning in the pane and legend, S4b only); one new stage S1c touches the `key.mjs` hot file and adds the first consumer of the tier value besides `management`.
Follow-ups. F1 move the router to a supported folder with a one-time wiring command (separate change, D-e); F2 hint-header contingency C1; F3 extend the `report` command into cost-per-subagent accounting (phase 7 "what did this session cost"); F4 per-agent-definition pins if the owner wants them; F5 re-verify E1/E3 after every Claude Code or CCR update; F6 optional `--max-price` (D-b); F7 unify `routableFromConfig` and the policy resolvability rule (V23); F8 show tool fidelity in the picker from `state/tool-fidelity.json`; F10 optional pane extensions (revision 5): a per-provider drill-down from the counts, and showing the pane figure beside the compiled figure permanently; F9 the owner relabelling of deposit providers from `paid` to `free-deposit` (an owner-run `retier`, then a keysync run and `rebuild`), and showing the free scope in the picker banner beyond problem states. REVISION 10 consequences and follow-ups: the router is rebuilt before G1 (every earlier hash and approval is void; the S1r budget numbers are MEASURED, not assumed); the policy gains one deliberate exception to "sticky is final" (the retry handoff, with a visible notice that is best effort); `banded` spread is default ON; the log layout changes (`agents.jsonl`, per-worker status, the sticky journal); and the UX wave, `report`, the self-test and the extended auto-rebuild follow G1. Follow-ups F11 least-loaded spread (ar-11) after the X3 and X5 results, F12 canary-comparison auto-rollback (ar-12), F13 verify `subagentStatusLine` from official docs (parked), F14 a per-agent-type rule via the optional E18 hint headers, F15 outcome-driven quarantine, a budget guard that degrades and a shadow A/B quality canary (all deferred, not planned), F16 re-measure the classifier against the current Claude Code version (Explore inherits main), F17 a picker filter "tool level >= N" and a sort by tool level (NOT added unless the owner asks, D-aa), F18 widen the S2d suite as new failure modes are found (D-ac). Consequences of D-aa to D-ac: the picker gains a column and a legend section on hot files after S4b; the S5 probe keeps section 11's ladder and gains the additive `lvr` field (CQ1 = YES); G3 gains precondition (10), so enforcement now waits for a real headless Claude Code run in the sandbox as well as the measured classifier.
