Recorded here because this planning run may write only this file; copy into `plans/open-questions.md` when approved.

- [x] Q7 CLOSED (owner decision D-ax, 2026-10-05): LF everywhere for the router files. The `.gitattributes` line `router/*.cjs text eol=lf` was CREATED; `router/uw-router.next.cjs` (sha256 `5da75baa60dadb3aa24ff281d4c5ac460f38067428f8f9067826c7bf516f1182`, was `4b3658b6...` as CRLF) and `router/uw-router.s0b.cjs` (sha256 `6efeb03d52fc482bcf5812f115676bf5868ec81a4c131fac1a49f9604cafeff0`, was `d5b66630...` as CRLF) were converted from CRLF to LF on 2026-10-05; the live `spike/uw-router.cjs` stays CRLF until G2a replaces it (the deploy writes the LF candidate: harmless to Node; the S0b differential test is EOL-insensitive). Reason: `core.autocrlf=true` on this machine would otherwise change the hash per checkout. The G1 plan hash and any approval are SUPERSEDED again by this byte change and must be redone after the final byte change. Original question kept for the record: should a `.gitattributes` line pin the router files to LF (recommended), or should the plan rely on the harness hashing the working-tree bytes? (The hash values `6c48c56f...` and `f3a927bb...` quoted in the first text described earlier router bytes.)

- [ ] PARKED (revision 10; not an owner question): verify the `subagentStatusLine` setting from OFFICIAL Claude Code docs before it is ever planned (idea: show the served model in the status line; not verified from docs, and it needs a keysync-owned `settings.json` edit). Q7 (line endings) was CLOSED by D-ax on 2026-10-05, so no owner question is open.

- [x] CQ2 CLOSED (owner, revision 10: NEVER EXPIRE; this REPLACES proposal P1 and section 11's 14-day FRESH and 30-day retention windows). Question kept for the record: should a stale tool-fidelity result (older than 14 days) silently compile as untested `u` for every model? The owner: "tool-test results NEVER EXPIRE, they only get updated when/if the sweep runs on them again; for health status it is understandable to track last-probed since the provider may remove or modify listings, but tool capabilities of a model most certainly do not change with time". RULING (D-ap): all age-based staleness is removed; the compiled class and the picker cell always come from the last recorded result; the probe date stays visible in `show`, `explain` and the picker detail; the only invalidations are a re-sweep, an outdated fixture version `fx` (cell `*`, class unchanged) and the ALIAS cap at `u`. Honest caveat recorded: a provider can swap the model behind an unchanged id; the policy detects that only through a re-sweep or live handoffs. What the owner will notice: tested models stay tested, the unverified warning stays small, and a model that has since changed is not caught until the next sweep.
- [x] CQ3 CLOSED (owner, revision 10: YES; D-aq). Question kept for the record: queue newly discovered probe-ok models for the tool probe automatically? RULING: yes, about 2 small requests per model (L1 and L2 first; L3 and L4 lazily, as already decided), inside the existing bench caps and the G7 spend rules; the first real run still needs the G7 approval and later incremental runs follow the caps. What the owner will notice: a small automatic quota spend after discovery, and new models' `TOOLS` cells filling in by themselves.
- [x] CQ1 CLOSED (owner, revision 10: YES; the label was CQ1 because `C1` is already the hint-header contingency of 12.2 and section 15). Question kept for the record: section 11's `lv` collapses L1 and L2 into the value 1 and cannot express "L1 passed, L2 failed" or "L4 passed, L3 failed", so should the tool-fidelity record gain the optional additive per-level result string `lvr`? RULING: YES. `lvr` (four characters over `p` passed, `f` failed, `n` not run, one per level L1..L4) is ADDED to the record of section 11 (10.3 B, S5, S5c); the compiled `v`/`t`/`x` derivation is UNCHANGED; the picker cell may now show `1` and distinguishes "L1 passed, L2 failed" from "L4 passed, L3 failed"; an out-of-order pass is ignored by the cell and reported by `show`; there is no coarse fallback from `lv` (10.3 C, I28). The picker `TOOLS` cell still uses section 11's ladder, record fields and `t` derivation, and the earlier wrong ladder and the level-1-as-`t` loosening stay withdrawn.

- OPEN NOTES (revision 10; landscape findings that pressure the plan, recorded so nobody discovers them later; none needs an owner answer now): (a) D-z: dynamic and free override an agent definition's deliberate `model: haiku`, only `inherit` exempts haiku, escape hatch `inherit`; (b) the `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` rationale is out of date (per-invocation and definition values beat the env var; `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL_FORCE` is the hard override): keep the doctor assertion, re-date it by Claude Code version; (c) the built-in Explore subagent now inherits main, so the haiku-keyed `exempt` list no longer covers it: re-measure the classifier against the current version (12.5, T6); (d) the CCR fallback chain stays OFF (a free-tier 429 becomes a subagent failure; its frequency is not measured; the ar-17 handoff re-routes the next request after a retry; third delivery, 6.1c: the stronger reasons are that the fallback is global, bypasses band, fit and cooldown, sleeps between attempts and would act on main-shaped requests, and a Retry-After above 60 s makes Claude Code fail the subagent before any fallback matters); (e) tag compliance by the main model is unproven in public: E10 gates `dynamic` enforcement.

- OPEN ITEMS of the third delivery (not owner questions): (a) CLOSED: the sources the research lane used for the free-tier limits, the Claude Code behaviour and the binary evidence are recorded in 6.1c with their URLs, each marked DOC-SUMMARY (they passed through a summarising fetch tool, so a figure is to be re-read on the official page before it is relied on for a decision); (b) the PARKED idea of a gateway transform plugin that rewrites `Retry-After` (X9(f)) would need a gateway restart (plugins are in the restart set), which conflicts with R1, so it is only ever tested in the sandbox; (c) the router executor's brief must be extended with D-ad and D-ae (they are router bytes before G1).

- [ ] CQ4 (revision 11, sa-T2; OPEN, NON-BLOCKING, NOT decided). One L3 pass makes a model `v` for good (D-ap: tool results never expire), while two failed tries make `x`. The audit asks for a repeat confirmation or a periodic re-check. WHAT THE OWNER WOULD NOTICE: (a) as written (one pass is enough, no re-check): fewest probe requests and a stable picker, but a lucky or partial pass could keep a model `v` for good, and a model that later breaks is caught only by a re-sweep or live handoffs; (b) require TWO L3 passes before `v` (a repeat confirmation): about one extra L3 request per candidate once (L3 is about 40,000 input tokens), fewer false `v`, and a model with one pass shows `3` but compiles `t` until confirmed; (c) a periodic re-check of `v` models (for example after a long window): catches silent provider swaps, but it is age-based staleness again and conflicts with D-ap and costs recurring quota. Default if you say nothing: (a). Cost of getting it wrong: a false `v` in the policy's top band (a) against extra spend (b) or the age-based rule the owner removed (c).
- [ ] CQ5 (revision 11 addendum; OPEN, NON-BLOCKING; to be ASKED when S2d is built). Should S2c self-test runs and S2d scenario-suite runs be delegated to the agent like the G1 sandbox runs (D-bj)? Today they use the same ceremony but are a separate owner act. WHAT THE OWNER WOULD NOTICE: if delegated, no typed approval per sandbox scenario run, so runs go faster, with the same isolation proof and tripwire; if not, each run pauses for the owner's typed approval. Default if you say nothing: NOT delegated (D-bj is not widened).
- [ ] CQ6 (revision 11 addendum; OPEN, NON-BLOCKING; after the post-sweep report). Which picker layout do you want for the model-level menu (tier cell, `~3` and `c`, "verified at N KB", the spawn mark, the thinking-first mark)? The agent will show a mock at 78 and at 132 columns with its recommendation (D-bq). WHAT THE OWNER WOULD NOTICE: more columns make the information visible at a glance but crowd the list at 78 columns; fewer marks keep the list clean but hide a fact until you open the detail. Default if you say nothing: the recommended layout of the report.
- [ ] CQ7 (revision 11; OPEN; BLOCKS THE FULL SWEEP, not the pilot). What dollar cap for the FULL sweep (D-bu)? The agent proposes a cap BEFORE the sweep, with the dry-run figures: 383 of 1,639 queued free-key models carry a listed price, about $43.00 at full depth, 111 of 1,639 are over the per-row ceiling and become `pending: priced-over-row-cap`, and the pilot uses `--max-spend 3`. WHAT THE OWNER WOULD NOTICE: a higher cap tests more priced models and spends real money on free-keyed providers; a lower cap leaves more priced rows pending and untested. Default: NO full sweep until a cap is approved.
- ANNOUNCED DEFERRALS of revision 11 (not owner questions): (a) the router-bytes change that ranks unknown-`pb` rows lowest for requests over 200 KB (sa-A1) and, if the classify log rotation is a router constant, the raised retention (sa-L), each need a NEW G1 run, which the delegated G1 runs (D-bj) allow without a typed hash; (b) the unrun X cases (X9(g) and (h), X3 (b), (c), (d) and (f), X8 OpenAI path, X6) go to S2d or the standalone check; (c) the plugin idea X9(f) stays PARKED; (d) the experiments E20-E26 stay open until measured; (e) ALL router-bytes deferrals are ONE named batch, ROUTER V3 (R-v3), made after the pilot-sweep data and proved by ONE new G1 run: (1) unknown-`pb` rows rank lowest for requests over 200 KB (sa-A1); (2) the classify log retention constant, `CLASS_MAX` 2 MiB with 1 generation at `router/uw-router.next.cjs` lines 64 and 96, raised (sa-L); (3) the headroom multiple k, if it must be a compiled or router constant (D-bl); and, only if the executor finds it hardcoded, the 1M floor (D-bk).

- ANNOUNCED DEFERRALS of revision 10 (not owner questions; reasons in revision 10 item 6): canary rollout feature and CLI (field inert), overflow rescue (sticky stays final; a 128k substitute outgrown by turn 40 fails until the TTL), the TRIAL display rename, the status-line display of the served model, per-agent-type rules, outcome-driven quarantine, a budget guard that degrades, a shadow A/B quality canary, least-loaded spread (ar-11; the retry-demotion half is superseded by the ar-17 cooling ladder, I13), canary-comparison auto-rollback (ar-12), an effort cap, a picker filter "tool level >= N" and a sort by tool level (D-aa: only if the owner asks). Interpretations I12-I21 of revision 10 item 9 and I22-I27 of item 18 stand unless the owner corrects them.

- [x] Q5 CLOSED (owner, revision 8: option B, decided). Where does a main provider with ZERO rows in the candidate set get reported? RULING: add a separate compiled list `noRowProviders`, built in S4b, in `HASH_KEYS`, with null-prototype structures and `own()`; `emptyProviders[]` semantics do NOT change (providers that have rows in the candidate set but no substitutable row; acceptance 6 and section 17 item 11 still expect exactly [`fx-free-b`]); `set` refusal rule (b) STAYS and `noRowProviders` is the standing signal after the policy is saved and main later changes provider; full text and tests in revision 8 item 1. Original question kept for the record (new in revision 7): as built, `emptyProviders[]` lists providers that have rows in the candidate set but no substitutable row (9.2b, acceptance 6, section 17); a provider with no rows at all, including main's own, was caught only at `set` time by refusal rule (b), so the pane and `show` could not say "empty" for it. Options were (a) correct the 5.2 prose and leave the gap, (b) add `noRowProviders`; the owner chose (b).
- [x] Q6 CLOSED (owner, revision 8: option B, decided). Rank key 2b gave price class 0 to a row that is free-tagged on a paid or deposit provider, so such a row ranked alongside price-0 free-tier rows. RULING: rank free-tagged rows on paid or deposit providers BELOW true price-0 free-tier rows. Price classes under 2b are now, in order: price 0 on a free-tier provider (class 0); free-tag-admitted rows on paid or deposit providers (class 0t, below class 0, above unknown); unknown price; positive price (input plus output). Admission is unchanged (strict free-tag rule, probe-ok, never `pay`). The CODE CHANGE to `menu/subagent-funnel.mjs` is BUILT AND VERIFIED (revision 9 item 1), with the tests of revision 8 item 2; the exact definition is in 5.3 (rank key 2b). It re-rotates the compiled hash once for a policy that has `ft` rows. Original options kept for the record: (a) keep as built, (b) rank them below; the owner chose (b), so the former default "(a), as built" no longer applies.

- ANNOUNCED DEFERRALS (not owner questions; each is a known gap that stays open on purpose and is named here so nobody discovers it later as a miss): (1) the injected-text TEMPLATE (the `renderBlock` head and notice wording) is not covered by `contentHash`; (2) `Win32CredManager` / `ApiKeyVault.ps1` function shapes (atomic `Save-KeyRegistry`, `Write` taking a plain string, dot-sourcing printing nothing) are UNVERIFIED by any reviewer because a classifier denied the read; the owner or a permitted read must confirm them before `retier` is ever run for real; (3) the enforce first-decision latency (about 15 ms for a NEW agent, dominated by `stickyPut`) is an open G3-sandbox item (12.1 S3 precondition (9)); (4) binding `accuracy.json` to measured evidence is S2 (12.1 S3 precondition (8)). Revision 9 item 11 lists further announced gaps and is part of this list. Also still deferred from revision 7 item 12: sticky entries outliving a tightened policy for up to 6 h, the legacy slot rewrite until G3, and the unused `models[]`/`counts.allowed` under `inherit`.

- [x] Q1 CLOSED (owner, revision 6: I1, I9, I10, I11 CONFIRMED as written; I3/D-d and I8 already decided). Confirm or correct the remaining INTERPRETATIONS: I1 (`inherit` overrides Toggle 1 and gives main's exact model, not an equivalent "type" from another provider), I3/D-d (`inherit` plus `ctx 1m` with a sub-1M main inherits anyway and only warns; the critic would have treated it as empty), and the lower-risk I9-I11 (the free-tag pattern and its bare-id input, the `models` scope with the strict rule on paid and deposit tiers, the tier of the WIRED key). I8 is decided (D-m) and is no longer asked. Default if you say nothing: as written. Cost of getting it wrong: I1 changes which subagents move to main's model, I3 decides whether a 1M requirement is a hard filter or a warning.
- [x] Q2 CLOSED (owner, revision 6: ACCEPTED as proposed, including 40 per subagent/helper type, 200 main, 3 sessions on 2 days, 30-day expiry). Confirm the PROPOSED classifier-accuracy thresholds of 12.5 that block enforcement: T1 at least 99% of real tool-carrying subagent requests classified `sub` (Wilson 95% lower bound at least 97%); T2 zero helper calls rewritten in at least 200 helper requests; T4 zero main requests misclassified; minimum samples (40 per subagent type and per helper type, 200 main, 3 sessions, 2 days); the record expires after a Claude Code or CCR update or 30 days. Default: as written. Cost: stricter numbers mean more labelled sessions before G3; looser numbers accept more bypass or more rewritten helper calls.
- [x] Q3 CLOSED (owner, earlier; recorded revision 6: premium = family `opus` or `fable`, OR output price >= $20 per M tokens). What counts as "Opus- or Fable-priced" for the D-b warning? Default: catalogue family `opus` or `fable`, OR output price at least $20 per M tokens (one named constant, listed by `explain`). Alternative: family only, or a number you choose. Cost: a too-narrow rule misses a pricey non-Anthropic model, a too-wide rule warns on routine mains and the warning stops being read.
- [x] Q4 DECIDED YES (owner, revision 6, D-p) with two conditions: a PERMANENT always-visible warning in the pane and legend naming the command `subagent-policy rebuild --if-stale yes --quiet yes`, and NO rebuild unless the owner-file content/hash changed in that picker session; non-fatal, quiet, failure shown on the next open, never enables enforcement, never restarts the gateway. Original question kept for the record (new in revision 5): May the picker wrapper `menu/uwpick-run.ps1`, after the picker exits and only when the pane saved a new owner policy, run `node keysync/key.mjs subagent-policy rebuild --if-stale yes --quiet yes` (non-fatal, time-bounded, a read-only `getConfig`, outside the picker process) so a pane save becomes live without a separate CLI step? Superseded default (it was NO; the owner answered YES): the pane would have said "saved; run subagent-policy rebuild to apply"; the pane now says "saved; closing the picker will run subagent-policy rebuild automatically (or run it now)", and the picker banner and doctor still show `policy not applied` until a rebuild has succeeded. Cost of getting it wrong: yes touches the ctrl+g handoff wrapper (a hot, fragile path) and makes a picker save live within seconds; no leaves a window in which the picker implies a policy the router is not yet applying (P13).

## 17. S1 build brief (an executor starts here; Phase A only: no live action, no gate, no CCR call; every Agent call `model: sonnet`)

REVISION 10 NOTE: this brief describes what S1 was asked to do and is HISTORICAL for the router. Router v2 (stage S1r, 12.1, section 6.1b) supersedes its router items: item 9 ("no timers", "`setTimeout`" in item 13) becomes "no timer outside a request" (5.4, section 4), item 10 (state files) gains `agents.jsonl`, `agents-<sid>.jsonl`, `status-<w>.json` and `cooling.json` and loses `decisions.jsonl` and `agents-<sid>.json`, and items 8 and 11 (compile, fixture counts) keep their counts but the hash fields change (5.2 revision 10 paragraph). An executor building S1r starts from section 6.1b and the S1r acceptance, not from this list.
1. Read first: section 4 (files), 5.2 (compiled schema), 6.1-6.2 (router), 7 and 7.1 (CLI, flags, exit codes, `retier`), 8 (compile rules), 9.1-9.2b (tests and fixtures), 12.0 (order).
2. CREATE: `menu/tiers.mjs`, `menu/pool-rule.mjs`, `menu/subagent-funnel.mjs` (the pure funnel every count comes from, imported later by the S4b picker pane: no I/O, no `node:` import, imports only `menu/pool-rule.mjs` and `menu/tiers.mjs`), `keysync/subagent-policy.mjs` (the I/O shell around it), `keysync/retier.mjs`, `router/uw-router.next.cjs`, `test/fixtures/subagent-flags.mjs`, `test/fixtures/subagent/{snapshot,bench,providers,registry,vault-providers,key-choices,observed,default-model,settings}.json`, `test/subagent-router.test.mjs`, `test/subagent-policy.test.mjs`, `test/subagent-cli.test.mjs`, `test/subagent-contract.test.mjs`, `test/keysync-tiers.test.mjs`.
3. EDIT minimally: `keysync/key.mjs` (case `subagent-policy`; then `add` tier validation and `retier`), `menu/cc-contract.mjs` and `menu/ccr-client.mjs` (additive constants only), `menu/style.mjs` (import `POOL_ALIAS_RE`), `test/contracts.test.mjs` (add `router` to the scan list). NEVER edit `keysync/run.mjs`, `keysync/keysync.mjs`, `refresh/*`, `menu/snapshot.mjs`, `menu/bench-data.mjs`, `spike/*`.
4. ORDER: S1.0 (fixtures, `tiers.mjs`, `pool-rule.mjs`, flag helper) first; then library and router in parallel (different files); then the CLI in `key.mjs`; then `add`/`retier`; then review by a different agent.
5. CLI: `node keysync/key.mjs subagent-policy <show|set|clear|explain|rebuild|rollback> [flags]`; every flag takes an explicit value; booleans are exactly `yes` or `no`; anything else exits 1.
6. FLAGS (exact): `--source same-provider|all-providers`, `--mode dynamic|inherit|free`, `--free-scope models|providers|providers+deposit`, `--ctx any|128k|200k|256k|512k|1m|prefer-256k|prefer-512k|prefer-1m`, `--enforce shadow|enforce`, `--inject off|on`, `--unverified allow-warn|allow-t|pin-only`, `--allow provider/model`, `--dry`, `--allow-empty`, `--quiet`, `--if-stale` (all `yes|no`), `--min-set N` (FRAGILE threshold only); FILE flags `--policy-file --state-dir --providers-file --snapshot-file --bench-file --observed-file --default-model-file --registry-file --key-choices-file --vault-providers-file --settings-file --tool-fidelity-file`, under the FIXTURE-MODE rule AS BUILT (revision 9 item 2): fixture mode is ON when ANY file flag is present and applies to ALL subcommands; `set`, `explain` and `rebuild` require `--policy-file`, `--state-dir` and `--providers-file` together, `show` requires `--policy-file` and `--state-dir`, `rollback` and `clear` accept `--state-dir` alone and refuse `--policy-file` alone; blank or whitespace-only file-flag values are rejected (exit 1); any explicit path under `~/.llmkeys`, `~/.claude`, `~/.uw/state` or `~/.uw/catalog` is refused (realpath of the nearest existing ancestor, compared case-insensitively, `..` and junction safe); optional flags not given go to `<state>/_not-given/<flag>`.
7. EXIT CODES: 0 ok; 1 usage or validation (`E_USAGE`, `E_TIER_INVALID`, `E_PRECONDITION`); 2 empty refusal (`E_EMPTY`); 3 explain unknown id; 4 input unreadable or ambiguous (`E_OWNER_CORRUPT`, `E_SNAPSHOT`, `E_TIER_UNREADABLE`, `E_KEY_AMBIGUOUS`); 5 write failure (`E_WRITE`).
8. COMPILE: stages 1-11 of section 8; tier from the registry through `filterRegistry` and `chooseKeys` (strict key-choices read); `FREE_TAG` on the BARE id; strict free-tag rule on `paid` and `free-deposit` in EVERY scope; alias via `POOL_ALIAS_RE` (flag `al`, tier at most `u`); `substitutable` pool drives `emptyProviders[]`, refusal and FRAGILE; rank keys 1, 2, 2b, 3, 4, 5, 6 (2b as built and verified: class 0, 0t, U, P; owner ruling Q6, revision 8 item 2 and revision 9 item 1); G17: an `excluded`-tier provider contributes nothing to any count, list, rank or hash (revision 8 item 3, built); substitute by `fnv1a32(utf8(agentKey)) mod K`, K = 3; id sanitiser `^[A-Za-z0-9_./:@+~-]{1,64}$` (raised to 160 characters by sa-A10, revision 11).
9. ROUTER: class `main`/`sub`/`aux`(+`exempt` under `inherit`) per 6.1; sticky map 6 h; exact-match slot until G3; no module-scope side effect, no timers, `route.__test = {reset, now}`; every smoke call awaited; `classLog` per request (5.5); never returns `undefined` for a non-empty model.
10. STATE FILES (all under the `--state-dir` in tests): `policy.json`, `status.json`, `decisions.jsonl`, `classify.jsonl`, `main-<sid>.json`, `agents-<sid>.json`, `shadow.flag`; owner file at `--policy-file`.
11. FIXTURE COUNTS TO HIT (9.2b): `free models` 5 (1 with ctx >= 1M), `free providers` 7 on 2 providers, `free providers + deposit` 9 on 4, `deposit-strict-skipped` 2, ALIAS 2, substitutable `fx-free-a` 3, `fx-free-b` 0, `fx-dep` 1, `fx-paid` 1, `emptyProviders[]` = [`fx-free-b`] (unchanged by the `noRowProviders` ruling, which is S4b work). Dynamic all-providers (acceptance 7, G17): `ALLOWED: 14 models (of 14 in the chosen scope; verified 3, small 0, unverified 11)` and `SUBSTITUTABLE: 10 of 14 allowed` (15 before G17 excluded `fxm-one`).
12. ACCEPTANCE: the 13 commands of section 12 S1 (acceptance 8 and 9 as amended in revision 7 item 1: they carry `--source all-providers`; a DRY first `set` without `--source` previews as `all-providers` with a NOTE, a REAL first `set` without it exits 1), run verbatim with `T=$(mktemp -d); F=$(node test/fixtures/subagent-flags.mjs --dir "$T")`.
13. HARD RULES: tests never read `~/.llmkeys`, `state/`, `service.json` or `~/.claude`; no `saveConfig`, no spawn of keysync, no `setTimeout` in the router; no secret in any output; any fake secret is assembled at runtime from split pieces; the real Credential Manager is never touched (fake vault seam only).
14. DONE = acceptance green, `git diff --stat` equals the allowed list of step 3, a reviewer pass by a DIFFERENT agent, then STOP: nothing is deployed and no gate is requested by the executor.
